four of the six residual pairs.  Contract \(e\) by either realizable
parity kernel at phase \(1\).  This crosses the two factors of each
\(P_j\).  For every supported \(p\)-string, the output has precisely
two complementary residual strings, each with one internal lift and a
nonzero coefficient.  Different \(p\)-strings cannot cancel.  The
resulting realizable quaternary therefore has
\[
 2\bigl(\lvert\supp U_p\rvert+\lvert\supp V_p\rvert\bigr)
\]
nonzero entries.  Six or eight entries contradict the card hypothesis.
Otherwise both supports are singletons.  Their opposite-parity
\(p\)-strings differ in exactly one bit, so the other input of
\(p\) is fixed.  Its point mass is a unary tensor factor of \(h\).

For type \(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{C}\), choose a residual pair \(e\) belonging to neither
the \(\mathsf P\) matching nor the fixed/varying matching of the \(\mathsf C\)
ruling.  Apply the same realizable parity contraction.  Each supported
\(\mathsf P\)-sector \(p\)-string leaves two residual strings.  Each of the two
\(\mathsf C\)-sector \(p\)-strings leaves exactly one: the singleton varying
endpoint and the kernel parity force the bit on the fixed factor, and
then force both remaining residual bits.  These lifts are unique and
all coefficients are nonzero.  If the \(\mathsf P\) sector has two
\(p\)-strings, the card has six nonzero entries.  If it has one, the
card has four nonzero entries but its projection onto \(p\) has
three strings.  A product of two nonsingular monomial binaries projects
onto any two inputs in exactly two strings when those inputs are matched,
and in four strings otherwise.  Both alternatives contradict the card
hypothesis.  Type \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{P}\) is identical.

Unless one of the stated factorizations has already occurred, every
deleted pair must consequently have type \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\).  Fix one such pair.
Its fixed binary factors and complementary-axis endpoints give exactly
the eight-string support in
\cref{eq:cert-nd-q6-four-flat-support}.  The support classification in
\cref{prop:cert-nd-q6-support-lowering} either gives an injective
\(I/X\)-contraction with six or eight projected strings, or identifies
a three-edge matching coset.  In the first case, injectivity gives one
nonzero summand per output string, so this realizable quaternary contradicts
the card hypothesis.  In the second case, one parity sector at any
edge of that matching is identically zero, contradicting the conclusion
that every deleted pair is \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\).  This completes the proof.

All contractions in this proof are applied directly to \(h\).
The factors are exact tensor factors, and are exposed only through
\cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation} when the reduction
procedure uses them.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Completion of the standard dihedral case}

\begin{theorem}[Standard \(\DihZ{2m}\)-sector deck rigidity]
\label{thm:normalized-dihedral}
Assume
\[
 \Stable(\cF,\DihZ{2m},f),\qquad \Even(\cF),\qquad
 \arity(f)=\nu(\cF)=2k+2,\qquad m\ge3,
\]
where
\[
 \DihZ{2m}
 =\{[D(t)],[XD(t)]:t\in\mu_m\}.
\]
Then \(\Close_{\{\cP\}}(\cF,\DihZ{2m},f)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Completion of \cref{thm:normalized-dihedral}]
If \(\arity(f)=4\), regenerate and close its complete deck.
In the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) case the output of
\cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary} is an invertible dressing of the irreducible
quaternary.  It is therefore a realizable endpoint-nondegenerate irreducible
eight-vertex signature.  Apply
\cref{thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy} to its signature set.
Direct realization and preservation under taking subsets give a hard or
tractable case satisfying \(\ReTract(K\cF)\) for the entire retained set \(\cF\).

For \(\arity(f)=6\), \cref{lem:nd-six-prime-base} contradicts the
irreducibility of the distinguished irreducible signature.  Now let \(n:=\arity(f)\ge8\).
At any deleted pair \(p\), two disagreeing nonzero sampled cards would
give the six-input output in \cref{lem:app-nd-localization}.  The first
proper contraction of the distinguished irreducible signature is zero or a matching product, and
further monomial contractions preserve that property.  This contradicts
the two inherited disagreeing nonzero cards.  Hence all samples at \(p\)
share one matching and all factor classes outside one designated edge \(e\).
The \hyperref[par:nd-common-factor-consequence]{common-factor consequence}
following \cref{lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer}, applied to both sectors,
therefore gives \(f=P\otimes q_{p,e}\). Here \(P\) is a nonzero product
containing at least one binary factor, contradicting irreducibility.

Consequently no higher-arity deck-stable signature branch remains.  The quaternary
case already resolves the whole signature set, and completes this branch. These are the allowed outcomes of
\(\Close_{\{\cP\}}(\cF,\DihZ{2m},f)\).
\end{proof}

The restriction \(m\ge3\) is essential here.  For \(m=2\), two forbidden
trace roots can exhaust \(\mu_2\), and evaluation on \(\mu_2\) does not
determine a polynomial of coordinate degree two.  That case belongs to the
separate normalized \(\KleinStd\) analysis.


\subsection{Marked coordinates and whole-network reductions}
\label{sec:marked-dihedral-sectors}

For the nonstandard marked groups, the same binary transfers acquire a
different edge matrix.  We first compute that matrix, then determine
which parts of the standard proof survive.  The matrices \(U_\tau\)
below change analytic coordinates.  The \(\DihY{2m}\) and
\(\DihXzero{2m}\) proofs additionally give whole-network
equivalences. The \(\DihXone{2m}\) proof instead keeps the original
network and tracks the resulting weights.

\begin{lemma}[Marked-sector coordinates and edge matrices]
\label{lem:marked-sector-dictionary}\leavevmode\newline
Use the form labels \(Z,Y,(X,0),(X,1)\) as sector indices.
For \(\tau=Z,Y\), let \(m\ge3\) and set
\begin{equation*}
 U_{Z}=I,\qquad
 U_{Y}=I-iX.
\end{equation*}
For \(\tau=(X,1)\), let \(m\ge4\) be even, fix
\(\eta\in\mu_{2m}\setminus\mu_m\), choose \(s_0\in\AlgNums\) with
\(s_0^2=\eta^{-1}\), and set
\begin{equation*}
 U_{X,1}=
 \begin{pmatrix}1&1\\1&-1\end{pmatrix}\diag(1,s_0),
 \quad s_0^2=\eta^{-1}.
\end{equation*}
Conjugation by \(U_\tau\) sends \(\DihZ{2m}\) projectively onto the
corresponding marked group.  Define the transformed edge matrix \(S_\tau\) by
\[
 S_\tau=U_\tau^{\mathsf T}XU_\tau.
\]
Then, projectively,
\begin{equation*}
 S_{Z}=X,\qquad
 S_{Y}=I,\qquad
 S_{X,1}=\diag(1,-\eta^{-1}).
\end{equation*}
For \(T\in\DihZ{2m}\), choose a fixed realizable marked-sector binary
\(B_T\) whose sector-coordinate transfer is \([T]\), and set
\(B'_T:=U_\tau^{-1}B_TU_\tau^{-\mathsf T}\).  Then, for
\(T,U\in\DihZ{2m}\),
\begin{equation}
 B'_T\doteq T S_\tau^{-1},
 \qquad
 (TS_\tau^{-1})S_\tau(US_\tau^{-1})
 =TU S_\tau^{-1}.
 \label{eq:marked-sector-fusion}
\end{equation}
Thus multiplication and reversal are preserved, and every monomial fusion
has the same unique internal lift. Consequently such a fusion introduces no
cancellation.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\[
 U_{Y}D(t)U_{Y}^{-1}\doteq C(t),\qquad
 U_{X,1}D(t)U_{X,1}^{-1}\doteq A(t),
 \qquad
 U_{X,1}XD(t)U_{X,1}^{-1}
 \doteq ZA(\eta t).
\]
\[
 U_{Y}^{\mathsf T}XU_{Y}=-2iI,
 \qquad
 U_{X,1}^{\mathsf T}XU_{X,1}
 =2\diag(1,-\eta^{-1}).
\]
Congruence gives \(B'_T\doteq TS_\tau^{-1}\), and the fusion
identity is matrix multiplication. All omitted scalars are fixed nonzero.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Sector coordinates and available kernels.}
For an \(r\)-ary current-coordinate signature \(h\), write
\[
 h^{(\tau)}=(U_\tau^{-1})^{\otimes r}h
\]
for its analytic sector-coordinate tensor.  A \emph{sector-\(X\) dressing} is input dressing by the fixed realizable binary \(B_X\). By
\cref{eq:marked-sector-fusion}, its transported transfer fuses as \(X\).
Put
\[
 \beta_{Y}:=1,
 \qquad
 \delta:=-\eta^{-1},\quad
 \beta_{X,1}:=\delta^{-1},
 \qquad
 \mathscr R_\tau:=\beta_\tau\mu_m,
\]
where the quantities involving \(\eta\) and \(\delta\) apply only in the
\((X,1)\)-sector.  For a quotient in \(\mathscr R_\tau\), division
by \(\beta_\tau\) gives a point of \(\mu_m\).  This division acts on coefficient ratios.  In one monomial parity plane, a
\emph{complementary-axis pencil} has its two coefficient matrices on
the two coordinate axes.  A sample with exactly one vanishing coordinate
is a singular binary.

\begin{lemma}[Symmetric binary pullback]
\label{lem:symmetric-binary-pullback}
Let \(\cC\in\{\cA,\cP,\cL\}\).  If
\(E,F\in\cC\) are nonsingular symmetric binaries, then
\(EFE\in\cC\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Closure under contraction proves the affine case, and the
equality/disequality normal forms prove the product case.  For the
local-affine case, write
\(E=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\b&c\end{smallmatrix}\right)\).
The diagonal twists in the definition of \(\cL\) preserve support, which
must be affine.  Nonsingularity excludes support size one, and affine
support excludes size three.  Full support is also impossible: the
\(\alpha=00\) condition requires \(b/a\in\mu_4\), whereas the
\(\alpha=01\) twist requires \(\zeta_8 b/a\in\mu_4\).
On anti-diagonal support symmetry gives \(E=bX\), and the same
\(\alpha=01\) twist again has a nonzero-entry ratio outside \(\mu_4\).
Consequently \(E=a\diag(1,i^k)\), with \(a\ne0\) and
\(k\in\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z\).  Conversely these four diagonal forms
satisfy both supported twists, \(\alpha=00,11\), and belong to \(\cL\).
Writing \(F=d\diag(1,i^\ell)\) in the same way gives
\(EFE=a^2d\diag(1,i^{2k+\ell})\in\cL\), as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Signature-set-wide reduction of the \(\DihY{2m}\) sector]
\label{thm:marked-B-reduction}
If
\[
 \Stable(\cF,\DihY{2m},f),\qquad
 \Even(\cF),\qquad m\ge3,
\]
then \(\Close_{\{\cP\}}(\cF,\DihY{2m},f)\).
\end{theorem}

The tractable cases here include
\(\PresK(K\cF;\cA)\) and \(\PresK(K\cF;\cL)\). The product case is
the only remaining tractable case.

\begin{proof}
Define the transformed signature set
\(\Gamma:=U_{Y}^{-1}\cF\) and the transformed active irreducible signature
\[
 f^{(Y)}:=(U_{Y}^{-1})^{\otimes\arity(f)}f\in\Gamma.
\]
This whole-problem change gives
\begin{equation*}
 U_{Y}^{\mathsf T}XU_{Y}=-2iI,
 \qquad
 U_{Y}^{-1}IU_{Y}^{-\mathsf T}=\frac{i}{2}X,
 \qquad
 U_{Y}^{-1}XU_{Y}^{-\mathsf T}=\frac{i}{2}I.
\end{equation*}
Thus edge and equality exchange, and \cref{lem:p1-equality-wire-equivalence}
gives
\begin{equation*}
 \KHolant(\cF)
 \equivT
 \Holant(\Gamma,X)
 \equivT
 \KHolant(\Gamma,I).
\end{equation*}
Define the signature set
\(\cF^\sharp:=\Gamma\cup\{I\}\).  Here the \emph{open-network
bijection} is the vertexwise change by \(U_{Y}^{-1}\), together
with the displayed edge/equality exchange, applied also to the ordered open
boundary legs. The inverse change restores the original network.  It
preserves arity, factorization, minimum arity of a nonbinary irreducible signature, and parity of
the arity of every irreducible signature. Hence \(\Even(\cF^\sharp)\).

For a realizable nonsingular \(b\) over \(\cF^\sharp\), its pullback
\(B\) satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 B\doteq U_{Y}bU_{Y}^{\mathsf T},
 \qquad
 [BX]=[U_{Y}bU_{Y}^{-1}],
\end{equation*}
using \(U_{Y}^{\mathsf T}X=-2iU_{Y}^{-1}\).  Infinite
order pulls back to the interpolation reduction of
\cref{lem:binary-interpolation}. Otherwise
\cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness} applies to the pullback transfer
\([BX]\).  Conjugating by \(U_{Y}^{-1}\) first puts
\([b]\) in \(\DihZ{2m}\).  Since \([X]\in\DihZ{2m}\), closure under
right multiplication then puts the realizable transformed transfer
\([bX]\) in \(\DihZ{2m}\).

Conversely, for the chosen representative \(B_T\), define its
nonzero representative scalar \(\beta_T\) by
\(B_TX=\beta_TU_{Y}TU_{Y}^{-1}\).  Then
\begin{equation*}
 B'_T
 =U_{Y}^{-1}B_TU_{Y}^{-\mathsf T}
 =\frac{i\beta_T}{2}T.
\end{equation*}
Since \([D(t)X]=[XD(t^{-1})]\) and
\([XD(t)X]=[D(t^{-1})]\), every class occurs and
\begin{equation*}
 G(\cF^\sharp)=\DihZ{2m}.
\end{equation*}
The open-network bijection also transports the complete deck: absorbing an
equality edge replaces a label by \(XT,TX\), or \(XTX\), which permute
\(\DihZ{2m}\).  Hence zeros, matching products, factor closure, and
minimum arity among irreducible signatures correspond, so the transformed state is deck-stable.
In particular,
\[
 \Stable(\cF^\sharp,\DihZ{2m},f^{(Y)}),
 \qquad
 \arity(f^{(Y)})=\nu(\cF^\sharp)=\arity(f).
\]

Apply \cref{thm:normalized-dihedral}.  Complete closure of each transported
concrete batch is resolved.  On the remaining branch there are
\(\cC\in\{\cA,\cP,\cL\}\) and one \(V\in\GL_2(\AlgNums)\) such that
\[
 V^{-1}(\Gamma\cup\{I\})\subseteq\cC,
 \qquad V^{\mathsf T}XV\in\cC.
\]

Define the common change-of-basis matrix \(W:=U_{Y}V\). Then
\(W^{-1}\cF\subseteq\cC\).  With
\[
 E=V^{\mathsf T}XV,
 \qquad F=V^{-1}IV^{-\mathsf T},
\]
both \(E,F\) are nonsingular symmetric members of \(\cC\), and
\cref{lem:symmetric-binary-pullback} gives
\[
 W^{\mathsf T}XW
 =-2iV^{\mathsf T}IV
 =-2iEFE\in\cC.
\]
Thus \(W\) is common.  The \(\cA,\cL\) cases are resolved
tractable cases satisfying \(\Tract(K\cF)\). \(\cP\) is the remaining tractable case.
The reductions above use \(U_{Y}\) for the whole network.
\(W=U_{Y}V\) records its contribution to the final witness.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Reduction of the
  \texorpdfstring{\(\DihXzero{2m}\)}{D2m (X,0)} sector]
\label{thm:marked-A0-reduction}
Assume
\[
 \Stable(\cF,\DihXzero{2m},f),\qquad
 \Even(\cF),\qquad m\ge4,\quad 2\mid m.
\]
Then \(\Close_{\{\cP\}}(\cF,\DihXzero{2m},f)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Use the Walsh matrix \(P\) from \cref{eq:Walsh-coordinates}, with
\(P^{-1}=P/2\).
Direct multiplication gives, projectively,
\begin{equation}
 PD(t)P^{-1}\doteq A(t),
 \qquad
 PXD(t)P^{-1}\doteq ZA(t),
 \label{eq:marked-A0-conjugacy}
\end{equation}
so \(P^{-1}\DihXzero{2m}P=\DihZ{2m}\).  More importantly,
\begin{equation}
 P^{\mathsf T}XP=2Z,
 \qquad
 P^{-1}IP^{-\mathsf T}=\frac12I.
 \label{eq:marked-A0-fixed-I}
\end{equation}
Thus this whole-problem change preserves the available binary equality.

Make available by reduction the fixed representative \(B_j\) of the mark
\(j=[Z]\), retaining its realization scalar and input orientation.  Since
\([B_jX]=j\), one has \(B_j\doteq Y\).  Put
\[
 \cF^+=\cF\cup\{B_j\},
 \qquad
 \Gamma=P^{-1}\cF^+,
 \qquad
 f^{(X,0)}=(P^{-1})^{\otimes\arity(f)}f.
\]
After recording the two nonzero representative scalars, use \(I,Y\in\Gamma\). Indeed
\[
 P^{-1}IP^{-\mathsf T}=\frac12I,
 \qquad
 P^{-1}YP^{-\mathsf T}=-\frac12Y.
\]
Write \(\Holant^Z(\Gamma)\) for the homogeneous \(Z\)-edge half-edge
problem.  The whole-problem change in
\cref{eq:marked-A0-fixed-I} gives
\[
 \KHolant(\cF^+)\equivT\Holant^Z(\Gamma),
\]
where the factor \(2\) per edge is known.  The exact local identities
\begin{equation}
 Z=XIXYX,
 \qquad
 X=-ZIZYZ
 \label{eq:marked-A0-edge-switch}
\end{equation}
give a signature-preserving open-network equivalence
\begin{equation}
 \Holant^Z(\Gamma)\equivT\KHolant(\Gamma).
 \label{eq:marked-A0-open-equivalence}
\end{equation}
For the forward reduction, replace a \(Z\)-edge by three \(X\)-edges with the available ordered vertices \(I,Y\). For the reverse reduction, replace an \(X\)-edge by three \(Z\)-edges with the same two
vertices.  Reversing the ordered \(Y\)-vertex changes only its recorded
nonzero sign.  Define
\[
 \Gamma^\sharp:=\Gamma\cup\{Z\}.
\]
The first identity in \cref{eq:marked-A0-edge-switch} directly realizes
\(Z\) in the \(X\)-edge model, so adding it changes neither the
problem nor the set of directly realizable signatures.

The two edge presentations therefore have the same projective set
\(\mathscr B\) of directly realizable nonsingular ordered binary tensors.
In the \(Z\)-edge model, open-network transport and
\cref{eq:marked-A0-conjugacy} give
\[
 \{[bZ]:b\in\mathscr B\}
 =P^{-1}\DihXzero{2m}P
 =\DihZ{2m}.
\]
Since \(m\) is even,
\([Y]=[XD(-1)]\in\DihZ{2m}\).  Hence, in the \(X\)-edge
presentation,
\hypertarget{eq:marked-A0-complete-group}{}
\begin{equation*}
 G(\Gamma^\sharp)
 =\{[bX]:b\in\mathscr B\}
 =\{[bZ][ZX]:b\in\mathscr B\}
 =\DihZ{2m}[Y]
 =\DihZ{2m}.
\end{equation*}

The complete deck transports as well.  Under \(P^{-1}\), the original
\(X\)-edge deck is the corresponding \(Z\)-edge deck.  Translating any
target dressing or closing edge by the second identity in
\cref{eq:marked-A0-edge-switch}, and then absorbing the inserted \(I,Y\)
binary chain into the adjacent fixed chosen representative, merely permutes
the \(\DihZ{2m}\) labels.  The same argument with the first identity gives
the inverse correspondence.  Thus zeros, matching products, factorization trees,
minimum arity of a nonbinary irreducible signature, and parity of the irreducible factors' arities correspond.  Therefore
\[
 \Stable(\Gamma^\sharp,\DihZ{2m},f^{(X,0)}),
 \qquad
 \arity(f^{(X,0)})=\nu(\Gamma^\sharp)=\arity(f),
 \qquad
 \Even(\Gamma^\sharp).
\]

Apply \cref{thm:normalized-dihedral}.  Every resolved complexity leaf, concrete factor
batch, and strict successor transports back through the two open-network
equivalences.  On a surviving tractable case with a common presentation, there are
\(\cC\in\{\cA,\cP,\cL\}\) and \(V\in\GL_2(\AlgNums)\) such that
\[
 V^{-1}\Gamma^\sharp\subseteq\cC,
 \qquad
 E:=V^{\mathsf T}XV\in\cC.
\]
Set \(W:=PV\).  Then \(W^{-1}\cF\subseteq\cC\).  Because
\(Z\in\Gamma^\sharp\), also
\[
 F:=V^{-1}ZV^{-\mathsf T}\in\cC.
\]
The binaries \(E,F\) are nonsingular and symmetric, so
\cref{lem:symmetric-binary-pullback} and \(XZX=-Z\) give
\[
 EFE=V^{\mathsf T}XZXV=-V^{\mathsf T}ZV,
 \qquad
 W^{\mathsf T}XW
 =2V^{\mathsf T}ZV
 =-2EFE\in\cC.
\]
Thus \(W\) is a common change-of-basis matrix for the original problem.  The
\(\cA,\cL\) cases are resolved tractable cases satisfying \(\Tract(K\cF)\) and \(\cP\) is
the remaining tractable case.  The witness \(W=PV\) combines the
whole-network equivalence with the transformed common presentation.
\(I\) remains available throughout.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The weighted \texorpdfstring{\(\DihXone{2m}\)}{D2m (X,1)} case}
\label{subsec:weighted-dihedral-case}

In the shifted dihedral case, a matching describes the support, but
factorization also requires the coefficients to split across its edges.
We test this by joining two copies. The resulting identities force the
ratio for changing one edge assignment to be independent of the others.
We develop the test in standard coordinates and then show how to realize
the required contractions with the weighted kernels of \(\DihXone{2m}\).

\begin{theorem}[\(\DihXone{2m}\)-sector deck rigidity]
\label{thm:marked-A-rigidity}
Assume
\[
 \Stable(\cF,\DihXone{2m},f),\qquad \Even(\cF),
 \qquad m\ge4,\quad 2\mid m.
\]
Then \(\Close_{\{\cP\}}(\cF,\DihXone{2m},f)\).
\end{theorem}

\subsubsection{Relative stability and endpoint compatibility}
\label{subsubsec:nd-relative-endpoints}

We work in the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) coordinates up to and including
\cref{lem:nd-relative-reentry}, using the
\hyperlink{eq:nd-sector-pencils}{standard kernels and sector pencils}
with \(m\ge3\) and even arity \(n=2k+2\ge6\). The support and coefficient
reconstruction applies to the selected occurrences under the deck hypotheses
stated below. \Cref{lem:marked-A-gauge-transport,lem:marked-A-primitive-transport}
then transfer these results to the weighted \(\DihXone{2m}\) kernels.
We begin by specifying relative deck stability.

\begin{definition}[Occurrence-level relative deck stability]
\label{def:occurrence-relative-deck-stability}
For a retained occurrence \(h\) at current reduction data \((\cF,G)\), write
\[
 \operatorname{RelStable}(\cF,G;h)
\]
when its complete oriented deck has been regenerated,
every already obtained proper card and its complete factorization tree
have been processed, the complete group remains \(G\), and, on the continuing branch,
every surviving nonzero proper card is a \(G\)-matching signature, while every
unresolved lower-arity nonbinary occurrence has been placed earlier in the
occurrence induction.  An active deck-stable minimum occurrence is
automatically relatively deck-stable.
\end{definition}

Under this hypothesis, the proper outputs needed below have already
been processed. \Cref{lem:nd-relative-reentry} checks the use of the
reconstruction results with this hypothesis, and
\cref{proofsubsec:nd-completion} records their occurrence-level descent.

For a fixed deleted pair \(p=\{i,j\}\), a \emph{residual matching} of a
nonzero card
\[
 P=\bigotimes_{\{u,v\}\in\mathcal M_p}B_{uv}
\]
is the perfect matching \(\mathcal M_p\) of the residual inputs
\([n]\setminus p\).  Its matching translation space is
\[
 M(\mathcal M_p)=
 \Span\{{\mathbf{e}}_u+{\mathbf{e}}_v:\{u,v\}\in\mathcal M_p\}.
\]
The even and odd families have the same residual matching at \(p\)
when their unordered edge sets agree, equivalently when their matching
translation spaces agree.
Support-parity and ordered factor-class disagreements remain separate
discrepancies.  For example, on residual inputs \(3,4,5,6\), the pairings
\(\{\{3,4\},\{5,6\}\}\) in both sectors give the same matching, whereas that
pairing and \(\{\{3,5\},\{4,6\}\}\) form a matching disagreement whose
union contains an alternating four-cycle.  The support integration below relates these local matchings for
different deleted pairs.

For the following support comparison suppose that both sector
pencils have type \(\mathsf{C}\). All ordered pairs containing \(\mathsf Z\) or \(\mathsf P\) are
handled separately in \cref{lem:app-nd-endpoint-type} below.

Compare one realizable nonzero even card with one realizable nonzero odd card at the fixed
deleted pair \(p\).  If their residual matchings differ, or their ordered factor classes differ
on at least two edges (including parity changes), this is a cross-sector
disagreement and is consumed
by \cref{lem:app-nd-localization,lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer}.  Hence the
continuing branch has one common residual matching with all nondesignated
factor classes aligned.

If the two sector rulings have the same residual matching but designate
different varying edges, close only their common fixed factors.  Up to affine
translations, the remaining support, in the distinguished bits \(x,y\) and
the two varying-edge bits \(u,v\), is
\begin{equation*}
  \begin{aligned}
    x=y&\ \Longrightarrow\ u=x+\alpha,\quad v\text{ free},\\
    x\ne y&\ \Longrightarrow\ v=x+\beta,\quad u\text{ free}.
  \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
A realizable parity kernel imposing \(u=x+\alpha\) has a unique lift and leaves
a six-string quaternary signature, hence not a two-monomial product.  Its
support contains a complementary pair. Realizable logical \(X\)-dressings
may move that pair to the two endpoints.  Add this quaternary signature and
its irreducible factors.  A retained nonbinary factor has arity at
most four: for the active minimum-arity nonbinary irreducible signature it is a strict lower-\(\nu\)
successor, and for a later occurrence it is assigned to the lower-arity
occurrence induction.  Stabilize the state of that quaternary occurrence, regenerate its complete
oriented deck, and only then invoke the equality-accessible quaternary
boundary and eight-vertex reduction.  Thus the different-edge branch is closed.
We may therefore assume below that both sectors use one common varying
edge.

To assemble the four slices into one affine support, their offsets must
fit the same affine translation space. After aligning the common factors,
write the four pencil-endpoint supports as
\begin{equation*}
  \supp h_\xi={\mathbf{a}}_\xi+L,
  \qquad
  h_{00}=A,\ h_{01}=B,\ h_{10}=C,\ h_{11}=D_{11},
\end{equation*}
where
\begin{equation*}
  L=\Span\{{\mathbf{e}}_{u_r}+{\mathbf{e}}_{v_r}:1\le r<k\},
  \qquad
  \dim L=k-1.
\end{equation*}
Define the endpoint displacement vectors
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbf{d}}_E={\mathbf{a}}_{00}+{\mathbf{a}}_{11},
  \qquad
  {\mathbf{d}}_O={\mathbf{a}}_{01}+{\mathbf{a}}_{10}.
\end{equation*}
Then the realizable even/odd card support translation spaces are
\begin{equation*}
  L_E=L+\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{d}}_E\},
  \qquad
  L_O=L+\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{d}}_O\},
  \qquad {\mathbf{d}}_E,{\mathbf{d}}_O\notin L.
\end{equation*}
The condition we need is
\begin{equation}
 {\mathbf{a}}_{00}+{\mathbf{a}}_{01}+{\mathbf{a}}_{10}+{\mathbf{a}}_{11}\in L
 \label{eq:app-nd-parallelogram}
\end{equation}
Equivalently,
\[
 {\mathbf{d}}_E+{\mathbf{d}}_O\in L,
 \qquad
 [{\mathbf{a}}_{00}]+[{\mathbf{a}}_{11}]=[{\mathbf{a}}_{01}]+[{\mathbf{a}}_{10}]
       \quad\text{in }\Ftwo^{n-2}/L,
 \qquad
 L+\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{d}}_E\}=L+\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{d}}_O\}.
\]
This condition is independent of the representatives
\({\mathbf{a}}_\xi\), since changing one representative changes the four-term sum by
an element of \(L\).

\begin{lemma}[Realizable-card endpoint test]
\label{lem:app-nd-parallelogram}
In the complementary-axis \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\) branch with one common varying edge, the
residual matchings of
the realizable nonzero cards represented by \(E(t)\) and \(O(s)\) agree if
and only if
\cref{eq:app-nd-parallelogram} holds.
Failure is localized using the realizable cards, not the endpoint slices.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
A monomial matching is determined by its weight-two strings, so the two
matchings agree exactly when their translation spaces agree.  Since
\({\mathbf{d}}_E,{\mathbf{d}}_O\notin L\),
\[
  L_E=L_O
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  {\mathbf{d}}_E+{\mathbf{d}}_O\in L
  \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  {\mathbf{a}}_{00}+{\mathbf{a}}_{01}+{\mathbf{a}}_{10}+{\mathbf{a}}_{11}\in L.
\]
Failure therefore invokes
\cref{lem:app-nd-localization,lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer} on the realizable
cards and is resolved.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Zero and proportional endpoint pencils]
\label{lem:app-nd-exceptional-endpoints}
Every endpoint pair in which at least one sector has type \(\mathsf Z\) or \(\mathsf P\)
yields at least one of the following concrete outcomes:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the branch is impossible because it would give \(f=0\);
\item all four slices have one common residual tensor, giving an exact
      binary--residual factorization of \(f\);
\item all but one residual matching factor are common, giving an exact
      factorization \(f=P\otimes q\), where \(q\) is a nonzero quaternary
      factor on the deleted pair and the exceptional residual edge;
\item realizable nonzero cards have a disagreement, which is localized and
      consumed by \cref{lem:app-nd-localization,lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer}.
\end{enumerate}
In item~3 the quaternary has matching-flattening rank two whenever one of
the two pencils has type \(\mathsf{C}\).  The factor \(q\) is available by the factor theorem and is a strict lower-\(\nu\) successor.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a type-\(\mathsf Z\) even pencil, two-card inversion gives \(A=D_{11}=0\), hence the
support condition \(x_i\oplus x_j=1\).  If the odd pencil also has type
\(\mathsf Z\), then \(f=0\), contrary to the active nonzero irreducible signature.  If it has type
\(\mathsf P\), the same inversion gives
\begin{equation*}
 f=G_p\otimes H_O,
  \qquad
  G_p=\begin{pmatrix}0&b\\ c&0\end{pmatrix},
\end{equation*}
where \(G_p\) is the displayed binary signature on \(p\) and \(H_O\) is the
complementary \((n-2)\)-ary signature.  This is an exact factorization of \(f\), and its factors
are exposed only by \cref{thm:external-factor}.  If the odd pencil has type
\(\mathsf C\), write its exact ruling as \(O(t)=P_O\otimes K_O(t)\), where \(P_O\)
is the product of the \(k-1\) fixed residual factors and \(K_O(t)\) is the
nonproportional binary pencil on its varying edge \(e\).  Two-card inversion
puts both \(B\) and \(C\) in \(P_O\otimes\Mat_2(\mathbb C)\). The zero
slices \(A,D_{11}\) lie there as well.  Hence
\[
 f=P_O\otimes q_{p,e}.
\]
The complementary-axis endpoints of \(K_O\) are nonproportional, so the
\(p\mid e\) logical flattening of \(q_{p,e}\) has rank two.  Apply
    \cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation}. The quaternary available by reduction is a
deck-stable lower-\(\nu\) successor, while every binary factor is sent through
\cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness}.  Exchanging the sectors covers every
type-\(\mathsf Z\) case.  A single zero sample in a type-\(\mathsf P\) pencil is included in
the exact proportional inversion and is never used by itself to infer a
factor.

For a proportional even pencil, inversion gives
\begin{equation*}
  A=aH_E,
  \qquad
  D_{11}=dH_E.
\end{equation*}
Choose a nonzero realizable even card as the representative of the line
\(\mathbb C H_E\), and compare it with two nonzero realizable odd cards.  Any
matching change or variation on at least two residual factor classes,
including parity changes, is a disagreement and is consumed by
\cref{lem:app-nd-localization,lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer}.  If no bounded
output occurs, the two sectors have one common residual matching and differ
on at most one designated edge \(e\).  Let \(P\) be the product of all fixed
factors outside \(e\).  Four-card inversion then puts
\(A,B,C,D_{11}\in P\otimes\Mat_2(\mathbb C)\), and therefore proves the exact
identity
\[
 f=P\otimes q_{p,e}.
\]
If no edge differs, absorb the last common factor into \(P\). This is the
stronger identity \(f=G_p\otimes H\).  If one edge differs, the remaining
quaternary is nonzero. In a \(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{C}\) or \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{P}\) case its \(p\mid e\) flattening has
rank two because the \(\mathsf C\)-endpoints are nonproportional.  In the \(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{P}\)
case different projective factor classes give the same rank-two conclusion, while
equal classes give the stronger binary--residual factorization.  Only after
this exact identity has been proved are \(P\) and \(q_{p,e}\) exposed by
\cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation}.  This covers \(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{P}\),
\(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{C}\), \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{P}\), and their possible zero samples.
\end{proof}

By \cref{lem:app-nd-exceptional-endpoints}, it remains to analyze two
nonproportional sector pencils.

We now describe the six-input contraction output obtained by retaining
the deleted pair and two residual edges. Label these inputs by
\(p=\{1,2\}\) and \(R=\{3,4,5,6\}\).  For each sector
\(\epsilon=x_1\oplus x_2\), let
\(M_\epsilon=\{e_\epsilon,e'_\epsilon\}\) be its residual matching, where
\(e_\epsilon=\{u_\epsilon,v_\epsilon\}\) is the fixed edge and
\(e'_\epsilon=(r_\epsilon,s_\epsilon)\) is the ordered varying edge.  There
are parameters \(a_\epsilon,b_\epsilon,o_\epsilon\in\Ftwo\) such that its
six-input support is parametrized by
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 (x_1,x_2)&=(z,z+\epsilon),\\
 x_{u_\epsilon}\oplus x_{v_\epsilon}&=a_\epsilon,\\
 (x_{r_\epsilon},x_{s_\epsilon})
   &=(o_\epsilon+z,o_\epsilon+z+b_\epsilon),
 \end{aligned}
 \qquad \epsilon,z\in\Ftwo.
 \label{eq:app-nd-four-flat-support}
\end{equation}
For fixed \((\epsilon,z)\), the parity equation on the fixed edge has two
solutions and the varying edge is determined.  Hence each of the four
endpoint slices indexed by \((\epsilon,z)\) is a two-string affine flat, each
sector has four strings, and the two sectors have the eight-string support used
in \cref{eq:app-nd-four-flat-support}.  The varying-edge assignments for
\(z=0,1\) are bitwise complements.  The coefficients on these slices need not be equal.

For a perfect matching \(M\) on \(2r\) inputs and parities
\((c_e)_{e\in M}\), the associated \emph{matching coset} is
\[
 \{{\mathbf{x}}\in\Ftwo^{2r}:x_u+x_v=c_{\{u,v\}}
       \text{ for every }\{u,v\}\in M\}.
\]

\begin{lemma}[A six-to-four contraction]
\label{lem:app-nd-six-to-four}
Suppose a standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) localization leaves exactly the distinguished
pair \(p\) and two residual monomial pairs, with support as in \cref{eq:app-nd-four-flat-support}.  Then either the six-input support is a single three-edge
matching coset, or a realizable \(I/X\)-contraction realizes a nonzero
quaternary of support size six or eight.  In the latter case, the output is
not a product of current-group monomial binaries and the procedure does not
re-enter a six-input state.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The explicit support in \cref{eq:app-nd-four-flat-support} is the Boolean
family exhaustively covered by
\cref{thm:cert-interface-nd-q6}.  That classification either identifies
the global matching coset or supplies a pair and parity \(\gamma\) with
injective projection.  Contracting it by the realizable parity-\(\gamma\)
kernel---\(I\) for \(\gamma=0\) and \(X\) for \(\gamma=1\)---gives one
nonzero internal extension per string,
so there is no cancellation and the output signature has the certified
six- or eight-string support.  Add this direct output and close its complete
factor batch.  A singular/unsafe factor, unary or odd factor, or a smaller
irreducible signature is handled by the stabilized procedure. A safe binary whose transfer class lies outside the
current group gives a successor with a strictly larger complete transfer group.  If the output itself
is irreducible, retain it as a lower-arity quaternary occurrence, stabilize its state, and
regenerate its complete oriented deck, and only then invoke the
equality-accessible quaternary boundary and eight-vertex reduction.  Since a product of two
nonsingular current-group monomial binaries has support size four, the
support-six/eight output cannot close as an unrecorded matching product.
This completes the reduction of the arity-six tensor through its
four-input output and factor batch.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Reduction of the endpoint cases]
\label{lem:app-nd-endpoint-type}
For every deleted pair \(p\), all endpoint combinations containing type
\(\mathsf Z\) or \(\mathsf P\) are resolved.  On the remaining branch, the
endpoint pair is \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\), \cref{eq:app-nd-parallelogram} holds, and there is one
common residual matching.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The \(\mathsf{Z}/\mathsf{Z}\) entry gives \(f=0\).  Every \(\mathsf{Z}/\mathsf{P}\), \(\mathsf{Z}/\mathsf{C}\), \(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{P}\),
\(\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{C}\), and transposed entry is covered by
\cref{lem:app-nd-exceptional-endpoints}.

It remains to take both types equal to \(\mathsf C\).  A matching change or two
different ordered factor classes invokes the trace cover
\cref{lem:app-nd-localization,lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer}.  After closing
common factors with the same projective class, two different variable edges give the six-string quaternary constructed above. Apply the lower-arity stabilized-quaternary route
given there.  For one common variable edge, failure of
\cref{eq:app-nd-parallelogram} is again a realizable-card disagreement.  If the
parallelogram holds, then, because \(n\ge6\), retain the varying edge and one
fixed residual edge together with \(p\). This gives the arity-six tensor
of \cref{eq:app-nd-four-flat-support}. By \cref{lem:app-nd-six-to-four},
any nonmatching quaternary output is processed through its full factor batch.
The sole support-level remaining case is a three-edge matching coset.
These alternatives exhaust \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\).
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Global support integration and the Reed--Muller alternative}
\label{proofsubsec:nd-global-support}

The \(\mathsf Z/\mathsf P/\mathsf C\) endpoint classification has resolved every case
containing \(\mathsf Z\) or \(\mathsf P\) and every
\(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\) family with a disagreement. The remaining case satisfies
\cref{eq:app-nd-parallelogram}, and the two sectors have the same residual
matching at every deleted pair.  These local translation spaces determine a self-dual code. A weight-two
codeword identifies a matched pair and then the global matching coset.
If there is no such codeword, the weight-four supports determine
\(RM(1,3)\). A realizable contraction with a minor proportional to
\(1-t^2\) excludes that remaining case.

\begin{lemma}[Endpoint-support saturation]
\label{lem:app-nd-endpoint-support-saturation}
Assume the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) analysis is on the remaining endpoint-
type branch.  Then, for each deleted pair
\(p=\{i,j\}\), there is a fixed-edge matching translation space \(L_p\) on the
residual coordinates and endpoint differences \({\mathbf{d}}_{E,p},{\mathbf{d}}_{O,p}\notin L_p\)
such that the four endpoint slices have full support on affine cosets
\({\mathbf{a}}_{ab}+L_p\), with every coefficient on each such coset nonzero.  Moreover
\[
 M_p:=L_p+\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{d}}_{E,p}\}
     =L_p+\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{d}}_{O,p}\}
\]
is the support translation space of the equal- and unequal-\(p\) realizable cards. It is the
translation space \(M(\mathcal M_p)\) of the residual matching coset,
abbreviated \(M_p\), and hence \(M_p=M_p^\perp\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On the remaining \(\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{C}\) branch,
\cref{lem:app-nd-sector-ruling} gives one fixed
nonzero monomial factor on every residual matching edge except the designated
variable edge.  Two samples with nonzero output and \cref{eq:app-nd-parallelogram} reconstruct
each slice by the four-card inversion used in
\cref{lem:app-nd-coefficient-line-factor}. Hence every string in the resulting
affine matching coset has a nonzero coefficient.  More explicitly, the two
complementary-axis endpoint matrices have disjoint monomial supports, so each
assignment to the remaining inputs is supplied by exactly one endpoint. Its coefficient is a
product of nonzero fixed monomial entries and a nonzero endpoint entry, with
no cancellation between endpoints. For
\({\mathbf{d}}_{E,p}={\mathbf{a}}_{00}+{\mathbf{a}}_{11}\) and
\({\mathbf{d}}_{O,p}={\mathbf{a}}_{01}+{\mathbf{a}}_{10}\), the parallelogram equation says
\({\mathbf{d}}_{E,p}+{\mathbf{d}}_{O,p}\in L_p\), which identifies the two displayed descriptions
of \(M_p\) and the four offsets.  Matchings belonging to different deleted
pairs are integrated into one common code by
\cref{lem:app-nd-support-integration}, rather than compared directly here.
\end{proof}

\phantomsection
\label{par:reed-muller-code}

For \(d\ge1\),
\[
 \operatorname{AGL}(d,2):=\Ftwo^d\rtimes\GL_d(\Ftwo)
\]
acts by \({\mathbf{x}}\mapsto A{\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{b}}\).  An \emph{affine relabelling} of inputs indexed by
\(\Ftwo^d\) is the corresponding permutation of those inputs.  Indexing the
eight coordinates by \(\Ftwo^3\) in lexicographic order, define the
first-order Reed--Muller code by
\[
 RM(1,3):=
 \{(\ell({\mathbf{t}}))_{{\mathbf{t}}\in\Ftwo^3}:
   \ell({\mathbf{t}})=a+u_1t_1+u_2t_2+u_3t_3,\ 
   a,u_1,u_2,u_3\in\Ftwo\}.
\]
Thus \(RM(1,3)\) consists of the sixteen truth tables of affine-linear
functions on \(\Ftwo^3\).  The natural action satisfies
\(\operatorname{AGL}(2,2)\cong S_4\).
\(\operatorname{AGL}(3,2)\) is two-transitive and preserves \(RM(1,3)\),
because affine precomposition preserves affine truth tables
\cite[Section~4.1]{Carlet2021}.

We now regard the support as a set of binary strings, using the projections
and affine operations of \cref{subsubsec:boolean-affine-tools}.  The
local matching equations will determine a self-dual binary code.  A
weight-two codeword identifies a global matched pair. If no such codeword
exists, the weight-four codewords identify the eight-coordinate Reed--Muller
code.  This reconstructs support only. Coefficients are treated afterward.

For a function
\(F:\Ftwo^d\to R\) into an abelian group, its additive derivative is
\[
 (\Delta_{\mathbf{a}}F)({\mathbf{x}}):=F({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{a}})-F({\mathbf{x}}).
\]
The identities \(\Delta_{\mathbf{a}}\Delta_{\mathbf{b}}F=0\) for all \({\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{b}}\) hold exactly when
\(F({\mathbf{x}})=F(0)+\ell({\mathbf{x}})\) for a group homomorphism
\(\ell:(\Ftwo^d,+)\to(R,+)\): the identities make every
\(\Delta_{\mathbf{a}}F\) constant, and the resulting
\(\ell({\mathbf{x}}):=F({\mathbf{x}})-F(0)\) is additive.

\begin{lemma}[Self-dual Steiner reconstruction]
\label{lem:self-dual-steiner-reconstruction}
Let \(D\le\Ftwo^n\) be self-dual, where \(n>4\), and suppose that \(D\)
has no codeword of weight two.  If every three distinct coordinates lie in the
support of a unique weight-four codeword of \(D\), then \(n=8\). After
identifying the coordinates with \(\Ftwo^3\), one has \(D=RM(1,3)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The weight-four supports form a \emph{Steiner quadruple system}: a family of
four-element blocks in which every three coordinates belong to exactly one
block.  Self-orthogonality
of \(D\) makes two blocks meet evenly. Uniqueness through triples then makes
the intersection of two distinct blocks either empty or a pair.

Fix a coordinate \(0\).  Define a binary operation \(+\) on the coordinates
by \(x+x=0\), \(x+0=x\), and, for distinct nonzero \(x,y\), let \(x+y\) be
the fourth point in the block through
\(0,x,y\).  This operation is commutative and every element has order two.
For the associativity check, define the auxiliary points \(u:=x+y\),
\(v:=y+z\), and \(w:=u+z\).  The symmetric
difference of the blocks \(\{0,x,y,u\}\) and \(\{0,y,z,v\}\) is the
weight-four codeword \(\{x,u,z,v\}\).  Symmetric difference with
\(\{0,u,z,w\}\) gives the block \(\{0,x,v,w\}\).  Uniqueness of the
block through \(0,x,v\) gives
\[
 (x+y)+z=w=x+v=x+(y+z).
\]
If an auxiliary point coincides---for example \(z=x+y\)---the same identity
reduces immediately to \(x+x=0\). Thus the displayed distinct-block
calculation and these collision cases cover all inputs.  The coordinates form an
elementary abelian group \(V\cong\Ftwo^r\), and \(n=2^r\).  If
\(\{a,b,c,d\}\) is any block, symmetric difference with the block through
\(0,a,b\) shows \(d=a+b+c\). Hence the blocks are exactly the affine
planes of \(V\).

Let \(B\) be the binary span of the block indicator vectors.  An affine plane is a translate of a two-dimensional
subspace of \(V\).  Orthogonality to every such plane is equivalent to
vanishing of every second additive derivative
\(\Delta_{\mathbf{a}}\Delta_{\mathbf{b}}F({\mathbf{x}})\). Hence \(B^\perp\) is exactly the space of truth
tables of affine-linear functions \(F:V\to\Ftwo\).  Thus
\(\dim B=2^r-r-1\).  Since \(B\subseteq D\) and
\(\dim D=2^{r-1}\), the inequality \(2^r-r-1\le2^{r-1}\), with \(r>2\),
forces \(r=3\). Equality gives \(D=B=RM(1,3)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Support integration]
\label{lem:app-nd-support-integration}
Assume the remaining endpoint-type case, so that
\cref{eq:app-nd-parallelogram} holds for every deleted pair.  Then
\(\supp(f)={\mathbf{a}}+D\) for a self-dual binary linear code \(D\le\Ftwo^n\).
Exactly one of the following support alternatives remains:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \(D\) is the matching translation space of one perfect matching on
      all \(n\) inputs, so \({\mathbf{a}}+D\) is one \emph{global matching coset};
\item \(n=8\) and, after relabelling the inputs, \(D=RM(1,3)\).
\end{enumerate}
The second alternative is incompatible with the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) deck for \(m\ge3\).  Consequently the sole
remaining support is one global matching coset.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply \cref{lem:app-nd-endpoint-support-saturation}.  Translation by the
\(00\)-slice gives, for any fixed deleted pair \(p\),
\begin{equation*}
  {\mathbf{a}}+D,\qquad
  D=(\{00\}\times L_p)
    +\Span\{(01,{\mathbf{a}}_{00}+{\mathbf{a}}_{01}),
             (10,{\mathbf{a}}_{00}+{\mathbf{a}}_{10})\}.
\end{equation*}
The two displayed generators are independent modulo
\(\{00\}\times L_p\). Hence \(\dim D=n/2\).  For an arbitrary deleted
pair \(p=\{i,j\}\), define the equal-coordinate slice
\(H_p:=\{{\mathbf{x}}\in D:x_i=x_j\}\).  The endpoint saturation and
the parallelogram give
\[
  \pi_{\{i,j\}}:D\longrightarrow\Ftwo^2\ \text{surjective},\qquad
  \pi_{[n]\setminus\{i,j\}}(H_{\{i,j\}})=M_{\{i,j\}},
\]
where \(M_p=M_p^\perp\) is the residual matching translation space from
\cref{lem:app-nd-endpoint-support-saturation}.  To see
self-orthogonality, take arbitrary \({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in D\).  Among the \(n>4\)
coordinate columns
\((x_\ell,y_\ell)\in\Ftwo^2\), two are equal. Relabel them \(i,j\).  Then
\({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}\in H_p\), so their residual projections lie in \(M_p\) and have zero
mutual dot product.  Their contribution on the deleted pair is
\(x_i y_i+x_j y_j=0\).  Hence \({\mathbf{x}}\cdot {\mathbf{y}}=0\), proving
\(D\subseteq D^\perp\).  Since \(\dim D=n/2\), equality follows:
\(D=D^\perp\).

Suppose first that \({\mathbf{e}}_u+{\mathbf{e}}_v\in D\).  Self-duality implies
\(x_u=x_v\) for every \({\mathbf{x}}\in D\), so \(H_{\{u,v\}}=D\).  Put
\(E=[n]\setminus\{u,v\}\).  Endpoint saturation gives
\[
 \pi_E(D)=M_{\{u,v\}},
\]
the translation space of the residual perfect matching.  For
\({\mathbf{y}}\in M_{\{u,v\}}\), choose a lift \({\mathbf{x}}\in D\).  Subtracting
\(x_u({\mathbf{e}}_u+{\mathbf{e}}_v)\) gives the zero extension \(\iota_E({\mathbf{y}})\in D\).  Hence
\[
 D=\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{e}}_u+{\mathbf{e}}_v\}
   \oplus\iota_E(M_{\{u,v\}}),
\]
which is exactly the translation space of the global perfect matching
\(\{\{u,v\}\}\cup\mathcal M_{\{u,v\}}\).  This is a support identity
only: it does not yet assert a tensor factorization of the coefficients.

It remains that \(D\) has no weight-two codeword.  Fix \(i\ne j\) and put
\(E=[n]\setminus\{i,j\}\).  The projection
\(H_{\{i,j\}}\to\Ftwo^E\) is injective, because its only possible nonzero nullspace
is \({\mathbf{e}}_i+{\mathbf{e}}_j\).  The functional \({\mathbf{x}}\mapsto x_i+x_j\) is nonzero on \(D\),
since otherwise \({\mathbf{e}}_i+{\mathbf{e}}_j\in D^\perp=D\).  Thus
\(\dim H_{\{i,j\}}=n/2-1\), and endpoint saturation identifies its image with
\(M_{\{i,j\}}\).  For a third coordinate \(\ell\), the residual matching
pairs \(\ell\) with a unique \(q\).  The unique lift of
\({\mathbf{e}}_\ell+{\mathbf{e}}_q\in M_{\{i,j\}}\) cannot have \(00\) on \(i,j\), since that would
be a weight-two codeword.  It is therefore
\({\mathbf{e}}_i+{\mathbf{e}}_j+{\mathbf{e}}_\ell+{\mathbf{e}}_q\in D\).  Hence every three coordinates lie in a unique
weight-four support, and
\cref{lem:self-dual-steiner-reconstruction} gives \(n=8\) and
\(D=RM(1,3)\).

To exclude this residue, identify the eight coordinate positions with
\(\Ftwo^3\) and, after an affine relabelling, take the deleted pair to be
\(\{000,001\}\).  On its equal-coordinate slice the residual matching is
\[
 \{010,011\},\qquad \{100,101\},\qquad \{110,111\}.
\]
If \(y_1,y_2,y_3\) are the three logical matching bits, the common bit on
the deleted pair is
\[
 b=y_1+y_2+y_3.
\]
Contract the deleted pair by the realizable sector phase \(t\).  The projection
of the equal slice is injective, so every string in the residual support has one
internal lift and its coefficient is multiplied by \(t^b\).  Fixing
\(y_3\) and inspecting the \(2\times2\) face indexed by \(y_1,y_2\), the
phase exponents are \((0,1,1,0)\), or their complement.  Thus the face has
one of the raw forms
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}A&tB\\tC&D\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 \begin{pmatrix}tA&B\\C&tD\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad A,B,C,D\ne0.
\]
The realizable \(t=1\) card lies in the fixed Segre ruling, so \(AD=BC\).
Nonzero row and column scalings therefore give, for some
\(\varepsilon\in\Ftwo\), the normalized block in the original basis
\hypertarget{eq:app-nd-RM-minor}{}
\begin{equation*}
 \begin{aligned}
  H_t&=\kappa\diag(r_0,r_1)C_\varepsilon(t)\diag(s_0,s_1),\\
  C_0(t)&=\begin{pmatrix}1&t\\t&1\end{pmatrix},
  &C_1(t)&=\begin{pmatrix}t&1\\1&t\end{pmatrix},\\
  \det H_t&=(-1)^\varepsilon
      \kappa^2r_0r_1s_0s_1(1-t^2).
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
with all scalar factors nonzero.  If an orientation
produces \(t^{-1}\), clear its nonzero monomial factor.  Since \(m\ge3\),
some realizable \(t\in\mu_m\setminus\{\pm1\}\) exists, and the displayed
nonzero minor contradicts the fixed Segre ruling.
\end{proof}

The support is therefore one global matching coset. To obtain a tensor
factorization, we still need to separate its coefficients. We first
handle the degenerate six-input coefficient tensors, then use two-copy
outputs to prove the coefficient product formula.

\subsubsection{The six-input coefficient calculation}
\label{proofsubsec:nd-six-port}

We now return to the matching-supported six-input coefficient cases retained
by the support analysis.  Trace-cover six-input disagreements have already been
reduced to quaternary and resolved. The only remaining case is the fixed
three-edge-matching coefficient pattern.  This part first handles
all degenerate coefficient cases and then analyzes the coefficient tensor with all eight entries nonzero.

For a quaternary contraction whose support is contained in two fixed residual
matching edges, write its logical coefficient matrix as
\[
 Q=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\c&d\end{pmatrix}.
\]
We include the cases \(0<|\supp Q|<4\).  A \emph{support-matching disagreement} means that its support in the original variables uses a residual pairing different from the fixed pairing. This is
the combinatorial disagreement of the trace-cover reduction.  By contrast,
\emph{not \(G\)-matching} means that the signature has no tensor
factorization into current-group binaries.  These two notions are not
interchangeable.

For each directly realized quaternary, first add it with its input order, scalar, and reduction. Jointly add all its irreducible factors. Test all three \(2+2\)
flattenings and consider every exposed factor. Make a realizable endpoint
dressing. Invoke \cref{lem:finite-q4-dispatch} only after tensor
irreducibility, endpoint nondegeneracy, non-\(G\)-matching status, and both
crossed rank hypotheses have been verified.

\begin{lemma}[Reductions of six-input coefficient tensors]
\label{lem:nd-six-coefficient-dispatch}
Let \(h\) be a directly realized six-input signature whose support is exactly the
eight-string coset of a fixed three-edge matching.  Before a quotient of its logical coefficients is
formed, close every already constructed four- or six-input batch as follows:
\begin{enumerate}
\item a zero quaternary is converted into an exact factorization of its parent arity-six tensor by
      the proportional-slice identity below;
\item a support-matching disagreement is sent to
      \cref{lem:app-nd-localization,lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer};
\item for a quaternary on the two retained matching edges, test \(Q\): if
      \(\det Q=0\), factor it exactly. If \(\det Q\ne0\), use the
      quaternary reduction just described;
\item jointly close all outputs under irreducible factors and consider every unary,
      singular, unsafe, infinite-order, or safe binary factor whose transfer class lies outside \(G\);
\item if no preceding concrete output applies, retain \(h\) only when all
      eight logical coefficients are nonzero.
\end{enumerate}
A quaternary with \(0<|\supp Q|<4\) enters item~3.
Every noncurrent factor or nonmatching quaternary outcome gives a resolved leaf or
strict successor.  If a nonzero child quaternary factors into two current-group
binaries, complete that output, record its rank-one equation, and continue the
finite analysis of the same originating arity-six tensor.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let \(u_0,u_1\) be the two standard-basis binary signatures supported on
the two allowed states of a selected matching edge.  Write
\[
 h=u_0\otimes H_0+u_1\otimes H_1.
\]
An allowed phase kernel has two nonzero coefficients
\(\alpha_t,\beta_t\), and its quaternary contraction is
\[
 q_t=\alpha_tH_0+\beta_tH_1,
 \qquad \alpha_t\beta_t\ne0.
\]
If \(q_t=0\), first suppose \(H_0=0\). Then \(H_1=0\), so \(h=0\) and its token is completed directly.  Otherwise \(H_0\ne0\) and
\[
 H_1=-\frac{\alpha_t}{\beta_t}H_0,
 \qquad
 h=\left(u_0-\frac{\alpha_t}{\beta_t}u_1\right)\otimes H_0.
\]
The zero quaternary output therefore gives an exact
binary--quaternary factorization of \(h\).  Close the complete
factor batch by \cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation}.  A
nonbinary factor gives a quaternary or lower-\(\nu\) successor. Unary factors and unsafe binaries enter their established
reductions. A safe binary \(B\) with \([BX]\notin G\) strictly enlarges the complete group.  If
all irreducible factors are already safe current-group binaries, the parent is a
\(G\)-matching signature and its fixed token is completed.

Now let \(q_t\ne0\) have logical coefficient matrix \(Q\) on the two retained matching
edges.  If \(\det Q=0\), then \(Q\) has rank one and the quaternary signature has an
exact \(2+2\) factorization.  When \(0<|\supp Q|<4\), at least one
logical factor has a zero entry, so one exposed binary is singular.
If both exposed factors are safe current-group binaries, complete this quaternary output, retain the equation \(\det Q=0\), and resume the coefficient analysis
of the same parent arity-six tensor. The child factorization alone does not complete the
parent token.
If \(\det Q\ne0\), its fixed-matching flattening has rank two.  Add the
realizable card with its input order, scalar, and reduction.  If \(Q\) is
supported on exactly two entries, nonzero determinant forces
them to be opposite logical entries.  Realizable logical \(X\)-translations
then make the quaternary a nonzero generalized equality, and
\cref{lem:pure-ge} applies.  If exactly three entries are nonzero, realizable
logical flips put a nonzero opposite pair at the two endpoints, so the card is
endpoint-nondegenerate.  After factor closure and removal of every
crossed rank-one factorization, it is irreducible, non-\(G\)-matching, and
has both crossed ranks at least two.  The case with four nonzero logical coefficients is
endpoint-nondegenerate after the same endpoint choice.  Thus the
quaternary reduction just described is applicable. Invoke
\cref{lem:finite-q4-dispatch,thm:equality-q4-boundary,%
cor:p1-eight-vertex-reduced-interface} only after their hypotheses have been
verified.  A support-matching disagreement instead enters the typed
trace-cover reduction.  These routes also process every exposed factor by
\cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness}.

For the matching-supported arity-six tensor retained from
\cref{lem:app-nd-support-integration}, the support is already the complete
eight-string global-matching coset, so its eight logical coefficients are
nonzero. The case analysis processes each phase contraction produced below
and each output of a later mixed two-copy batch within the originating
fixed local queue.  We may therefore write its coefficients as \(c_{abc}\),
\(a,b,c\in\Ftwo\), and normalize \(c_{000}=1\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[One-coordinate six-input minor localization]
\label{lem:nd-q6-minor-localization}
Let \(q=(q_{ab\xi})_{a,b,\xi\in\Ftwo}\) be the logical coefficient tensor of
an arity-six tensor whose support is contained in a fixed three-edge matching.
Close every quaternary and factor batch produced below by the
quaternary reduction just described and the binary case analysis.  On any branch
with no resolved leaf or strict stable successor, fully close every factor
batch.  Each resulting arity-six tensor is then either zero, a \(G\)-matching signature, or has rank at most one in each of
\[
 a\mid(b,\xi),\qquad b\mid(a,\xi),\qquad \xi\mid(a,b)
\]
The first two alternatives also have all three ranks at most one.  Their
rank equations are recorded before their child tokens are completed, so
the conclusion holds at every allowed phase assignment in any later
two-copy batch to which this lemma is applied.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Consider \(a\mid(b,\xi)\).  Close the \(\xi\)-edge by every allowed phase,
obtaining the logical matrix pencil
\[
 A+tB
\]
in the \(a,b\) coordinates.  If \(\det(A+tB)\ne0\) at an allowed phase, the resulting quaternary signature enters the preceding nonzero-minor reduction.  If the
determinant vanishes for every \(t\in\mu_m\), it is a degree-two polynomial
with at least three roots and is therefore identically zero.

The two-dimensional rank-one-line classification is elementary.  Write
nonzero rank-one endpoints as \(A={\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf T}\) and
\(B={\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathsf T}\).  The mixed coefficient of
\(\det(A+tB)\) is, up to sign,
\(\det[{\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{x}}]\det[{\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{y}}]\).  Hence either \({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{x}}\) are proportional or
\({\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{y}}\) are proportional. Zero or proportional endpoints satisfy the same
conclusion directly.  In the first case \(A,B\) have one common
\(a\)-factor, so the \(a\mid(b,\xi)\) flattening of \(q\) has rank at most
one.  In the second case they have one common \(b\)-factor, giving an exact
binary factor of \(q\). Take factor closure and apply the factorization reduction.
If closing this factorization gives a resolved leaf or strict stable
successor, the parent branch ends.  Otherwise all exposed factors are
current-group matching factors, so \(q\) itself is a \(G\)-matching signature and all three logical flattenings have rank at most one. Record
these equations before completing its child token.  Thus every branch
continuing the parent analysis has the stated \(a\)-rank equation.
Repeating the same argument after permuting \(a,b,\xi\) proves the other two
rank bounds.
\end{proof}

After \cref{lem:nd-six-coefficient-dispatch}, all eight logical
coefficients on the continuing branch are nonzero.  Retain their notation and
normalize the remaining global-matching six-input support to
\(X^{(1,2)}\otimes X^{(3,4)}\otimes X^{(5,6)}\), where the superscript records
the ordered pair of inputs on which the binary disequality tensor \(X\) acts.
For each selected pair of matching edges, use both realizable parity-zero and
parity-one kernels.  The parity-zero contraction on the first two edges
gives
\[
 c_{000}c_{111}=c_{001}c_{110}.
\]
The parity-one contractions on the three choices of edge pair give the
remaining equalities among
\[
 c_{001}c_{110},\qquad
 c_{010}c_{101},\qquad
 c_{011}c_{100}.
\]
Every nonzero determinant is handled by the quaternary reduction just described.  Hence, on the continuing branch, all four products are equal:
\begin{equation}
  c_{000}c_{111}
  =c_{001}c_{110}
  =c_{010}c_{101}
  =c_{011}c_{100}.
  \label{eq:app-nd-six-relations}
\end{equation}
Define
\[
  \alpha=\frac{c_{100}}{c_{000}},\qquad
  \beta=\frac{c_{010}}{c_{000}},\qquad
  \gamma=\frac{c_{001}}{c_{000}}.
\]
These three quantities are exposed by explicit factor cards.
For \(\alpha\), join inputs \(3\) and \(5\), the first inputs of the second
and third matching edges, by the realizable parity-zero kernel
\(M_0(1)=I\).  The remaining logical coefficient matrix, with the first matching edge
as its row edge, is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}
 c_{000}&c_{011}\\
 c_{100}&c_{111}
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
Its determinant vanishes by \cref{eq:app-nd-six-relations}, so exact factor
extraction exposes on the first matching edge a binary with ordered ratio
\(\alpha^{\pm1}\).  Cycling the three matching edges exposes
\(\beta^{\pm1}\) and \(\gamma^{\pm1}\) in the same way.  Process every exposed binary by \cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness}. Hence on the continuing
branch
\[
 \alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\mu_m.
\]

Fuse the fixed representatives with transfers
\(D(\alpha^{-1})\),
\(D(\beta^{-1})\), and
\(D(\gamma^{-1})\) onto the three oriented matching edges, and
divide by the recorded common scalar \(c_{000}\).  Equation
\cref{eq:app-nd-six-relations} then gives
\[
 N_r({\mathbf z})=
 \begin{cases}
  1,&\wt({\mathbf z})\le1,\\
  r,&\wt({\mathbf z})\ge2,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad {\mathbf z}\in\Ftwo^3,\qquad
 r:=\frac{c_{000}c_{110}}{c_{100}c_{010}}.
\]
It remains to prove that \(r\) is also a group phase.  On this normalized six-input contraction output, contract inputs \(1\) and \(3\), the first inputs
of the first and second matching edges, by the realizable parity-one kernel
\(M_1(u)=M_{\alpha=1}(a=u)\), \(u\in\mu_m\). In the displayed input
orientation this is
\[
 M_1(u)=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\ u&0\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Write the bits on the six inputs as
\(x_1,\ldots,x_6\). The support matching means
\(x_2=1-x_1\), \(x_4=1-x_3\), and \(x_6=1-x_5\).
The kernel joining inputs \(1\) and \(3\) therefore imposes
\(x_1\oplus x_3=1\) and contributes the factor \(u^{x_1}\).
After this contraction, use \(x_2\) (with \(x_4=1-x_2\)) as the row bit
and \(x_5\) (with \(x_6=1-x_5\)) as the column bit.  Ordering the rows and
columns changes only the nonzero representative scalar or reverses
an input.
The resulting quaternary signature on inputs
\(2,4,5,6\), up to reversal and a nonzero representative scalar, is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}1&r\\ u&ur\end{pmatrix}
 =\begin{pmatrix}1\\u\end{pmatrix}
  \begin{pmatrix}1&r\end{pmatrix}.
\]
This is an exact tensor factorization, and its two binary factors have
ordered ratios \(u^{\pm1}\) and \(r^{\pm1}\).  Expose them through
\cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation} before testing the complete transfer group.  Process both factors by \cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness} within
the selected occurrence's fixed local queue.  Thus the coefficient
calculation continues only when both factors are safe with transfer classes in
\(\DihZ{2m}\).  On that branch,
\(r^{\pm1}\in\mu_m\), and hence \(r\in\mu_m\).  \hypertarget{eq:app-nd-Nr}{}
Thus the parameter in the displayed coefficient tensor \(N_r\) belongs to
\(\mu_m\).
Closing the third matching pair gives the quaternary whose logical
coefficient matrix is
\hypertarget{eq:app-nd-Qr}{}
\begin{equation*}
  Q_r=
  \begin{pmatrix}
    2&1+r\\
    1+r&2r
  \end{pmatrix},
  \qquad
  \det Q_r=-(r-1)^2.
\end{equation*}
Attach the supplied \(I\) through an \(X\)-edge at the second input of
each residual matching pair. These two realizable bit flips give a
quaternary supported on \(\{0000,0011,1100,1111\}\), with the same
logical coefficient matrix \(Q_r\). Its endpoint entries \(2,2r\) are
nonzero. For \(r\ne-1\), all four logical coefficients are nonzero, and
their cross-ratio is
\[
 \operatorname{cr}(Q_r)=\frac{(Q_r)_{00}(Q_r)_{11}}
                 {(Q_r)_{01}(Q_r)_{10}}
          =\frac{4r}{(1+r)^2}.
\]
If this support-four quaternary is affine- or local-affine-transformable,
\cref{lem:p1-eight-vertex-local-affine-collapse} reduces the latter case
to affine transformability. The diagonal normal form of
\cref{prop:p1-affine-eight-vertex-certificate} then gives an affine
reference signature on the same four-point support. On the two equal-input
pairs, a diagonal change of basis rescales the rows and columns of
\(Q_r\), preserving its cross-ratio. Clause~\textnormal{(ii)} of
\cref{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-gadget-exits} therefore gives
\(\operatorname{cr}(Q_r)\in\{1,-1\}\).
The value \(+1\) is equivalent here to
\(\det Q_r=0\), hence to \(r=1\), which is the product case removed below.
Therefore a nonproduct affine signature has \(\operatorname{cr}(Q_r)=-1\), giving
\(r+r^{-1}=-6\).  Every other outcome is handled by the quaternary reduction.
This is a direct quaternary signature with its inherited input order and nonzero
representative scalar.  Add it and its complete batch of irreducible factors
before applying the quaternary reduction.  If \(r=1\), then
\(N_1\equiv1\), so the parent arity-six tensor itself is the product of its
three matching binaries. Complete the originating fixed token after the
binary case distinction.  If \(r\ne1\), the displayed fixed-matching minor is
nonzero, and the quaternary reduction of
\cref{lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer,lem:nd-six-coefficient-dispatch} applies.
For the exceptional value \(r=-1\), the equality exit is the
following explicit two-copy construction.  Define the arity-six signature
\[
 F_r(z_1,\ldots,z_6)=
 \prod_{j=1}^3\one[z_{2j-1}\oplus z_{2j}=1]\,N_r((z_1,z_3,z_5)),
\]
where the three displayed logical bits are the first inputs of the three
paired-disequality edges.  Take two copies, leave inputs \(1,2\) of each copy
external, and join corresponding inputs \(3,4,5,6\) by four realizable equality
links.  The resulting quaternary tensor is
\hypertarget{eq:app-nd-r-minus-one-wiring}{}
\begin{equation*}
 W_r(a,b,c,d)=\sum_{u,v,w,z\in\Ftwo}
 F_r(a,b,u,v,w,z)F_r(c,d,u,v,w,z).
\end{equation*}
Direct substitution into \(N_{-1}\) gives
\[
 W_{-1}(a,b,c,d)=4\,\one[(a,b,c,d)\in\{0101,1010\}].
\]
Applying a realizable \(X\)-translation on the second external input of each
copy sends these two strings to \(0000,1111\), so the output is
\(4(\Eq_4)\) in the displayed normalization.  This terminates by
\cref{cor:higher-equality-unary,lem:common-unary-exit}.  Otherwise the
affine cross-ratio test would force
\(r+r^{-1}=-6\), impossible for a root of unity.  Hence every nonproduct
case not already terminated enters
\cref{cor:p1-eight-vertex-reduced-interface}.

\subsubsection{Laurent polynomials}
\label{subsubsec:laurent-polynomials}
\label{subsec:laurent-gauge-conventions}

The minors below are polynomials in the phase variables. Reversals and
weighted kernels can also introduce negative exponents, so we use the
following Laurent-polynomial convention. For a commutative ring \(R\),
a Laurent polynomial is a finite sum
\[
 P({\boldsymbol\lambda})=\sum_{{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathbb Z^r}c_{\mathbf{u}}{\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf{u}},
 \qquad
 {\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf{u}}:=\prod_{j=1}^r\lambda_j^{u_j},
\]
with \(c_{\mathbf{u}}\in R\) and all but finitely many \(c_{\mathbf{u}}\) zero. Equivalently,
\(P\in R[\lambda_1^{\pm1},\ldots,\lambda_r^{\pm1}]\).  Coefficient
extraction is \([{\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf{u}}]P:=c_{\mathbf{u}}\).  When a
Boolean vector is used as an exponent, its entries are lifted from
\(\{0,1\}\) to \(\mathbb Z\), so exponent arithmetic is never modulo two.
Multiplication by a monomial with sufficiently large nonnegative
exponents clears all negative exponents. The coordinate degree in
\(\lambda_j\) of a nonzero \(P\) is
\[
 \deg_{\lambda_j}P
 :=\max\{u_j:c_{\mathbf{u}}\ne0\}-\min\{u_j:c_{\mathbf{u}}\ne0\}.
\]
By convention, \(\deg_{\lambda_j}0:=-\infty\). Consequently, every finite
upper degree bound used below includes the zero Laurent polynomial.

\begin{lemma}[Grid interpolation for bounded Laurent degree]
\label{lem:nd-grid-interpolation}
Let \(m\ge3\), let \(c_1,\ldots,c_r\in\mathbb C^\times\), and let
\(P(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r)\) be a Laurent polynomial such that,
after multiplying by a monomial, its degree in each variable is at most
two.  If \(P\) vanishes on the product of cosets
\(\prod_{j=1}^r c_j\mu_m\), then \(P\) is identically zero.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
After the monomial shift, substitute \(\lambda_j=c_jt_j\).  Fix all
variables except the last one.  The result is a univariate polynomial of
degree at most two with at least three distinct roots \(t_j\in\mu_m\),
hence is zero.  Induction on the number of variables proves that every
coefficient polynomial vanishes.  The nonzero monomial used to clear the
Laurent exponents never affects a value on the stated cosets.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Polarized coefficient integration}
\label{proofsubsec:nd-polarization}

The remaining tensor has a fixed matching support and nonzero logical
coefficients.  We test their factorization by the following two-copy
construction, called \emph{polarization}.  Each phase assignment gives a realizable arity-six tensor.  Its rank-one equations give polynomial identities in the phases.
One set of minors makes the coefficient ratio constant within each of
two layers. A second minor equates the ratios between the layers.
Interpolation then gives a ratio independent of all other logical inputs,
which yields the coefficient product formula.

Let \(w:\Ftwo^s\to\mathbb C^\times\), \(s\ge3\), be the logical coefficient
tensor on the global matching, with every matching edge in its fixed
orientation.  Fix distinct logical coordinates \(i,k\) and take two equally
oriented copies.  Leave the entire \(i\)-edge of each copy external.  On the
two \(k\)-edges, join one pair of corresponding inputs by a realizable
equality link and leave the other two inputs external. After one recorded
\(X\)-dressing they form the third external matching edge and carry one
common logical bit \(\xi\).  For every \(\ell\notin\{i,k\}\), close the two
\(\ell\)-edges by one equality link and one phase-dressed equality link.
Unique monomial lifts give, after division by the nonzero representative scalar, the logical coefficient tensor of the direct six-input output
\begin{equation}
  q^{\,i,k}_{ab\xi}({\boldsymbol\lambda})
  =\sum_{\mathbf z}{\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf z} w_i(a,\xi,{\mathbf z})w_i(b,\xi,{\mathbf z}),
  \qquad
  {\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf z}=\prod_{\ell\notin\{i,k\}}\lambda_\ell^{z_\ell}.
  \label{eq:app-nd-polarized-card}
\end{equation}
Here \(w_i(a,\xi,{\mathbf z})\) means that coordinates \(i,k\) of \(w\) are
\(a,\xi\), respectively, and the other coordinates are ordered as \({\mathbf z}\).
The fixed realization scalars may replace \(\mu_m\) in each variable by a
nonzero coset \(c_\ell\mu_m\). \Cref{lem:nd-grid-interpolation} applies to these cosets.  Their Cartesian product is the \emph{polarization grid}.
Every grid coordinate is nonzero. \Cref{lem:nd-grid-interpolation} turns the
allowed phase evaluations into identities among the original coefficients.

\begin{lemma}[Polarized determinant]
\label{lem:app-nd-polarized-determinant}
Assume the global matching support has been fixed by
\cref{lem:app-nd-support-integration}, and use
\cref{lem:nd-q6-minor-localization} to retain every one-coordinate rank
equation across completed current-group factor batches.  Form every realizable two-copy output
\(q^{\,i,k}({\boldsymbol\lambda})\) in \cref{eq:app-nd-polarized-card}.  Suppose that,
after the concrete six-input case analysis has removed every violating batch,
each retained two-copy output has rank at most one in each of its three
one-logical-coordinate flattenings
\[
 a\mid(b,\xi),\qquad b\mid(a,\xi),\qquad \xi\mid(a,b).
\]
Any two-copy output violating this hypothesis is resolved before
this lemma is invoked
by \cref{lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer,lem:nd-six-coefficient-dispatch,%
lem:actual-binary-completeness}.  Then
\begin{equation}
  w({\mathbf z})=w(\zero^s)\prod_{i=1}^s\rho_i^{z_i}
  \label{eq:app-nd-coefficient-product}
\end{equation}
for nonzero scalars \(\rho_i\).  Consequently the signature is a tensor product
of binary signatures on the global perfect matching.  This is an exact
tensor identity. The individual factors are made available only through
\cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation} and are then sent through
the binary case analysis.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(i\ne k\) and \(\xi\in\Ftwo\), and abbreviate
\[
 u_{\mathbf z}:=w_i(0,\xi,{\mathbf z}),\qquad v_{\mathbf z}:=w_i(1,\xi,{\mathbf z}).
\]
Rank at most one of the \(a\mid b\) face, including a zero output, gives the
determinant polynomial
\begin{equation*}
  \Delta_\xi({\boldsymbol\lambda})
  :=
  \left(\sum_{\mathbf z}{\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf z}u_{\mathbf z}^2\right)
  \left(\sum_{\mathbf z}{\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf z}v_{\mathbf z}^2\right)
  -
  \left(\sum_{\mathbf z}{\boldsymbol\lambda}^{\mathbf z}u_{\mathbf z}v_{\mathbf z}\right)^2.
\end{equation*}
This polynomial vanishes on the full product of phase cosets.  Its coordinate
degree is at most two, so \cref{lem:nd-grid-interpolation} makes it the zero
polynomial.  If \({\mathbf z},{\mathbf z}'\) are adjacent in the remaining \((s-2)\)-cube, the
coefficient of \({\boldsymbol\lambda}^{{\mathbf z}+{\mathbf z}'}\) is
\begin{equation*}
  (u_{\mathbf z}v_{{\mathbf z}'}-u_{{\mathbf z}'}v_{\mathbf z})^2.
\end{equation*}
All values of \(w\) are nonzero. Hence
\(w_i(1,\xi,{\mathbf z})/w_i(0,\xi,{\mathbf z})\) is independent of \({\mathbf z}\) for fixed \(\xi\).

To equate the ratios between the two \(\xi\)-layers, use an
\(a\mid\xi\) face of the same arity-six output.  Rank at most one of
\(a\mid(b,\xi)\) gives the genuine face minor
\begin{equation*}
  \Gamma({\boldsymbol\lambda})
  =q^{\,i,k}_{000}({\boldsymbol\lambda})q^{\,i,k}_{101}({\boldsymbol\lambda})
   -q^{\,i,k}_{001}({\boldsymbol\lambda})q^{\,i,k}_{100}({\boldsymbol\lambda})=0.
\end{equation*}
The polynomial \(\Gamma\) vanishes on the full product of phase cosets and
has coordinate degree at most two.  Hence
\cref{lem:nd-grid-interpolation} makes it identically zero.
At \({\boldsymbol\lambda}^{2{\mathbf z}}\), only the ordered pair \(({\mathbf z},{\mathbf z})\) contributes, and its
coefficient is
\begin{equation*}
  w_i(0,0,{\mathbf z})w_i(0,1,{\mathbf z})
  \bigl(w_i(0,0,{\mathbf z})w_i(1,1,{\mathbf z})
        -w_i(1,0,{\mathbf z})w_i(0,1,{\mathbf z})\bigr).
\end{equation*}
It vanishes, so the edge ratio in coordinate \(i\) is the same for
\(\xi=0\) and \(\xi=1\).  Combining this equality with the preceding
constancy over the remaining \((s-2)\)-coordinate cube shows that
\[
 \rho_i:=\frac{w(1,{\mathbf x}_{-i})}{w(0,{\mathbf x}_{-i})}
\]
is independent of every coordinate of \({\mathbf x}_{-i}\), where \({\mathbf x}_{-i}\) is the
ordered tuple of all coordinates except \(i\), and \(w(b,{\mathbf x}_{-i})\) inserts
\(b\) in coordinate \(i\).  Repeat for each \(i\).
Starting at \(\zero^s\) and flipping the nonzero coordinates of \({\mathbf z}\) in any order
then multiplies by the corresponding constant edge ratios, giving
\cref{eq:app-nd-coefficient-product}.  The vanishing two-face minors make
the result independent of the order of the flips.
\end{proof}

Together, the two minors make every logical-coordinate ratio constant
on the coefficient cube. The interpolation uses the full phase grid
specified above.

\subsubsection{Composition of reductions and occurrence-level descent}
\label{proofsubsec:nd-completion}

The reconstruction results supply an exact factorization when all
local comparisons continue. We now record how their gadget outputs and
factor reductions compose, and how processing further occurrences
decreases the occurrence measure.

Every contraction above uses fixed representatives. The only
analytic objects are slices, pencil endpoints, inverse evaluation formulas,
and coefficient comparisons.  The two-root choices are legal for
\(m\ge3\).  Unique-lift monomial splices are cancellation-free. Phase
traces and two-copy sums may cancel and are handled by their explicit zero-output analysis or by grid interpolation.
Realizable four- or six-input outputs therefore have the direct-gadget reduction direction.  In
contrast, \cref{lem:app-nd-coefficient-line-factor,%
lem:app-nd-support-integration,lem:app-nd-polarized-determinant} use analytic
reconstruction only to prove tensor identities. Their proper factors are
added through \cref{thm:external-factor,lem:factor-saturation} with the Turing reduction supplied by the factor theorem recorded.  All bounded
outputs have size \(O(\arity(f))\).  Their
complete concrete batches are resolved by
\cref{lem:app-nd-bounded-consumer,lem:nd-six-coefficient-dispatch,%
def:relative-stratum-protocol,lem:matching-synthesis-measure}. In particular,
no nonmatching arity-six tensor is recursively re-entered.

For relative worklist invocations, let \(\mathcal W\) be the finite
multiset of unresolved irreducible occurrences in the retained factorization trees. Distinct occurrences of the same normalized signature are counted separately.  For
each unresolved occurrence \(u\), with underlying irreducible
signature \(h_u\), let \(A\) denote the current coordinate matrix and put
\(H:=\overline G(A,\cF)\).  Fix the
occurrence's own finite card/factor universe
\[
 \mathscr U^{\mathrm{fac}}(A,h_u,H)
 =Q_{u,\mathrm{done}}\sqcup Q_u
\]
when that occurrence first enters the worklist.  Let \(\Phi_u\) be its
stored factor potential.  Define
\[
 \Theta_{\mathrm{MD}}(\mathcal W)
 =
 \{\!\{
   (\arity(h_u),|Q_u|,\Phi_u):u\in\mathcal W
 \}\!\},
\]
using strict lexicographic order on each triple and its well-founded multiset
extension, with the conventions of
\cref{subsubsec:worklist-termination}.

Certifying an occurrence removes its triple.  If the selected signature
itself factors, its triple is replaced by triples having strictly smaller
first coordinate.  If processing one child-card index exposes lower-arity
occurrences while the parent remains unresolved, that index is first completed
or its factor potential strictly decreases. Hence the old parent triple is
replaced by a smaller parent triple together with child triples of smaller
arity.  This also decreases the multiset measure.  The bounded four- or six-input reductions
create no fresh unresolved same-arity occurrence: their complete factorization trees are processed inside the originating \(Q_u\).

\begin{lemma}[Relative further applications]
\label{lem:nd-relative-reentry}
Let \(h\) be an irreducible occurrence of even arity in the current
reduction data \((\cF,\DihZ{2m})\) in the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) coordinates, and suppose
\(\operatorname{RelStable}(\cF,\DihZ{2m};h)\), under the standing
assumption \(m\ge3\).  Every invocation of deck stability of a minimum-arity irreducible signature
in the preceding active-signature argument remains valid with \(h\) as the
selected occurrence and with the other local hypotheses unchanged.
In particular, this applies to
\cref{lem:app-nd-sector-ruling,lem:app-nd-endpoint-type,%
lem:app-nd-support-integration,lem:nd-six-coefficient-dispatch,%
lem:nd-q6-minor-localization,lem:app-nd-polarized-determinant}.
Specifically, every
surviving nonzero proper card is a \(\DihZ{2m}\)-matching signature, and
every lower-arity nonbinary occurrence exposed during its processing is
handled earlier in the occurrence induction.  The chosen representatives,
current group and fixed local queue are those of \(h\).  These are the
replacements for the minimum-arity argument in the preceding active-signature
proof. All its other hypotheses and reduction directions are retained.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The sector-ruling, endpoint, support, six-input, and polarization arguments
use minimum arity only to exclude a surviving proper nonmatching card or
nonbinary factor.  Under the present hypothesis each such object has
already produced a resolved leaf or strict stable successor, or has been
certified as a \(\DihZ{2m}\)-matching signature.  All other inputs---the
fixed representatives, normalized group, even arity, and tensor
irreducibility of \(h\)---are unchanged.  Thus every local construction and
reduction used in the active-signature proof remains applicable.
\end{proof}


\subsubsection{Weighted kernels and arity-four reductions}

We now return to the marked coordinates of
\cref{lem:marked-sector-dictionary}. The following support calculation
applies to both displayed marked forms. Its \(\DihXone{2m}\) case
supplies the arity-four step for the weighted argument.

\begin{lemma}[Supports of marked quaternaries]
\label{lem:marked-q4-support-atlas}
Let \(q\ne0\) be a retained quaternary occurrence in a current
\(\DihY{2m}\)-state (\(m\ge3\)) or \(\DihXone{2m}\)-state (even
\(m\ge4\)).  Suppose its complete oriented deck has been
regenerated and every member of that deck is zero or a safe current-group
binary.  Let
\(\tau=Y\) or \((X,1)\), respectively, and define the
sector-coordinate signature
\[
  q^{(\tau)}:=(U_\tau^{-1})^{\otimes4}q.
\]
After realizable sector-\(X\) dressings, for some
\(J\subseteq\{1,2,3\}\), nonzero \(a,h\), and nonzero
\(b_\ell,d_\ell\) (\(\ell\in J\)),
\begin{equation}
 q^{(\tau)}
 =a\delta_{\zero^4}+h\delta_{\one^4}
  +\sum_{\ell\in J}
   \bigl(b_\ell\delta_{{\mathbf p}_\ell}
        +d_\ell\delta_{\overline{\mathbf p}_\ell}\bigr),
 \label{eq:marked-q4-complement-form}
\end{equation}
where \({\mathbf p}_1,{\mathbf p}_2,{\mathbf p}_3\) represent the three complementary pairs of
weight-two strings.  The assertion includes all six contracted pairs, both
orientations, all zero-card patterns, and the fixed representative scalars.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For an ordered input pair with residual slices \((A,B,C,D_{11})\), the complete
card pencils, up to their nonzero representative scalars, are
\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{ccc}
\toprule
 &E(t)&O(t)\\ \midrule
 \DihY{2m}&A+tD_{11}&tB+C\\
 \DihXone{2m}&A+\delta tD_{11}&tB+\delta C,
 \quad \delta=-\eta^{-1},\\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
