The bracketed tensor products act on the six inputs by the \(L_j\)-actions.  Because \(R_+^{-1}=R_-/4\) and
\(Q_1^{-1}=-Q_1\), every local action and permutation is projectively
realizable and invertible.  Thus all three equivalences in
\cref{eq:tetrahedral-H6-equivalence} are reversible identities.
Every \(f_\sigma\), \(\sigma\in\Sigma_+\), therefore directly gives \(\Hcore\). Four copies then directly give \(\RMcore\) by
\cref{eq:app-v4-H6-R8-output}. \Cref{lem:tetrahedral-stable-HR-augmentation}
retains both cores and regenerates the deck if the group stays \(\TetInt\).
The remaining deck-stable set then satisfies the hypotheses of
\cref{thm:a4-relative}. For any retained set \(\cF\) directly realizing
\(f_\sigma\), the direct-gadget reductions point from
\(\KHolant(\cF,\Hcore,\RMcore)\) through
\(\KHolant(\cF,f_\sigma)\) to \(\KHolant(\cF)\).

After the arity-four case, let a signature \(f\) have even arity at
least six.  Rank zero is impossible and rank one factors.  In ranks two
through four, \cref{lem:platonic-high-arity-localization} either gives the
proper fixed-matching factor or directly realizes an arity-six signature satisfying \cref{eq:platonic-all-card-condition}.
The six-input card-map rank is then recomputed from its own coefficients before
\cref{lem:tetrahedral-bridge,lem:tetrahedral-orientation} is invoked. 

\subsubsection{Relative rigidity of the retained set}

\begin{theorem}[Tetrahedral relative rigidity]
\label{thm:a4-relative}
Assume
\[
 \Stable(\cF,\TetInt,f),\qquad
 \{\alpha\Hcore,\beta\RMcore\}\subseteq\cF,\qquad
 \alpha\beta\ne0.
\]
Then \(\Close_{\{\cA\}}(\cF,\TetInt,f)\).
\end{theorem}

To obtain a common affine basis, every retained occurrence \(g\) must be
classified with all twelve tetrahedral kernels.  A binary factor may have
transfer in \(\TetInt\setminus \KleinStd\), so the complete family is
\(\{\Phi_p^g(C):C\in\mathcal K_{\TetInt}^{\rm act}\}\) for each pair
\(p\).  The six-input classification supplies the two sets below. An
eight-input base and a stabilizer-code shortening argument then handle
larger arities.

Define the projective six-input matching set, \(\Hcore\)-orbit, and their union by
\begin{align*}
 \mathfrak D_6(\TetInt)
   &=\left\{\left[\prod_{\{a,b\}\in M}(U_{ab})^{(a,b)}\right]:
       M\text{ is a perfect matching of }[6],\ [U_{ab}X]\in\TetInt
     \right\},\\
 \mathscr H_6
   &=\operatorname{Orb}_{(\TetInt)^6\rtimes S_6}([\Hcore]),
 \qquad
 \mathscr U_6=\mathfrak D_6(\TetInt)\sqcup\mathscr H_6.
\end{align*}
Here \(U_{ab}\) is the binary matrix assigned to \(\{a,b\}\), ordered with \(a<b\). Define their affine cone by
\(\mathscr C_6:=\{0\}\cup\operatorname{cone}(\mathscr U_6)\).
Here the local action uses the fixed representatives of
\(\TetInt\), so every orbit equivalence is a realizable reversible input
dressing.  Their disjointness and exact cardinalities are given by
\cref{thm:cert-interface-A4-q8}.

\begin{proposition}[The tetrahedral arity-eight case]
\label{prop:a4rel-required-eight-port}
Let \(g\ne0\) be an arity-eight signature in the normalized tetrahedral
branch whose every tetrahedral pair card lies in \(\mathscr C_6\).
Then \(g\) factors across a \(2|6\) cut or \(g\in\cA\) in the normalized
basis.  Thus every irreducible eight-input remaining case is affine. Factors are
exposed only through factor closure.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a pair map \(\Phi=\Phi_p^g\), choose a kernel with nonzero image.
By a realizable tetrahedral multiplication on the deleted pair, take its
domain point to be \([Q_0]\). Dress the residual inputs and permute them to
normalize \(\Phi(Q_0)\) projectively to a three-tetrahedral-binary product
or \(\Hcore\). By \cref{eq:tetrahedral-domain}, the four domain lines
through \([Q_0]\) have opposite endpoints represented by \(Q_0\pm S\),
where \(S=Q_1\pm Q_2\pm Q_3\). Their images sum to \(2\Phi(Q_0)\).
If the two images on each line are dependent, their difference
\(2\Phi(S)\) lies in \(\Span\{\Phi(Q_0)\}\). The four directions \(S\)
span \(\Span\{Q_1,Q_2,Q_3\}\), so \(\rank\Phi=1\). Dual-basis
reconstruction then gives a tensor factor across the \(2|6\) cut. Otherwise the
exhaustive classification with arbitrary scalar coefficients
\cref{thm:cert-interface-A4-q8} says that every nonaffine parent has
a realizable card outside \(\mathscr C_6\), contrary to the hypothesis.
The remaining signatures are affine, and any factors are available by
\cref{lem:factor-saturation}.
\end{proof}

To compare the twelve cards, we describe their coefficient space by a
stabilizer encoder. Its input tensor factors are called logical qubits,
and its output factors are the physical qubits corresponding to the
original signature inputs. The next two lemmas bound the varying part by
four logical qubits. The erasure criterion then identifies an output
qubit that can be separated, allowing the shortening argument to select
a realizable tetrahedral kernel.


\subsubsection{Hilbert-space conventions}
\label{subsubsec:hilbert-conventions}
\label{subsec:stabilizer-conventions}

The stabilizer argument uses the Hermitian inner product
\(\langle {\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}}\rangle={\mathbf{u}}^\dagger{\mathbf{v}}\),
conjugate-linear in its first argument, with \(A^\dagger\) denoting
conjugate transpose. Put
\(\mathcal H_n:=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes n}\), and write
\[
 |{\mathbf x}\rangle:=|x_1\rangle\otimes\cdots\otimes|x_n\rangle,
 \qquad
 \langle {\mathbf x}|:=|{\mathbf x}\rangle^\dagger
 \quad({\mathbf x}\in\Ftwo^n).
\]
A unitary on \(\mathcal H_n\) satisfies
\(U^\dagger U=UU^\dagger=I_{\mathcal H_n}\), while an isometry
\(V:\mathcal H_k\to\mathcal H_n\) satisfies
\(V^\dagger V=I_{\mathcal H_k}\).
These Hermitian conventions are distinct from the bilinear Frobenius pairing
used for Holant contractions and from isometries of symmetric binary forms.

A density operator \(\rho\) has trace one and satisfies
\({\mathbf{u}}^\dagger\rho{\mathbf{u}}\ge0\) for every \({\mathbf{u}}\).
For a nonzero vector \(|\psi\rangle\), its pure-state density operator is
\[
 \rho_\psi=\frac{|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|}
                  {\langle\psi,\psi\rangle}.
\]
It is unchanged by nonzero scalar multiplication of \(|\psi\rangle\).
For a density operator \(\rho\) on
\(\mathcal H_A\otimes\mathcal H_B\), fix an orthonormal basis
\(\{|b_j\rangle\}_j\) of \(\mathcal H_B\).  The partial trace is specified,
for every \(|a\rangle,|a'\rangle\in\mathcal H_A\), by
\[
 \langle a|\operatorname{Tr}_B(\rho)|a'\rangle
 =\sum_j\langle a,b_j|\rho|a',b_j\rangle.
\]
The result is independent of the chosen orthonormal basis of
\(\mathcal H_B\).  The operator
\(\operatorname{Tr}_B(\rho)\) is the \emph{marginal} on \(\mathcal H_A\). A one-qubit marginal is maximally
 mixed when it is \(I_{\mathbb C^2}/2\).  Every bipartite pure state has a Schmidt
decomposition
\[
 |\psi\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^s
 \sigma_j|{\mathbf{u}}_j\rangle\otimes|{\mathbf{v}}_j\rangle,\qquad \sigma_j>0,
\]
with both displayed families orthonormal. \(s\) is the Schmidt rank
\cite[Section~2.5]{NielsenChuang2010}.  For \(n\ge1\), \(r\in[n]\), and a
one-qubit linear operator \(R:\mathbb C^2\to\mathbb C^2\), define
\[
 R_r:=I_{\mathbb C^2}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes R\otimes
      I_{\mathbb C^2}^{\otimes(n-r)}:\mathcal H_n\to\mathcal H_n.
\]

\subsubsection{Pauli and stabilizer conventions}
\label{subsubsec:pauli-stabilizer}

The \(n\)-qubit Pauli group is
\[
 \mathcal P_n^{(4)}
 :=
 \{i^c\textstyle\bigotimes_{j=1}^nX^{a_j}Z^{b_j}:
   c\in\mathbb Z_4,\ {\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{b}}\in\Ftwo^n\}.
\]
Its projectivization by the scalar subgroup
\(\{\pm I_{\mathcal H_n},\pm iI_{\mathcal H_n}\}\) is identified with
\(\mathsf P([n])=\Ftwo^n\oplus\Ftwo^n\). The label
\({\mathbf{a}}\mid {\mathbf{b}}\) is the concatenation of the two binary strings.  This label space
has the symplectic form
\[
 \omega(({\mathbf{a}}\mid {\mathbf{b}}),({\mathbf{a}}'\mid {\mathbf{b}}'))={\mathbf{a}}\cdot {\mathbf{b}}'+{\mathbf{b}}\cdot {\mathbf{a}}'.
\]
Here symplectic means alternating
\(\omega({\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{v}})=0\) and nondegenerate
\(\{{\mathbf{v}}:\omega({\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{w}})=0\text{ for all }{\mathbf{w}}\}=\{0\}\).  For a subspace \(S\),
put \(S^{\perp_\omega}:=\{{\mathbf{v}}:\omega({\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{s}})=0\text{ for every }{\mathbf{s}}\in S\}\).
\(S\) is \emph{symplectic Lagrangian} when \(S=S^{\perp_\omega}\).  If \(S\) is
Lagrangian, a \emph{Lagrangian complement} of \(S\) is a Lagrangian subspace
\(T\) satisfying \(\mathsf P([n])=S\oplus T\).

A \emph{projective Clifford} is the class, modulo scalars in \(S^1\),
of a unitary \(U\) satisfying
\(U\mathcal P_n^{(4)}U^\dagger=\mathcal P_n^{(4)}\).  Clifford conjugation induces a symplectic linear map
on Pauli labels \cite[Chapter~10]{NielsenChuang2010}.  A stabilizer code is
the common eigenspace
\[
 \{{\mathbf{v}}:P {\mathbf{v}}=\chi(P){\mathbf{v}}\text{ for every }P\in\widetilde S\}
\]
of a commuting Pauli subgroup \(\widetilde S\), where
\(\chi:\widetilde S\to\mu_4\) is an eigenvalue character agreeing with the
scalar by which every scalar Pauli acts.  A
one-dimensional stabilizer code is a stabilizer state, and a nonzero tensor
spanning it is a stabilizer tensor.
Up to nonzero scalar normalization, the nonzero affine signatures are
exactly the stabilizer states: their amplitudes have affine support and
a quadratic phase polynomial with even mixed coefficients
\cite[Theorem~5]{DehaeneDeMoor2003}. Clifford changes preserve this class.

A stabilizer isometry is an isometry whose image is a stabilizer code and
whose logical Paulis have physical Pauli representatives. Equivalently it is
a Clifford encoder with fixed stabilizer-state ancillas.  Here an
\emph{encoder} is the code isometry, a \emph{physical operator} acts on its
ambient output space, the corresponding \emph{logical operator} is the
induced operator on the encoded input space, and an \emph{ancilla} is a fixed
initialized tensor factor.  If \(S\) is the
projective stabilizer label space, the logical Pauli label space is
\(S^{\perp_\omega}/S\). For a \(2^k\)-dimensional code it has binary
dimension \(2k\).  A Clifford decoder is a Clifford unitary \(D\) satisfying
\[
 DV|\psi\rangle=|\psi\rangle\otimes|\eta\rangle
 \qquad(|\psi\rangle\in\mathcal H_k)
\]
for a fixed stabilizer ancilla \(|\eta\rangle\).  These standard
encoder--decoder and logical-Pauli facts follow from the stabilizer normal
form \cite[Chapters~3--4]{Gottesman1997}. In particular, decoding a
one-dimensional stabilizer code on \(m\) qubits sends its spanning tensor to a nonzero
multiple of \(\delta_{\zero^m}\), as used below.



\begin{lemma}[Sharp stabilizer-line bound]
\label{lem:a4rel-sharp-stabilizer-line}
Let \(u,v\) be nonproportional nonzero affine signatures of common
arity \(m\), and let \(c\ne0\).  If \(u+cv\) is also nonzero and affine, then, after applying the same analytic Clifford change to both
tensors, followed by a common nonzero scalar normalization sending
\(u\) to \(\delta_{\zero^m}\), the support of the transformed
\(v\) is either a singleton disjoint from zero or a linear subspace of
dimension at most two.  In the already normalized case
\(u=\delta_{\zero^m}\), the same support alternative holds for \(v\)
in the current coordinates, without a further Clifford change.
\end{lemma}

% Lean: StabilizerLine.normalized_sharp_support and source_sharp_stabilizer_line.
\begin{proof}
Choose a unitary Clifford \(C\) with \(Cu=\alpha\delta_{\zero^m}\),
where \(\alpha\ne0\), and apply \(\alpha^{-1}C\) to both tensors.
Relabel the resulting second tensor as \(v\).
Write \(v\) in affine normal form as
\(v=\lambda\one[{\mathbf{a}}+L]i^Q\), where \(\lambda\in\mathbb C^\times\) is the scale,
\({\mathbf{a}}+L\) is the affine support, \(Q\) is the quadratic phase polynomial, and
\(d:=\dim L\).  If
\(0\notin {\mathbf{a}}+L\), the support size \(1+2^d\) forces \(d=0\).  If
\(0\in {\mathbf{a}}+L=L\) and the zero coefficient cancels, \(2^d-1\) forces \(d=1\).
Otherwise both \(v\) and \(w:=\delta_{\zero^m}+cv\) have support \(L\).
Their quotient \(q({\mathbf{x}}):=w({\mathbf{x}})/(cv({\mathbf{x}}))\) equals one on \(L\setminus\{0\}\),
whereas \(q(0)=1+1/(cv(0))\ne1\).  If \(d\ge3\), choose independent
\({\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{b}},{\mathbf{e}}\in L\).  Since both phases are quadratic, their quotient satisfies
\[
 q(0)q({\mathbf{a}}+{\mathbf{b}})q({\mathbf{a}}+{\mathbf{e}})q({\mathbf{b}}+{\mathbf{e}})
 =q({\mathbf{a}})q({\mathbf{b}})q({\mathbf{e}})q({\mathbf{a}}+{\mathbf{b}}+{\mathbf{e}}).
\]
Every displayed argument except zero is nonzero, so the identity forces
\(q(0)=1\), a contradiction.  Hence \(d\le2\).
The support argument starts only from \(u=\delta_{\zero^m}\). It therefore
also proves the already normalized clause in the statement.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Tetrahedral card maps have at most four logical qubits]
\label{lem:a4rel-four-active-qubits}
Let
\(
 \Phi:\Mat_2(\mathbb C)\to(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes m}
\)
be linear.  Suppose that \(\Phi(C_{\rm ker})\) is zero or affine for every binary contraction
kernel \(C_{\rm ker}\) with \([C_{\rm ker}]\in\TetInt\).
Then there are an analytic Clifford operator \(C\) on the \(m\) output
qubits and a subspace \(R\le\Ftwo^m\), \(\dim R\le4\), such that
\begin{equation}
 \supp(C\Phi(C_{\rm ker}))\subseteq R
 \qquad(C_{\rm ker}\in\Mat_2(\mathbb C)).
 \label{eq:a4rel-common-active-support}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, the image of \(\Phi\) is contained in one stabilizer code
with at most four logical qubits.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If \(\Phi=0\), take \(R=\{0\}\).  Otherwise at least one tetrahedral test
point has nonzero image because those points span \(\Mat_2\).  Precompose
\(\Phi\) by a realizable tetrahedral multiplication carrying that point to
\(A:=Q_0-Q_1-Q_2-Q_3\), relabel the resulting map as \(\Phi\), and hence
assume \(\Phi(A)\ne0\).  Define
\[
 B_0:=2Q_0-A,\qquad B_j:=A+2Q_j\quad(1\le j\le3).
\]
These are the four opposite-parity tetrahedral sign points paired with
\(A\). They
form a basis and satisfy
\[
 A+B_0=2Q_0,\qquad B_j-A=2Q_j\ (1\le j\le3),\qquad
 2A=-B_0+B_1+B_2+B_3.
\]
Choose an analytic Clifford \(C\) with
\(C\Phi(A)=\alpha\delta_{\zero^m}\), where \(\alpha\ne0\).
For the following analysis put \(\Psi(C_{\rm ker}):=\alpha^{-1}C\Phi(C_{\rm ker})\).
The common scalar leaves every support unchanged.  The already normalized clause of
\cref{lem:a4rel-sharp-stabilizer-line} applies in these fixed coordinates:
\(\Psi(A)=\delta_{\zero^m}\), and the displayed identities give the required
affine sums for the \(B_j\).  Thus each nonzero
\(\Psi(B_j)\) not proportional to \(\delta_{\zero^m}\) has, off zero, either
one point or the three nonzero points of a two-dimensional
\(\Ftwo\)-subspace.  The last displayed identity makes
every point in their union occur at least twice.  If its span had dimension
at least five, total incidence at most twelve would force sizes
\((3,3,3,1)\) or \((3,3,3,3)\).  In type \((3,3,3,1)\), double incidence
leaves at most five distinct nonzero points, and any three-point support is
the nonzero part \(\{{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{y}}\}\) of a two-dimensional
\(\Ftwo\)-subspace.  Its linear dependence is
nontrivial, so those at most five points have span at most four.

In type \((3,3,3,3)\), double incidence leaves at most six points.  The only
remaining extremal case has six points, each occurring in exactly two of
the four supports.  Regard a point shared by supports \(i,j\) as an edge
between vertices \(i,j\).  The four supports form a loopless
three-regular multigraph on four vertices, and the linear dependence at each
vertex is the sum of its three incident edge labels.  The binary
vertex--edge incidence matrix has rank at least two: its rank is
\(4-c\), where the graph has \(c\le2\) connected components.  Hence the six
edge labels satisfy at least two independent linear dependencies and again span at
most four.  Thus their union spans some \(R\) with \(\dim R\le4\).  Since
the \(B_j\)'s form a basis and the common nonzero scalar leaves
supports unchanged, \cref{eq:a4rel-common-active-support} follows.
\end{proof}

For shortening, use the identification of the octahedral group
\(\OctDD\) with the projective one-qubit Clifford group in the
quaternion coordinates of \cref{eq:quaternion-basis}.  The normal subgroup
\(\TetInt\triangleleft\OctDD\) has index two.  Each of its two cosets
contains the following eight full-support representatives:
\begin{align}
 \mathcal C_+
 &=\{Q_0+\epsilon_1Q_1+\epsilon_2Q_2+\epsilon_3Q_3:
       \epsilon_j\in\{\pm1\}\},\notag\\
 \mathcal C_-
 &=\{Q_d+\epsilon Q_o:
       d\in\{0,3\},\ o\in\{1,2\},\ \epsilon\in\{\pm1\}\}.
 \label{eq:a4rel-full-support-cosets}
\end{align}
On the open set \(C_{01}C_{10}\ne0\), the coefficient cross ratio is \(\operatorname{cr}(C)=C_{00}C_{11}/(C_{01}C_{10})\).  Direct use of
\cref{eq:quaternion-basis} gives
\begin{equation}
 C_{xy}\ne0,\qquad \operatorname{cr}(C)=-1
 \qquad(C\in\mathcal C_+\cup\mathcal C_-).
 \label{eq:a4rel-full-support-cross-ratio}
\end{equation}
Indeed, in \(\mathcal C_+\) the diagonal and off-diagonal products are
\(2\) and \(-2\), while in \(\mathcal C_-\) they are \(1\) and \(-1\).
After division by one entry, every such coefficient signature and its pointwise inverse
have entries in \(\mu_4\), so both are nowhere-zero affine phase tables.

\subsubsection{Stabilizer error correction}
\label{subsubsec:stabilizer-error-correction}

A stabilizer tableau is a binary symplectic generator matrix whose rows label
Pauli generators, together with their phases. Multiplying generators is an
elementary row operation, while a Clifford coordinate change acts by a
symplectic change of columns \cite[Chapter~4]{Gottesman1997}.  A hyperbolic
pair is a pair \({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}}\) with
\(\omega({\mathbf{p}},{\mathbf{q}})=1\). Symplectic Gaussian elimination is row reduction by
changes of symplectic basis.  A nowhere-zero stabilizer quadratic phase is
\[
 \zeta({\mathbf z})=\zeta_0i^{Q({\mathbf z})},\qquad \zeta_0\in\mathbb C^\times,
\]
where \(Q:\Ftwo^k\to\mathbb Z_4\) is multilinear of degree at most two with
even mixed coefficients.  After fixed physical and logical Clifford
coordinate changes, a stabilizer isometry has amplitude normal form
\[
 V|{\mathbf z}\rangle=\zeta({\mathbf z})|{\mathbf{c}}+M^{\mathsf T}{\mathbf z}\rangle,
 \qquad
 {\mathbf{c}}\in\Ftwo^n,\quad M\in\Mat_{k\times n}(\Ftwo),\quad\rank M=k,
\]
a standard consequence of stabilizer-tableau reduction
\cite[Chapter~4]{Gottesman1997}.


Erasure of physical output qubit \(r\) of an isometry \(V\) is correctable exactly
when the single-site Knill--Laflamme condition holds:
\[
 V^\dagger R_rV\in\mathbb C I_{\mathcal H_k}
 \qquad\text{for every one-qubit operator }R.
\]
For a specified erased qubit, the error-operator products are the
matrix units \((\lvert a\rangle\langle b\rvert)_r\), \(a,b\in\{0,1\}\).
They span its operator space, giving the displayed criterion
\cite{KnillLaflamme1997}. Equivalently, one may test the Pauli basis
\(I,X,-iY,Z\). The erased qubit then carries no information about the
logical input.
In the stabilizer setting, an encoder tableau is the tableau of a Clifford
encoder. Its symplectic row reduction records physical representatives of logical Paulis and the residual stabilizer generators.

\begin{lemma}[Stabilizer erasure and column normal form]
\label{lem:stabilizer-erasure-column-normal-form}
Let
\(
 V:(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes k}\to(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes N}
\)
be a stabilizer isometry and let \(r\) be a physical output qubit.  If
erasure of \(r\) is correctable, meaning
\(V^\dagger R_rV\) is scalar for every one-qubit operator \(R\),
exactly one of the following normal forms applies after a fixed one-qubit
Clifford on \(r\):
\begin{enumerate}
\item there are a one-qubit stabilizer state \(\lvert\chi\rangle_r\) and a
      stabilizer isometry \(W\) such that
      \begin{equation}
       V\lvert\psi\rangle
       =\lvert\chi\rangle_r\otimes W\lvert\psi\rangle;
       \label{eq:stabilizer-erasure-pure}
      \end{equation}
\item there are an auxiliary qubit \(a\) and a stabilizer isometry
      \(
       W:\mathbb C^2_a\otimes(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes k}
       \to(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes(N-1)}
      \)
      such that
      \begin{equation}
       V\lvert\psi\rangle
       =2^{-1/2}\sum_{c\in\Ftwo}
        \lvert c\rangle_r W(\lvert c\rangle_a\otimes\lvert\psi\rangle).
       \label{eq:stabilizer-erasure-mixed}
      \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
If no single-site erasure is correctable, then, after postcomposing the
outputs by a tensor product of one-qubit Cliffords and precomposing the input
by one logical Clifford, there are a vector
\({\mathbf{c}}\in\Ftwo^N\), and a matrix
\(M=({\mathbf{m}}_1\ \cdots\ {\mathbf{m}}_N)\in\Ftwo^{k\times N}\) such that
\begin{equation}
 V\lvert {\mathbf z}\rangle
 =\zeta({\mathbf z})\lvert {\mathbf{c}}+M^{\mathsf T}{\mathbf z}\rangle,
 \qquad \rank M=k,\qquad {\mathbf{m}}_r\ne0\quad(1\le r\le N),
 \label{eq:stabilizer-column-normal-form}
\end{equation}
where \(\zeta({\mathbf z})=\zeta_0i^{Q({\mathbf z})}\) is a nowhere-zero stabilizer quadratic
phase in the precise sense of
\cref{subsubsec:stabilizer-error-correction}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By the Hilbert-space and error-correction conventions of
\cref{subsubsec:hilbert-conventions,subsubsec:stabilizer-error-correction},
correctability of erasure
at \(r\) is the single-site Knill--Laflamme condition
\begin{equation}
 V^\dagger P_rV=\alpha_P I
 \qquad(P\in\{I,X,-iY,Z\}).
 \label{eq:stabilizer-single-erasure-KL}
\end{equation}
Here the Hermitian Pauli matrix is \(\sigma_y=-iY\), with \(Y=ZX\).
Conjugate \(P_r\) through a Clifford encoder and then evaluate its fixed
ancillas.  The three nonidentity expectations in
\cref{eq:stabilizer-single-erasure-KL} are either all zero or exactly one is
\(\pm1\).  Thus the input-independent marginal at \(r\) is respectively
maximally mixed or a pure one-qubit stabilizer state.  In the pure case its
Schmidt rank is one, which gives \cref{eq:stabilizer-erasure-pure}.
A one-qubit Clifford sends the pure factor to a computational-basis state.
Its fixed Pauli generator can then be removed from the encoder tableau.
The remaining stabilizer generators and logical representatives are those
of \(W\), so \(W\) is a stabilizer isometry as asserted.

In the maximally mixed case a Schmidt decomposition gives
\[
 V\lvert\psi\rangle
 =2^{-1/2}\bigl(\lvert0\rangle_rW_0\lvert\psi\rangle
                 +\lvert1\rangle_rW_1\lvert\psi\rangle\bigr).
\]
Applying \cref{eq:stabilizer-single-erasure-KL} to the four matrix units on
\(r\) gives \(W_c^\dagger W_d=\delta_{cd}I\).  Hence
\(
 W(\lvert c\rangle_a\otimes\lvert\psi\rangle)=W_c\lvert\psi\rangle
\)
is an isometry.  To show that \(W\) is a stabilizer isometry, we eliminate the erased
qubit from the encoder tableau as follows.
% Lean: PauliPostselection, BellPostselection, BellCoefficients,
% MixedStabilizerResidual.mixedResidual_stabilizer.
First let \(U\) be any stabilizer isometry and let \(P\) be a Hermitian
Pauli with \(U^\dagger PU=0\).  Some Hermitian code stabilizer \(S\),
with \(SU=U\), anticommutes with \(P\).  Otherwise \(P\) would preserve
the code, and its induced unitary on the code would be zero.  Since
\((PS)^2=-I\), the operator
\[
 C_P:=2^{-1/2}(I+PS),\qquad
 C_PU=2^{-1/2}(I+P)U
\]
is a Clifford quarter turn: conjugating a Pauli that commutes with \(PS\)
leaves it fixed, and conjugating one that anticommutes with \(PS\)
multiplies it by \(PS\).  Thus the normalized positive Pauli projection
on the right preserves the stabilizer-isometry property, including the
physical representatives of logical Paulis.

Apply this observation to \(U_0=I_a\otimes V\), with output order
\((a,r,\text{remaining qubits})\), and to the commuting Hermitian Paulis
\(P_Z=Z_aZ_r\) and \(P_X=X_aX_r\).  The maximally mixed case gives
\[
 U_0^\dagger P_ZU_0=U_0^\dagger P_XU_0
 =U_0^\dagger P_ZP_XU_0=0.
\]
For \(U_1=2^{-1/2}(I+P_Z)U_0\), commutation and \(P_Z^2=I\) give
\(U_1^\dagger P_XU_1=U_0^\dagger(P_X+P_ZP_X)U_0=0\).
Consequently both normalized projections are stabilizer isometries.
Writing \(\lvert\beta\rangle=(\lvert00\rangle+\lvert11\rangle)/\sqrt2\),
their exact coefficient identity is
\[
 U_2:=\tfrac12(I+P_X)(I+P_Z)(I_a\otimes V)
      =\lvert\beta\rangle_{ar}\otimes W.
\]
Indeed, the coefficient at \(\lvert cc\rangle_{ar}\) is
\(\sum_d\langle d\rvert_rV\psi_d
 =2^{-1/2}W(\sum_d\lvert d\rangle_a\psi_d)\),
and the coefficients at \(\lvert01\rangle,\lvert10\rangle\) vanish.
A controlled addition from \(a\) to \(r\), followed by a Hadamard on
\(a\), sends \(\lvert\beta\rangle\) to \(\lvert00\rangle\).
The resulting tableau has the fixed generators \(Z_a,Z_r\).
Multiplying its other stabilizer rows by these generators removes their
entries on \(a,r\), and the same operation removes those entries from
the logical representatives.  Deleting the two fixed-zero coordinates
therefore leaves the code and logical-Pauli data of \(W\).
This proves \cref{eq:stabilizer-erasure-mixed}.  These are analytic encoder
operations. The realizable Holant kernel is selected in the shortening
argument that follows.

Suppose now that no single-site erasure is correctable.  For each \(r\),
failure of the displayed Knill--Laflamme condition gives a one-site Pauli
whose pullback through the stabilizer encoder is nonscalar.  By the logical
Pauli convention in \cref{subsubsec:pauli-stabilizer}, this
is a nontrivial logical Pauli with a physical representative supported on
\(r\) alone.  Representatives on
different inputs commute, so their logical Paulis commute.  Independent
physical one-qubit Cliffords send them to \(Z_r\), and one logical Clifford
sends the generated commuting logical subgroup to \(Z\)-type.  Consequently
each \(V\lvert {\mathbf z}\rangle\) is a simultaneous eigenvector of every physical
\(Z_r\), and hence is one computational-basis ket times a phase.  Its
\(r\)-th bit is \(c_r+{\mathbf{m}}_r\cdot {\mathbf z}\). Nontriviality of the logical Pauli gives
\({\mathbf{m}}_r\ne0\).  If \(\rank M<k\), some
\(0\ne {\mathbf d}\in\ker M^{\mathsf T}\) makes \(V\lvert {\mathbf z}\rangle\) and
\(V\lvert {\mathbf z}+{\mathbf d}\rangle\) proportional, contrary to
isometry.  Thus \(\rank M=k\), and the stabilizer amplitude normal form in
\cref{subsubsec:stabilizer-error-correction} makes the remaining
nowhere-zero phase quadratic modulo four, proving
\cref{eq:stabilizer-column-normal-form}.
\end{proof}

The next lemma applies the code structure to a signature with some
inputs left unencoded. Its physical inputs include both those untouched
inputs and the output qubits of the encoder.

\begin{lemma}[Shortening a nonaffine encoded signature by a tetrahedral contraction]
\label{lem:a4rel-physical-code-shortening}
Let \(t\ge1\), let
\(V:(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes k}\to(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes N}\)
be a stabilizer isometry with \(k\le4\) and \(N\ge k+2\), and let
\(\xi\in(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes(t+k)}\setminus\cA\) be the logical
coefficient tensor, including the \(t\) untouched inputs. Put
\(F:=(I^{\otimes t}\otimes V)\xi\).  Then \(F\) factors or one realizable
tetrahedral contraction of two physical qubits is nonzero and nonaffine.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply \cref{lem:stabilizer-erasure-column-normal-form}.  If erasure of a
physical output qubit \(r\) of the encoder is correctable, its pure-marginal case
\cref{eq:stabilizer-erasure-pure} gives a unary tensor factor.  In the
maximally-mixed case \cref{eq:stabilizer-erasure-mixed} isolates an
auxiliary qubit \(a\).  Contract \(r\) with one of the \(t\) untouched
inputs using the realizable kernel \(Q_0\), whose class lies in
\(\TetInt\).  In the analytic local
frame of \(r\), this acts on the untouched-input/\(a\) coefficient array by
an invertible one-qubit Clifford.  The residual encoder is a nonzero
stabilizer isometry, and a Clifford and its inverse preserve \(\cA\).
Consequently the shortened card is nonaffine.

Assume now that no one-site erasure is correctable.  Write the normal
form of \cref{eq:stabilizer-column-normal-form} explicitly as
\[
 \widetilde V=\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^N C_j^{-1}\right)VL,
 \qquad
 \widetilde\xi=(I^{\otimes t}\otimes L^{-1})\xi,
\]
where \(L\) is the logical Clifford and the \(C_j\)'s map normal-form
output coordinates back to original coordinates.  Then
\(\widetilde\xi\notin\cA\), since Clifford maps and their inverses
preserve \(\cA\), and
\(F=(I^{\otimes t}\otimes(\bigotimes_jC_j)\widetilde V)
\widetilde\xi\).  Let \({\mathbf{c}}\) and \(M=({\mathbf{m}}_1\ \cdots\ {\mathbf{m}}_N)\) be the
data for \(\widetilde V\).  Choose a \(k\)-column basis of
\(M\).  Since \(N\ge k+2\), choose two positions \(r,s\) outside it. Deleting
their columns leaves rank \(k\).  Choose matrix representatives \(C_r,C_s\)
of the two analytic local Clifford frames used in the normal form, with
\([C_r],[C_s]\in\OctDD\).  Normality and
index two imply that
\begin{equation*}
 \{[C_r^{\mathsf T}C_{\rm ker}C_s]:[C_{\rm ker}]\in\TetInt\}
\end{equation*}
is one of the two projective \(\TetInt\)-cosets in \(\OctDD\)
(the \(X\)-twist only relabels that coset).  By
\cref{eq:a4rel-full-support-cosets,eq:a4rel-full-support-cross-ratio}, choose
a realizable \(C_{\rm ker}\) with \([C_{\rm ker}]\in\TetInt\) whose effective contraction matrix
\(C_{\rm eff}=C_r^{\mathsf T}C_{\rm ker}C_s\) has full support and cross ratio
\(-1\).  Thus the analytic frames determine a realizable kernel \(C_{\rm ker}\) in the
original coordinates.

For a logical string \({\mathbf z}\), the two contracted bits are
\[
 \ell_r({\mathbf z})=c_r+{\mathbf{m}}_r\cdot {\mathbf z},\qquad
 \ell_s({\mathbf z})=c_s+{\mathbf{m}}_s\cdot {\mathbf z}.
\]
Each logical string \({\mathbf z}\) determines a unique physical codeword, so
the contraction multiplies its coefficient by
\[
 \omega({\mathbf z})=C_{\rm eff}(\ell_r({\mathbf z}),\ell_s({\mathbf z})).
\]
The full-support property makes \(\omega\) nowhere zero.  By
\cref{eq:a4rel-full-support-cross-ratio}, after multiplication by a common
nonzero scalar, \(\omega=i^{q({\mathbf z})}\) for a modulo-four quadratic \(q\), and
\(\omega^{-1}\) has the same property.  The undeleted columns retain rank
\(k\), so the residual map to computational-basis strings is injective.  Multiplication
by \(\omega\), its inverse, and this injective stabilizer encoding preserve
affine membership in both directions, as does the normal-form phase
\(\zeta({\mathbf z})\).  Since \(\widetilde\xi\notin\cA\), the realizable two-input
\(C_{\rm ker}\)-card is nonzero and nonaffine.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Gadget tetrahedral localization]
\label{prop:a4rel-required-localization}
Let \(g\) be an even-arity irreducible signature in a fixed-\(I\)
standard marked \(A_4\) state whose complete transfer group remains
\(\TetInt\) after factor closure.  Assume the established terminal
exits do not apply.  If \(g\notin\cA\) and \(\arity(g)\ge10\), some realizable
tetrahedral pair card of \(g\) is nonzero and nonaffine.  Iteration therefore
reaches a realized nonaffine signature on at most eight inputs, unless a factor,
terminal, or restart with a strictly larger complete transfer group occurs, using one \(g\)-vertex and
\(O(\arity(g))\) realizable binaries.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If every realizable pair card were zero or affine, reconstruction and
\cref{lem:a4rel-four-active-qubits} would place each card-map image in a
stabilizer code with \(k\le4\) logical qubits.  Frobenius reconstruction
would then write \(g=(I^{\otimes2}\otimes V)\xi\), with \(V\) encoding
those \(k\) qubits into \(N=\arity(g)-2\ge8\) sites and
\(\xi\notin\cA\).  Since \(N\ge k+2\),
\cref{lem:a4rel-physical-code-shortening} gives a factor or a nonaffine
realizable card, a contradiction.  Iterating this two-input descent reaches
arity at most eight unless a terminal, factor, or recomputed-group restart
occurs.  The direct-gadget and factor-closure directions are
\cref{eq:direct-gadget-reduction,lem:factor-saturation}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Induction over the retained signatures}
We close the relative tetrahedral argument by a finite induction on
unresolved occurrences and their remaining contractions.  On entering the argument,
consider nonzero odd-arity signatures by \cref{thm:external-odd}, unaries by
\cref{lem:common-unary-exit}, nullaries by preprocessing, and newly exposed
binaries by
\cref{lem:binary-interpolation,lem:actual-binary-completeness}.  Thus, on a
continuing branch, every unresolved nonbinary irreducible occurrence has
even arity and every binary transfer belongs to the recomputed group
\(\TetInt\).

Let \(\mathcal P\) be the finite multiset of unresolved nonbinary
irreducible occurrences.  When a token \(u\), carrying \(g_u\), is
selected, fix the finite current-coordinate universe consisting of all
iterated proper tetrahedral pair cards of \(g_u\), all realizable
dressings and permutations of inputs used by the finite reductions below, and the
complete factorization tree of every output.  This universe is finite: every
proper contraction or factor lowers arity, \(\TetInt\) and the reduction
list are finite, each arity has finitely many input choices and dressings,
and each tensor has a finite irreducible-factorization tree.  Write its remaining indices as \(Q_u\)
and let \(\varphi_u\in\mathbb N\) be the sum of their factor potentials from
\cref{eq:factor-potential}.  Define
\hypertarget{eq:platonic-occurrence-rank}{}
\begin{equation*}
 \Theta_{\rm Pl}(\mathcal P)
 =\{\!\{(\arity(g_u),|Q_u|,\varphi_u):u\in\mathcal P\}\!\},
\end{equation*}
using lexicographic order on triples and its well-founded multiset
extension.

Before applying the next induction step to \(u\), regenerate its complete
oriented current-group deck.  Process each proper card and factor, either
resolving it or entering it as a lower-arity occurrence with smaller
\(\Theta_{\rm Pl}\).  The queue \(Q_u\) then contains only
unconsumed finite reduction or certificate indices, not unresolved proper
signatures.  Before a proper card is returned, consume its unique
originating index in \(Q_u\) and attach that index, marked completed, to the
child's reduction data. It is not copied into the child's unfinished index set.
If the card factors, its finite factorization tree is processed under that same
originating index, and every intermediate split strictly lowers the stored
factor potential.

Processing \(u\) therefore replaces its triple by a finite multiset of
child triples.  Every proper child has smaller first coordinate. If a
residual copy of \(u\) remains, it has the same arity but one fewer fixed
card/factor index or smaller factor potential.  Thus every inserted
triple is strictly smaller than the removed parent triple in lexicographic
order.  Certification removes the triple altogether.  The standard
multiset extension therefore makes every same-coordinate local step strictly decrease
\(\Theta_{\rm Pl}\). A terminal, lower \(\nu\), or strict enlargement of the complete transfer group is instead an outer exit.

\begin{lemma}[The arity-four case for Platonic groups]
\label{lem:platonic-prepared-q4}
Let \(\cF\) be the current retained factor-closed fixed-\(I\)
signature set with marked group of the resulting set
\[
 G\in\{\TetInt,\OctDD,\OctTT,\OctDT,
          \OctTD,\IcoZero,\IcoOne\}.
\]
Let \(q\in\cF\) be a nonzero irreducible quaternary occurrence whose
complete oriented deck and all proper cards and factors have been processed
as above.
For each canonical pair \(p\), put
\(L_p:=\mathsf R_X\circ\Phi_p^q\).  Suppose
\[
 L_p(K_g)=0\quad\text{or}\quad [L_p(K_g)]\in G
 \qquad
 (K_g\in\mathcal K_G^{\rm act})
\]
for all six canonical pairs.  Then \(q\) has exactly the factor, generalized
equality, endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex, or outside-binary outcomes of
\cref{lem:platonic-quaternary-boundary}. In particular, no irreducible
quaternary occurrence continues in the internal tetrahedral or either
icosahedral form.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof of \cref{lem:platonic-quaternary-boundary} uses deck stability
only to supply the displayed pair conditions and to exclude an unresolved
proper card or factor.  Here the pair conditions are assumed explicitly,
and the preceding steps have already resolved or entered every such proper object at
a smaller value of \(\Theta_{\rm Pl}\). The finite preserver classification
for complementary pairs and all of its realizable terminal gadgets therefore apply.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Adding the tetrahedral core signatures]
\label{lem:tetrahedral-stable-HR-augmentation}
Assume \cref{eq:platonic-six-four-predicate} with \(G=\TetInt\), and suppose the full-rank
analysis reaches a signature \(f_\sigma\), \(\sigma\in\Sigma_+\), by reversible realizable tetrahedral dressings and a permutation of inputs.  Then nonzero scalars \(\alpha,\beta\) are recorded such
that the signature set
\[
 \cF_{\rm HR}:=
 \cF\cup\{\alpha\Hcore,\beta\RMcore\}
\]
satisfies \(\KHolant(\cF_{\rm HR})\equivT\KHolant(\cF)\).  Close
the finite core batch and consider its group.  On an unchanged
\(\TetInt\)-branch, regenerate the complete deck of
\(\alpha\Hcore\), retain every card and its factorization tree, close the retained set under irreducible factors, consider all outputs, and form the group and deck for this set.
Every branch is then an outer exit or has a retained residue
\(\cF_{\rm HR}^\star\) with
\[
 \Stable(\cF_{\rm HR}^\star,\TetInt,\alpha\Hcore),
 \qquad
 \{\alpha\Hcore,\beta\RMcore\}\subseteq\cF_{\rm HR}^\star.
\]
Only these deck-stable reduction data are passed to \cref{thm:a4-relative}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The reversible identities \cref{eq:tetrahedral-H6-equivalence} produce
\(\alpha\Hcore\), and four copies followed by the forward gadget
\cref{eq:app-v4-H6-R8-output} produce \(\beta\RMcore\).  Direct realization
and set inclusion give the displayed Turing equivalence.  First
preprocess and jointly close the two cores under irreducible factors, consider every
terminal output, and consider the complete transfer group.  A terminal, lower
minimum arity, or strict enlargement of the complete transfer group is an outer exit.
Otherwise the group remains \(\TetInt\) and the minimum nonbinary arity
remains six.

Now regenerate the complete oriented deck of \(\alpha\Hcore\).
Retain every card with its ordered kernel and scalar factor, process
its entire finite factorization tree, consider all resulting binaries and
terminals, and consider the complete transfer group.  The core \(\Hcore\) is
irreducible by \cref{proofsubsec:v4-hcore}. Its pair cards are matching
products because this holds for \(f_\sigma\) by
\cref{lem:tetrahedral-orientation} and the displayed equivalence is a realizable reversible \((\TetInt)^6\rtimes S_6\)-dressing.
\Cref{lem:platonic-matching-contraction-closure} then makes every iterated
proper realizable card zero or a matching product of safe tetrahedral binaries.  Thus
any new singular or outside binary, lower-arity nonbinary factor, or group
change is an outer exit.  On the unchanged branch, regenerate the deck
once more.  It is complete, all of its cards and factors are retained, and
every card is zero or a \(\TetInt\)-matching product of safe binaries. Hence
\cref{def:deck-stable-state} gives the asserted stable tuple.  The two
cores are certified affine signatures and are excluded from every subsequent
unresolved-occurrence set. Adding these signatures does not create a fresh
fixed-\((H,\nu)\) token.
\end{proof}



\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:a4-relative}]
Let \(\mathcal P\) contain every unresolved nonbinary irreducible
occurrence of \(\cF\). The two displayed cores are already known to be affine and are
excluded from this multiset.  Prepare a selected occurrence and induct
on \(\Theta_{\rm Pl}(\mathcal P)\).

Arity four is \cref{lem:platonic-prepared-q4}.  For the next step, assume
the complete oriented deck and all proper cards and factors have been
processed as above, and consider a six-input
occurrence \(g\).  Its quaternary cards have already been closed, so every
continuing one is a product of two safe tetrahedral binaries.  For a
canonical pair \(p\), compute \(\rho=\rank\Phi_p^g\).  Rank zero gives
\(g=0\), rank one factors, and ranks two or three are closed by
\cref{lem:platonic-deficient-rank}.  At rank four, fixed residual matchings
factor by \cref{lem:platonic-global-ruling}. Otherwise clauses~\textnormal{(i)--(iii)}
of \cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-incidence}, together with
\cref{lem:platonic-rainbow-localization,lem:tetrahedral-bridge,%
lem:tetrahedral-orientation}, give either the resolved
endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex leaf or a signature
\(f_\sigma\) with \(\sigma\in\Sigma_+\).  Its projective class lies in \(\mathscr H_6\)
by \cref{eq:tetrahedral-H6-equivalence}, and \(f_\sigma\) is affine by
\cref{lem:tetrahedral-affine}.  Because \(g\) already has six inputs, this
rainbow localization closes no common-factor inputs: \(f_\sigma\) differs
from \(g\) only by a nonzero scalar, a permutation of inputs, and reversible
realizable \((\TetInt)^6\)-dressings.  Affine membership and the
\(\mathscr H_6\)-orbit conclusion therefore pull back to \(g\).  Thus the projective class of every continuing six-input occurrence
lies in \(\mathfrak D_6(\TetInt)\cup\mathscr H_6\).

Let \(g\) have arity eight.  Prepare every tetrahedral pair card.
For a factored six-input card, process its factorization tree under the
originating \(g\)-index. A continuing result consists only of safe
tetrahedral binaries and hence has projective class in \(\mathfrak D_6(\TetInt)\).  Before an
irreducible six-input card is entered as a child occurrence, consume its
originating \(g\)-index.  Replacing the parent triple by the residual
eight-input triple with one fewer index and the six-input child triple is a
strict multiset decrease of \(\Theta_{\rm Pl}\).  The preceding six-input
base then places the child's projective class in \(\mathscr H_6\).  Therefore all pair cards
lie in \(\mathscr C_6\), and
\cref{prop:a4rel-required-eight-port} says that \(g\) factors or is affine.

Finally let \(\arity(g)\ge10\).  If \(g\notin\cA\),
\cref{prop:a4rel-required-localization} gives a terminal output, a factor, a successor with a strictly larger
complete transfer group, or a nonzero nonaffine realizable card of smaller arity.  The
first three are outer exits or decrease the current token.  In the last
case the parent card index is consumed before its factor-closed child is
entered, so \(\Theta_{\rm Pl}\) strictly decreases.  Induction makes every
child factor affine. Their tensor product would then be affine, contradicting
the nonaffine card.  Hence \(g\) is affine.

After adding signatures, consider the complete transfer group.  A strict
change is an outer exit. Otherwise prepare the next occurrence from its
regenerated current-group deck.  Every continuing step removes or
strictly lowers a triple of \(\Theta_{\rm Pl}\), so every irreducible
occurrence is eventually affine.  The disequality edge and all twelve
tetrahedral binaries are affine in this basis by
\cref{eq:tetrahedral-domain}. \(\Hcore\) is affine by
\cref{proofsubsec:v4-hcore}, and \(\RMcore\) is affine because
\(RM(1,3)\) in \cref{eq:RM-core} is a linear code. Tensor closure,
the normalization data, and
\cref{def:common-K-presentation} give \(\PresK(K\cF;\cA)\), proving the
claimed relative closure.
\end{proof}



\subsubsection{Completion of the internal tetrahedral case}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:tetrahedral-deck-rigidity}]
Varying card matchings give a realizable rainbow localization by
\cref{lem:platonic-rainbow-localization}.  The separated-sign classification and permutation split
then give either the resolved endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex leaf or a
signature \(f_\sigma\), \(\sigma\in\Sigma_+\).  Fixed matchings expose a proper
factor by \cref{lem:platonic-global-ruling,lem:factor-saturation}. Closing
its finite factorization tree gives a resolved complexity leaf, lower \(\nu\), or a successor with a strictly larger
complete transfer group, as in the octahedral proof.

In the \(\Sigma_+\) case, apply
\cref{lem:tetrahedral-stable-HR-augmentation}.  Every outer-exit branch is
already closed.  On its sole deck-stable \(\TetInt\) branch,
\cref{thm:a4-relative} gives the common affine \(K\)-presentation, and the
recorded Turing equivalence transports that conclusion back to \(\cF\).
  This proves \(\Close_{\{\cA\}}(\cF,\TetInt,h)\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{The external \texorpdfstring{\(A_4\)}{A4} form}

The external form has the same domain incidence as \(\TetInt\), but
its matching deck has no rainbow line.  Its arity-four case follows from
the conditions for complementary pairs below. At higher arity, connected domain
incidence forces all cards to share one matching and hence a proper factor.

\begin{proposition}[External tetrahedral preservers for complementary pairs]
\label{prop:external-a4-q4-preservers}
For a projective matrix set \(\mathcal S\), define its projective
coefficient set by
\[
\mathcal R_{\mathcal S}
 :=\{[c_Q(C)]:[C]\in\mathcal S\}.
\]
Coordinate matrices act on these projective sets representative-wise.
Let
\(\Theta_{\rm ext}:=c_Q\circ\theta_{\rm ext}\circ c_Q^{-1}\), and use the
Frobenius matrix \(J_{\mathrm{Frob}}=\diag(1,-1,1,-1)\) and adjoint
\(A^\sharp=J_{\mathrm{Frob}}A^{\mathsf T}J_{\mathrm{Frob}}\) from
\cref{eq:platonic-q4-adjoint}. Consider the coordinate maps \(A\) that satisfy the conditions
for both complementary pairs. For every \([{\mathbf r}]\in\mathcal R_{\TetExt}\), we require
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
  &A{\mathbf{r}}=0\quad\text{or}\quad[A{\mathbf{r}}]\in\mathcal R_{\TetExt},\\
  &A^\sharp {\mathbf{r}}=0\quad\text{or}\quad
    [A^\sharp {\mathbf{r}}]\in\mathcal R_{\TetExt}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:external-a4-two-sided-definition}
\end{equation}
This is representative-independent.  There is one zero map. Up to nonzero
scalar, the nonzero maps have rank distribution
\((144,0,0,576)\) in ranks \((1,2,3,4)\), respectively.  Under the
Frobenius-dual reconstruction
\cref{eq:platonic-frobenius-reconstruction}, every rank-one or rank-four
map reconstructs a tensor with a binary-pair factorization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The adjoint is taken for the bilinear Frobenius form, without complex
conjugation.  Since \(R_{\rm ext}^{\mathsf T}=R_{\rm ext}\), conjugation
by \(R_{\rm ext}\) is
self-adjoint, so \(\Theta_{\rm ext}^\sharp=\Theta_{\rm ext}\).  Moreover,
\[
 [R_{\rm ext}^2]=[Q_0+Q_1]\in N_{\PGL_2}(\TetInt),
 \qquad
 \Theta_{\rm ext}^2(\mathcal R_{\TetInt})
 =\mathcal R_{\TetInt}.
\]
Put \(A'=\Theta_{\rm ext}^{-1}A\Theta_{\rm ext}\).  If
\([{\mathbf{r}}]\in\mathcal R_{\TetInt}\), then
\([\Theta_{\rm ext}{\mathbf{r}}]\in\mathcal R_{\TetExt}\), so the first
condition for \(A\) implies the standard first condition for \(A'\).  Also
\[
 (A')^\sharp=\Theta_{\rm ext}A^\sharp\Theta_{\rm ext}^{-1}.
\]
Because
\(\Theta_{\rm ext}^2(\mathcal R_{\TetInt})
=\mathcal R_{\TetInt}\), one has
\[
 \Theta_{\rm ext}^{-1}(\mathcal R_{\TetInt})
 =\Theta_{\rm ext}(\mathcal R_{\TetInt})
 =\mathcal R_{\TetExt};
\]
the adjoint condition transports in the same way.
Reversing the calculation gives the converse. Thus the external
conditions for both complementary pairs correspond to the standard
tetrahedral conditions in the \(A_4\) row of
\cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-q4}\textnormal{(i)}.  Rank-one reconstruction gives a
binary-pair factorization.  In rank four, for some
\(U,V\in\GL_2(\mathbb C)\), the standard map is \(C\mapsto UCV\) or
\(C\mapsto UC^{\mathsf T}V\).  Since
\(A=\Theta_{\rm ext}A'\Theta_{\rm ext}^{-1}\), transport gives,
respectively,
\[
 C\mapsto\theta_{\rm ext}(U)C\theta_{\rm ext}(V),
 \qquad
 C\mapsto\theta_{\rm ext}(U)R_{\rm ext}^{-2}C^{\mathsf T}
 R_{\rm ext}^2\theta_{\rm ext}(V).
\]
Either display reconstructs two crossing nonsingular binaries.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[External tetrahedral deck rigidity]
\label{thm:external-a4-deck-rigidity}
Let \((\cF,\TetExt,f)\) be a deck-stable retained,
factor-closed state containing the supplied \(I\) and
\(\arity(f)=\nu(\cF)\).  Then an established terminal, a
restart with a strictly larger complete transfer group, or a proper tensor factor of \(f\) occurs.
Thus this case has no irreducible remaining case or tractable case with a common affine presentation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At arity four, use the transfer-valued pair maps
\(L_p=\mathsf R_X\circ\Phi_p^f\).  The exact
complementary-pair adjoint identity
\cref{eq:platonic-q4-map-adjoint,eq:platonic-q4-adjoint} gives the
conditions on the map and its adjoint in
\cref{eq:external-a4-two-sided-definition}.  By
\cref{prop:external-a4-q4-preservers}, rank zero makes \(f=0\), ranks one
and four factor, and ranks two and three do not occur.

For \(\arity(f)\ge6\), fix a pair and let \(\Phi\) be its card map.  The
realizable kernel set \(\mathcal K_{\TetExt}^{\rm act}\) spans
\(\Mat_2\), so rank zero is impossible and rank one
factors.  In ranks two through four, the kernel-deleted rich-line graph is
connected by
\cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-incidence}\textnormal{(iii)}.  A change of
matching along an edge would give a kernel-avoiding rich domain line with
three distinct images.  Their matchings are pairwise distinct: if two
repeated a matching, flattening across one of its edges absent from the
third would give ranks \(1,1,4\), contradicting collinearity.
Apply \cref{lem:platonic-rainbow-localization} before using the quaternary
incidence classification.  Each common factor has transfer in
\(\TetExt\), and the realizable kernels span \(\Mat_2\), so
realizable closures preserve all three images and produce a quaternary
\(\TetExt\)-rainbow line.  This contradicts
\cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-incidence}\textnormal{(i)}.
Thus all nonzero cards use one matching \(M\).

Now \(\Psi=\Phi\circ\theta_{\rm ext}\) maps the twelve standard test points
to zero or an \(M\)-matching product.
\Cref{lem:platonic-global-ruling} factors the tensor reconstructed from
\(\Psi\), and composing
its varying factor with \(\theta_{\rm ext}^{-1}\) pulls the tensor identity back to \(\Phi\).  Since \(|M|\ge2\), dual reconstruction and
\cref{lem:factor-saturation} expose a proper factor. Its binaries give the
stated terminal or restart.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The two icosahedral displays}

Recall \(\phi=(1+\sqrt5)/2\), \(\tau=\phi^{-1}=\phi-1\), and the
standard sixty-point model \(\IcoZero\) from
\cref{eq:icosahedral-domain}.  Its axes, tetrahedral sign points, and
additional even-permutation points contribute \(4,8,48\) classes.
\Cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-bridges}\textnormal{(iii)} proves directly
that these points are closed under
multiplication, inverse, and transpose.

For the opposite display, use the fixed-equality card and network correspondence
\cref{lem:icosahedral-fixed-I-dictionary}.  It replaces every standard
kernel by its realizable \(\kappa_D\)-image while leaving the retained state and
the supplied \(I\) unchanged.

Use the common rainbow normalization and separated-sign system
\cref{eq:platonic-rainbow-normalization,eq:platonic-rainbow-sum,%
eq:platonic-separated-sign-tests} with \(G=\IcoZero\).

\begin{lemma}[Icosahedral separated-sign solutions and uniform separator]
\label{lem:icosahedral-bridge}
\label{lem:icosahedral-separator}
The separated-sign system for normalized maps
\(\Phi:\Mat_2(\mathbb C)\to(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes4}\) with
\(G=\IcoZero\) has exactly six rank-deficient solutions and thirty full-rank
solutions.  For each full-rank solution \(\Phi\), the realizable kernel
\begin{equation*}
  K_{\IcoZero}=\phi Q_1-\tau Q_2+Q_3,
  \qquad [K_{\IcoZero}]\in\IcoZero
\end{equation*}
realizes the quaternary \(q_{\IcoZero}:=\Phi(K_{\IcoZero})\), whose
three flattening ranks are \((3,3,3)\), and the realizable kernel on
the canonical pair \((1,2)\)
\begin{equation*}
  L_{\IcoZero}=Q_0-\tau^2Q_1+\tau Q_3,
  \qquad [L_{\IcoZero}]\in\IcoZero
\end{equation*}
gives the nonsingular sequential contraction
\(B_{\rm out}:=\Phi_{(1,2)}^{q_{\IcoZero}}(L_{\IcoZero})\), with
\([B_{\rm out}X]\notin\IcoZero\) in every case.
For \(\IcoOne\), the corresponding realizable kernels are
\[
 K_{\IcoOne}:=\kappa_D(K_{\IcoZero}),\qquad
 L_{\IcoOne}:=\kappa_D(L_{\IcoZero}).
\]
They give the covariant quaternary and a nonsingular binary whose transfer
lies outside \(\IcoOne\), directly in the fixed-\(I\) coordinates.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The line classification, separated-sign solutions, five frames, and
all thirty full-rank cases, including arbitrary scales and cancellations,
are covered by
\cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-bridges}\textnormal{(iii)}.
The same interface supplies the realizable binary with transfer outside the complete transfer group.  Thus
\cref{eq:direct-gadget-reduction} gives the required direction.
\Cref{lem:binary-interpolation,lem:actual-binary-completeness} resolves
infinite order, while finite order contradicts completeness.
\Cref{lem:icosahedral-fixed-I-dictionary} gives the stated direct gadget certificate for the other display.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Icosahedral deck rigidity]
\label{thm:icosahedral-deck-rigidity}
If \cref{eq:platonic-six-four-predicate} holds and
\[
 G\in\{\IcoZero,\IcoOne\},
\]
then \(\Close_{\varnothing}(\cF,G,h)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For \(G=\IcoZero\), varying card matchings and connected incidence
supply a mixed line. By
\cref{thm:cert-interface-platonic-incidence}\textnormal{(i)} it is not a
mixed five-line. Its rainbow three-line and
\cref{lem:platonic-rainbow-localization,lem:icosahedral-separator}
therefore give a nonsingular binary with transfer outside the complete
transfer group. For \(G=\IcoOne\), apply the same construction through
\cref{lem:icosahedral-fixed-I-dictionary}, which preserves the binary equality.

If all nonzero cards use one matching \(M\), the twelve test points map
to zero or an \(M\)-matching product. Since \(|M|=2\),
\cref{lem:platonic-global-ruling,lem:factor-saturation} exposes a proper
factor. Close its finite factorization tree: a nonbinary factor lowers arity, a
singular or unsafe binary is terminal, and a safe binary whose
transfer class lies outside the current group strictly enlarges that group.
All-safe current-group binary
factors would contradict the irreducible branch.  Thus every branch is a
resolved complexity leaf or outer deck successor, proving the conclusion.  The separator uses only the rank recomputed on
the realizable six-input coefficients.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Completion of the Platonic classification}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:platonic-decks}]
Work at the stable tuple in the theorem.  First apply the established reductions to a nonzero odd
signature by \cref{thm:external-odd}, a unary by
\cref{lem:common-unary-exit}, a nullary factor by preprocessing, and every
new binary by
\cref{lem:binary-interpolation,lem:actual-binary-completeness}.  Thus a
continuing branch is all-even and every binary transfer lies in the
complete transfer group.

If \(G=\TetExt\), apply
\cref{thm:external-a4-deck-rigidity}.  Its factor branch is closed by
factor closure.  For every other Platonic form, including
\(\IcoOne\), use the direct coefficient interfaces already proved
above.  Hence assume
\[
 G\in\{\TetInt,\OctDD,\OctTT,\OctDT,
          \OctTD,\IcoZero,\IcoOne\}.
\]
At arity four,
\cref{lem:platonic-quaternary-boundary,%
lem:p1-platonic-eight-vertex-rank-terminal,%
cor:higher-equality-unary,lem:common-unary-exit}
and the local-reduction conclusion following
\cref{lem:platonic-finite-q4-terminal} close every output.  Suppose
\(n:=\arity(f)=\nu(\cF)\ge6\).

Fix a canonical pair \(p\) and compute \(r=\rank\Phi_p^f\) from the coefficients.  Rank zero gives \(f=0\) by
\cref{eq:platonic-frobenius-reconstruction}.  At rank one there are nonzero
tensors \(U,F_{\rm res}\) with
\[
 \Phi_p^f(C)=\operatorname{tr}(U^{\mathsf T}C)F_{\rm res},
 \qquad f=U^{(p)}\otimes F_{\rm res},
\]
so \cref{lem:factor-saturation} gives a proper factor route.  Closing that
finite factorization tree either lowers \(\nu\), reaches a binary terminal or
successor with a strictly larger complete transfer group, or expresses \(f\) entirely as safe current-group
binaries, contradicting its irreducible choice.

Let \(r\in\{2,3,4\}\).  Deck stability gives \cref{eq:platonic-all-card-condition}, so
\cref{lem:platonic-high-arity-localization} applies.  The
fixed-matching route again gives a proper factor.  Close its finite factorization tree by the same alternatives. An all-safe current-group binary product
again contradicts irreducibility.  The mixed-matching case
realizes a nonzero
six-input \(h\), using one \(f\)-vertex and exactly \((n-6)/2\) realizable
kernels, with
\[
 2\le\rank\Phi_p^h\le r
\]
and \cref{eq:platonic-all-card-condition}.  Form the signature set
\(\cF_h:=\cF\cup\{h\}\).  Direct realization and set inclusion
give
\[
 \KHolant(\cF_h)\leT\KHolant(\cF),
 \qquad
 \KHolant(\cF)\leT\KHolant(\cF_h).
\]

Preprocess and close this entire finite batch under irreducible factors, apply the established reductions to its newly
exposed outputs, and consider the complete transfer group.  If \(h\) factors, its
selected varying matchings exclude a product entirely of safe current-group
binaries, so a lower-arity nonbinary factor or an earlier terminal occurs.
If \(n>6\), a retained six-input irreducible signature lowers \(\nu\). A strict
enlargement of the complete transfer group is likewise an outer deck successor.  If \(n=6\),
the localization has no closing kernels: the contraction \(C\) in the
proof of \cref{lem:platonic-high-arity-localization} is the identity, and
\(h\) is only the recorded permutation of inputs and nonzero scalar multiple of
\(f\).  We identify it with the same occurrence and reuse its fixed
card/factor index. No fresh fixed-\((H,\nu)\) occurrence is inserted.

On the only remaining irreducible six-input branch with unchanged marked group,
regenerate the complete oriented deck of \(h\).  Retain every card
with its ordered kernel, scalar, and reduction to the preceding set. Process its complete finite factorization tree under factor closure. Consider every resulting binary and
terminal. Recompute the complete transfer group.  A lower-arity factor or group
change is an outer deck successor.  On the unchanged branch regenerate the deck
again.  Condition \cref{eq:platonic-all-card-condition} and
\cref{lem:platonic-matching-contraction-closure} make every card zero or a
matching product of safe binaries with transfers in the current group, and all card factors are retained.
Consequently \cref{def:deck-stable-state} gives the stable tuple
\[
 \Stable(\cF_h^\star,G,h),
 \qquad \arity(h)=\nu(\cF_h^\star)=6.
\]

Only for these deck-stable reduction data compute \(\rho_h:=\rank\Phi_p^h\).  If \(\rho_h\le3\), apply
\cref{lem:platonic-deficient-rank}.  If \(\rho_h=4\), apply, according to
the marked group of the resulting set,
\cref{thm:tetrahedral-deck-rigidity,thm:octahedral-deck-rigidity,%
thm:icosahedral-deck-rigidity}. The only remaining tractable case is the
internal tetrahedral one, whose theorem supplies the stated common affine
presentation for the whole retained set. The other outcomes are resolved
complexity leaves or outer deck successors.

These reductions exhaust the stable six-input residue. Each construction
records its input order and nonzero scalar, and its
reduction points to the preceding retained problem.  The finite separated-sign classifications have constant size. Localization uses \((n-6)/2\) kernels.  These bounds
and the local occurrence induction complete the proof.
\end{proof}
\section{Synthesis of the Binary-Disequality Dichotomy}
\label{sec:p1-synthesis}

The four group analyses give either a complexity conclusion for the
retained set or a construction that makes strict progress. We now combine
them using the descent measure from \cref{lem:matching-synthesis-measure}.
The resulting proof of \cref{thm:matching-deck-classification} classifies
deck-stable states. \Cref{cor:equality-accessible-closure} then uses
stabilization to classify every retained factor-closed set containing \(I\).
Finally, factor closure and the equality-edge-model equivalence give
\cref{thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy,thm:p1-deq2-dichotomy} for the original
input sets. At each step, hardness follows the reductions, while a
tractable case supplies the criterion for the whole preceding set.

Every continuing signature set \(\cF\) in this section contains \(I\),
so \(Z=K^{\otimes2}I\in K\cF\).  Hence
\cref{lem:tract-X-anchor} gives
\[
  \Tract(K\cF)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
  \ReTract(K\cF).
\]
This equivalence identifies the branchwise criterion \(\Tract\) with the
equality-accessible criterion \(\ReTract\).

\subsection{Synthesis of the Equality-Accessible Matching-Deck Classification}
\label{proofsec:matching-deck-synthesis}

\begin{lemma}[Descent in the four group cases]
\label{lem:token-coverage}
For every deck-stable tuple \((\cF,G,f)\), work in the fixed coordinate matrix \(A=I\), put
\[
 H=\overline G(I,\cF)=G(\cF),
 \qquad
 \mathscr U^{\mathrm{fac}}(I,f,H)=Q_{\rm done}\sqcup Q,
\]
and fix the displayed partition.  Then the unfinished-token batches and
the further reductions invoked by the active-signature completion proofs in
\cref{sec:normalized-dihedral,sec:proper-deck,sec:v4-deck,sec:platonic-decks}
satisfy \cref{def:frozen-token-coverage}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix the displayed partition for the deck-stable tuple \((\cF,G,f)\) in
the fixed-\(I\) coordinates. Each nonzero oriented one-copy proper card
determines a member of \(\mathscr U(I,f,H)\). All constructions with that
normalized output share a token in the one-copy queue. A continuing
one-copy step with unchanged \((H,\nu)\) therefore works on a single
unfinished token. Each genuine split in its stored tree decreases the
factor potential by \cref{lem:factor-saturation,eq:factor-potential}.
Completing the card and its tree moves the token to \(Q_{\rm done}\).

It remains to check reductions beyond the fixed one-copy deck.
In the standard marked-dihedral branch, \cref{thm:normalized-dihedral}
resolves the whole retained set at arity four through
\cref{thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy}. Its higher-arity arguments
exclude the distinguished irreducible signature: the arity-six base is
\cref{lem:nd-six-prime-base}, and localization followed by the
\hyperref[par:nd-common-factor-consequence]{common-factor consequence}
gives a proper factor at larger arity. Thus these local reductions end in
a factorization or a resolved complexity leaf.

For \(\DihY{2m}\), the open-network correspondence in
the proof of \cref{thm:marked-B-reduction} transports arity, factorization, ordered
cards, and their factorization trees to the \(\DihZ{2m}\) deck.
It therefore transports the fixed partition bijectively. For \(\DihXzero{2m}\), the
fixed-\(I\) identities \cref{eq:marked-A0-fixed-I,eq:marked-A0-edge-switch}
and the deck relabelling in the proof of
\cref{thm:marked-A0-reduction} give the same bijective transport to the
\(\DihZ{2m}\) reductions, including their fixed card and factor indices.
For \(\DihXone{2m}\), \cref{lem:marked-A-gauge-transport}
transports the weighted local comparisons used in
\cref{thm:marked-A-rigidity}. Together with the arity-four reduction
\cref{lem:marked-A-q4}, these resolve the complexity, factor the selected
occurrence, or give an outer deck successor. The factorization completes
the selected card and factor indices.

In the cyclic branch, let \(N=|G|\). The \(N=2\) boundary uses
\cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary,lem:finite-q4-dispatch}.
For \(N\ge4\), \cref{lem:proper-cyclic-q4} gives either
a realizable irreducible endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature,
resolved by \cref{thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy}, or a nonsingular
anti-diagonal factor of a two-copy output. Factor closure and
\cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness} make the latter available and resolve
its complexity or strictly enlarge the complete transfer group.
In the Reed--Muller argument, \cref{lem:rm-weight-two-integration} gives
a proper factor or a realizable \(\mathrm{Even}_4\), resolved by
\cref{lem:equality-anchor}. These cyclic multi-copy reductions therefore
give a resolved complexity leaf or an outer deck successor.

In the standard and exotic Klein-four branches, arity-four batches are
processed through \cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary,%
cor:p1-eight-vertex-reduced-interface,lem:finite-q4-dispatch}.
At higher arity, \cref{thm:standard-v4-relative} either resolves the whole
set through the real-valued dichotomy or supplies a realizable quaternary
batch. Its complete factor batch gives a resolved complexity leaf, a strict
group enlargement, or a decrease of \(\nu\).
\Cref{thm:exotic-v4-relative-rigidity} excludes the remaining exotic
higher-arity cases by proper factorization.

For the external tetrahedral form,
\cref{thm:external-a4-deck-rigidity} gives a resolved complexity leaf,
strict group enlargement, or a proper tensor factor. For the other
Platonic forms at arity at least six,
\cref{lem:platonic-high-arity-localization} gives a proper
tensor factor or a directly realized six-input signature, with its kernel
tuple, input order, and scalar. Process this output and all its factors
before continuing. A nonbinary irreducible factor of smaller arity lowers
\(\nu\), and a new transfer class triggers the complete-group comparison.
On the remaining six-input deck-stable state, recompute the card-map rank.
\Cref{lem:platonic-deficient-rank} handles rank at most three.
At rank four, the hypotheses of \cref{eq:platonic-six-four-predicate}
permit \cref{thm:tetrahedral-deck-rigidity,thm:octahedral-deck-rigidity,%
thm:icosahedral-deck-rigidity}, according to the current marked form.
In the internal tetrahedral case, the core retention and regenerated
decks in \cref{lem:tetrahedral-stable-HR-augmentation} justify the
finite occurrence induction of \cref{thm:a4-relative}, giving a common
affine presentation for the entire retained set. This is the assembly in
\cref{thm:platonic-decks}.

The multi-copy constructions above are resolved by their local
contraction, factorization, or finite-induction arguments. After those
arguments are completed, their outputs have outcomes~1--3 of
\cref{def:frozen-token-coverage}.  Any fixed-\((H,\nu)\)
proper-factor continuation arising from a one-copy reduction remains within
its originating unfinished token and satisfies outcome~4: completion
decreases \(|Q|\), while an intermediate split decreases its stored factor
potential.  The fixed-\(I\) reduction data preserve these indices under the coordinate
correspondences above. Thus outcome~4 has the required strict progress,
and all conditions of \cref{def:frozen-token-coverage} hold.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:matching-deck-classification}]
Run the procedure of
\cref{def:relative-stratum-protocol,lem:matching-synthesis-measure}
in the fixed-\(I\) coordinates, with \(A=I\).
Fix the coefficient field \(L_0\) of \cref{lem:normalization-ledger}.
Gadget evaluation and pivot-normalized factorization keep the pulled-back
data over this field. The same lemma restores the original coordinates
after any continuing normalization in \cref{sec:p1-eight-vertex}.
Consequently \cref{eq:deck-group-bound} gives the uniform bound
\(|G(\cF)|\le M_{L_0}\) at every continuing state.
Keep the finite worklist of irreducible occurrences.
For the selected minimum-arity root \(f\), put \(H=G(\cF)\) and use the canonical card and
factor indices of \(\mathscr U^{\mathrm{fac}}(I,f,H)\) from
\cref{def:relative-stratum-protocol}. While \((H,\nu)\) is unchanged,
carry its fixed partition into completed and unfinished indices.
A token is completed only after its stored card and entire factorization
tree have been processed.  At each outer deck successor, apply
\cref{thm:external-odd,lem:common-unary-exit,lem:binary-interpolation}
to any odd-arity member, unary factor, or unsafe binary.
If only unary and binary irreducible factors remain, the arity-two case
applies. Otherwise choose a minimum-arity nonbinary irreducible signature
and reapply \cref{lem:minimum-core-deck}. An interrupted token
is carried to the successor until its processing is complete.  Thus every
continuing node consists of a deck-stable all-even tuple
\((\cF,H,f)\), together with its existing queue \(Q\), where
\(H=\overline G(I,\cF)=G(\cF)\) is finite by
\cref{lem:bounded-torsion}.  Use the previously defined measures
\(\Xi(I,\cF)\) and \(\Xi_{\mathrm{full}}(I,\cF,f,Q)\) from
\cref{lem:matching-synthesis-measure,eq:deck-closure-measure}.
By \cref{lem:token-coverage}, all four group cases satisfy
\cref{def:frozen-token-coverage}. Thus
\cref{lem:matching-synthesis-measure} permits well-founded induction on
\(\Xi_{\mathrm{full}}\), including every restart.
Strict group enlargement lowers the first coordinate, a lower minimum arity
lowers the second, fixed-\((H,\nu)\) closure decreases \(|Q|\) in the third, and a
genuine factor split lowers \(\Phi_Q\) in the fourth.  A newly exposed safe binary with transfer class outside \(H\)
gives an outer deck successor. If the resulting complete transfer group
remains \(H\), the binary is processed within the current node.
An unsafe binary gives a resolved complexity leaf.  Reductions compose toward the predecessor. A tractability criterion or
common presentation passes back by
\cref{lem:downward-inheritance,lem:normalization-ledger}.
In the group cases below, \(G\) denotes the current complete transfer
group \(H=G(\cF)\).

First suppose \(\arity(f)=4\).  Apply
\cref{thm:marked-finite-group-routing}. The following list is exhaustive.
\begin{enumerate}
\item If the transfer span is proper and \(G=\CycX{2}\), use item~1
      of \cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary}.  If \(G=\CycX{N}\), with even
      \(N\ge4\), use
      \cref{lem:proper-cyclic-q4,thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy}.
\item If \(G=\DihZ{2m}\), \(m\ge3\), use item~2 of
      \cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary}.
\item If \(G=\DihY{2m}\), with \(m\ge3\), use
      \cref{thm:marked-B-reduction}.  If
      \(G=\DihXzero{2m}\), with even \(m\ge4\), use
      \cref{thm:marked-A0-reduction}.  If
      \(G=\DihXone{2m}\), with even \(m\ge4\), use
      \cref{lem:marked-A-q4,cor:p1-eight-vertex-reduced-interface}.
\item For the standard Pauli or exotic Klein group, use items~3 and~4,
      respectively, of \cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary}.
\item For \(G=\TetInt\), any of the four octahedral forms
      \(\OctDD,\OctTT,\OctDT,\OctTD\), or either icosahedral display
      \(\IcoZero,\IcoOne\), use
      \cref{lem:platonic-quaternary-boundary}. For
      \(G=\TetExt\), use
      \cref{thm:external-a4-deck-rigidity}.
\end{enumerate}
Each cited construction is applied to the retained set and its
complete oriented deck, with the factor processing specified after
\cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary}. For endpoint-nondegenerate irreducible
quaternary outputs, \cref{lem:finite-q4-dispatch} supplies the binary
case distinction. The sector matrices
\(U_{Y},P,U_{X,1}\) are used through their stated
equivalences or coefficient identities, with continuing outputs expressed
in the fixed-\(I\) coordinates.
A directly realized endpoint-nondegenerate irreducible quaternary is
added to the set and handled by \cref{thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy}.
For a possibly reducible eight-vertex output,
\cref{cor:p1-eight-vertex-reduced-interface} instead gives a complexity
conclusion or its full factor batch. The latter is processed by
\cref{lem:actual-binary-completeness} as described after
\cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary}. A directly realized generalized equality
is handled by \cref{lem:pure-ge}.
Thus every arity-four case yields a resolved complexity leaf or a
successor with smaller \(\Xi_{\mathrm{full}}\). In a tractable case,
\cref{lem:tract-X-anchor} gives \(\ReTract(K\cF)\).
The retained irreducible quaternary excludes the arity-two case, leaving
one common \(\cA\)-, \(\cP\)-, or \(\cL\)-presentation, with the class
supplied by the cited result.

Assume henceforth that \(\arity(f)\ge6\).  Apply
\cref{thm:dressed-kernel-observability}.  If the transfer span is
\(\Span\{I,X\}\), then \cref{thm:reed-muller-rigidity} applies to the
current minimum-arity deck-stable signature. Its arity-four reductions
are \cref{thm:equality-q4-boundary,lem:proper-cyclic-q4}.
It gives a resolved complexity leaf, a strict stable successor, or one
common flat-Lagrangian \(K\)-presentation. The last is an affine
tractable case.

It remains to consider the full transfer span.  Apply
\cref{thm:marked-finite-group-routing}.  Its marked list is exhaustive
because
\[
 G\triangleleft\langle G,[Z]\rangle,
 \qquad [\langle G,[Z]\rangle:G]\le2,
 \qquad \langle[X],[Z]\rangle\cong V_4.
\]
The continuing state is all-even and has minimum nonbinary arity at least
six, so it satisfies the arity and parity hypotheses of the following results:
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
marked form of the complete transfer group&relative theorem\\
\midrule
\(\DihZ{2m},\DihY{2m}\) (\(m\ge3\)),
\(\DihXzero{2m},\DihXone{2m}\) (even \(m\ge4\))&
\cref{thm:marked-dihedral}\\
standard Pauli \(\KleinStd\) and \(\KleinEx\)&
\cref{thm:v4-relative}\\
all marked Platonic forms&
\cref{thm:platonic-decks}\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Within the marked-dihedral row, the \(\DihY{2m}\) branch is a
whole-problem equality-edge-model equivalence.  The \(\DihXzero{2m}\) branch uses
the fixed-\(I\) whole-problem/open-network equivalence
\cref{eq:marked-A0-fixed-I,eq:marked-A0-open-equivalence}. Its two edge-replacement constructions use only \(I,Y\), and its final common witness is pulled back
inside \cref{thm:marked-A0-reduction}.  For \(\DihXone{2m}\),
\cref{thm:marked-A-rigidity} resolves arity four by its reduction for the whole signature set and excludes every higher-arity distinguished irreducible signature.
The sector matrix \(U_{X,1}\) expresses the weighted pencils
and fusion identities used in that proof. The realized contractions
remain in the current fixed-\(I\) coordinates.
Each relative theorem gives the alternatives specified in
\cref{def:protocol-discharged}: a resolved complexity leaf or a strict
stable successor. Group enlargement and arity descent lower \(\Xi\).
At fixed group and arity, the card and factor processing verified in
\cref{lem:token-coverage} lowers the remaining coordinates of \(\Xi_{\mathrm{full}}\).
The induction therefore handles every continuing successor.

The two transfer spans and the marked group list exhaust the possible
states. A surviving common product, affine, or local-affine presentation
satisfies \(\ReTract(K\cF)\) by
\cref{def:common-K-presentation,def:tract-X}.
The flat-Lagrangian case is contained in the affine case.
Each common presentation uses one matrix for the transformed edge and
every retained signature. It pulls back through the stated whole-problem
equivalences and \cref{lem:normalization-ledger}.
Other tractable cases already carry the criterion by
\cref{def:protocol-discharged}. The anchor equivalence at the start of this
section identifies it with \(\Tract(K\cF)\).

If \(\Tract(K\cF)\) holds, \cref{thm:predicate-algorithms,eq:K-equivalence}
gives polynomial-time evaluation. If it fails, preservation under taking
subsets and the preceding pullbacks exclude every tractable conclusion.
The induction leaves a hard problem reducing to the original retained
problem. This proves both cases of \cref{thm:matching-deck-classification}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Classification of equality-accessible signature sets]
\label{cor:equality-accessible-closure}
Let \(\cF_0\) be a retained, factor-closed finite algebraic
signature set containing the supplied equality \(I\).  If
\(\ReTract(K\cF_0)\) holds, then
\(\KHolant(\cF_0)\in\FP\). Otherwise
\(\KHolant(\cF_0)\) is \(\SharpP\)-hard.  Here
\(\ReTract(K\cF_0)\) is equivalent to \(\Tract(K\cF_0)\), since
\(Z=K^{\otimes2}I\in K\cF_0\).  The factor access used in reaching
this conclusion is a Turing reduction. Every deck is regenerated over the
current signature set and its complete transfer group.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Because \(Z=K^{\otimes2}I\in K\cF_0\),
\cref{lem:tract-X-anchor} identifies
\(\ReTract(K\cF_0)\) with \(\Tract(K\cF_0)\).  If this
criterion holds, \cref{thm:predicate-algorithms,eq:K-equivalence} gives
\(\KHolant(\cF_0)\in\FP\).

Suppose the criterion fails. Apply \cref{thm:external-odd} in
\(K\)-coordinates to any odd-arity member, and
\cref{lem:binary-interpolation,lem:common-unary-exit} to any unsafe binary.
Their tractable cases are excluded by the failed criterion.
A state whose irreducible factors all have arity at most two would
satisfy the arity-two case. Otherwise a minimum-arity nonbinary
irreducible signature exists and has even arity.

Apply \cref{lem:minimum-core-deck} to that signature.
Its lower-arity outcome is handled by strong induction on \(\nu\).
Its stable outcome satisfies the hypotheses of
\cref{thm:matching-deck-classification}, whose \(\Xi_{\mathrm{full}}\)-induction
handles all subsequent group changes and restarts.
Every continuing extension is Turing-equivalent to its predecessor and
retains the preceding set. A tractable criterion on an extension would
restrict to \(\cF_0\) by \cref{lem:downward-inheritance}, and
\cref{lem:normalization-ledger} pulls back any intervening normalization
and common presentation. This would contradict the assumed failure.
Thus the remaining outcome is hardness, which transfers along the
reductions to \(\KHolant(\cF_0)\).
\end{proof}

\subsection{From the equality-accessible dichotomy to the binary-disequality dichotomy}

\Cref{cor:equality-accessible-closure} gives polynomial-time evaluation
when \(\ReTract\) holds and hardness otherwise.  Its tractable cases
are the arity-two tensor closure and the common affine, product, or
local-affine classes. The flat-Lagrangian case lies in the affine class.
We first apply this criterion to the input set in
\cref{thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy}, then use the edge-model equivalence
to deduce \cref{thm:p1-deq2-dichotomy}.

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy}]
Apply \cref{subsec:zero-nullary}, retaining the name \(\cF\). The supplied
\(I\) survives. Since \(Z=K^{\otimes2}I\in K\cF\),
\cref{lem:tract-X-anchor} identifies \(\ReTract(K\cF)\) with
\(\Tract(K\cF)\). If this criterion holds,
\cref{thm:predicate-algorithms,eq:K-equivalence} gives
\(\KHolant(\cF)\in\FP\), with the deleted nullary scalars restored.

Suppose the criterion fails. The supplied \(I\) excludes the
common-one-sided exception. By \cref{lem:factor-saturation}, we may add
\(\Pi(\cF)\) by a Turing equivalence. A tractable presentation of the
extension would restrict to the original set by
\cref{lem:tract-X-anchor,lem:downward-inheritance}, so its criterion also
fails. Each original signature is reconstructed from its irreducible factors
by tensor product, an input permutation, and a nonzero scalar, as
described after \cref{lem:factor-saturation}.
All four cases of \(\ReTract\) in \cref{def:tract-X} are preserved by
these operations with the same common basis. Thus a tractable presentation of
\(K\Pi(\cF)\) would extend to \(K(\cF\cup\Pi(\cF))\), a contradiction.
The reconstruction and \cref{eq:factor-saturation} give
\(\KHolant(\Pi(\cF))\equivT\KHolant(\cF)\), so we may replace the
initial set by \(\Pi(\cF)\) with the criterion still false. Retain the name \(\cF\) for this irreducible set.
This finite set is factor-closed, and the irreducible binary equality
\(I\) remains present.

The retained set now satisfies \cref{cor:equality-accessible-closure},
which gives hardness because the criterion fails. The factor-closure
equivalence transfers it to the original input problem. For the original
input set, decidability follows directly from
\cref{thm:predicate-algorithms,lem:tract-X-anchor}, before factor replacement.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{thm:p1-deq2-dichotomy}]
