Apply \cref{subsec:zero-nullary}, retaining the name \(\cF\).
By \cref{lem:p1-equality-wire-equivalence},
\[
 \Holant(\cF,X)\equivT\KHolant(\cF\cup\{I\}).
\]
Apply \cref{thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy} directly to
\(\cF\cup\{I\}\). Its criterion is
\(\ReTract(K(\cF\cup\{I\}))\), which is equivalent to
\(\ReTract(\cF\cup\{X\})\) by the
\hyperlink{eq:retract-edge-bridge}{criterion correspondence following
\cref{lem:p1-equality-wire-equivalence}}. Both complexity conclusions transfer
through the displayed equivalence, and the criterion is decidable by
\cref{thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy}. The preprocessing convention gives
the same criterion for the original input set. The edge replacements
are linear-size, and the deleted nullary scalars are restored exactly.
\end{proof}

\section{Conclusion}
\label{sec:p1-conclusion}

The supplied binary disequality signature yields a decidable
\(\FP\)-versus-\(\SharpP\)-hard classification for every fixed finite
set of algebraic complex-valued Boolean signatures.
\Cref{thm:p1-deq2-dichotomy,thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy} express this
classification using, respectively, ordinary edges with an \(X\)-vertex
and \(X\)-edges with an \(I\)-vertex.  The binary anchor leaves
four tractable cases: the arity-two tensor closure and common
affine, product, or local-affine transformability.

The proof combines arity reduction with the complete transfer group.
A new contraction or factor can lower the minimum nonbinary arity or
enlarge that group, changing the applicable structural analysis.
The progress mechanism in \cref{lem:matching-synthesis-measure} controls
both changes and completes the finite card and factor processing between
them. It thereby assembles the four group analyses into a classification
of the entire signature set.

The eight-vertex reductions provide the common arity-four step.
\Cref{thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy} extends the single-signature
classification of Cai and Fu
\cite[arXiv version~2, Theorem~3.1]{CaiFu2023}
to a signature set containing an endpoint-nondegenerate irreducible
quaternary, with the same reduced predicate \(\ReTract\) and without
requiring binary equality. The finite classifications in Appendix~\ref{sec:exact-finite-certificates}
supply the stated low-arity calculations used by the structural branches.

\section*{Code Availability}
The accompanying
\href{https://github.com/liuchliuch/holant-binary-disequality-lean/tree/ec3e598e380c57e1c4d1a3c73a4ad1b4f7eed711}%
{\textcolor{repositorylink}{\GitHubIcon Lean formalization}}
(fixed source revision \texttt{ec3e598}) gives the public statements in
\path{Holant/Statements.lean} and their proofs in \path{Holant/Proofs.lean}.
In the \path{Holant.Theorems} namespace, \path{binaryDisequality} proves the
complexity dichotomy in \cref{thm:p1-deq2-dichotomy},
\path{quaternary} the one in \cref{thm:p1-quaternary-kholant-dichotomy},
and \path{literalEquality} the one in \cref{thm:p1-eq2-kholant-dichotomy}.
For each of these names, \path{Holant/Decidability.lean} exports the
corresponding exact-input recognition theorem with suffix \texttt{\_uniform}.
Supplementary lemmas and exact finite
certificates are included through \path{Holant/Library.lean}.
The statement-only reference \path{Verification/Challenge.lean} and
\path{Verification/comparator.json} support independent comparison using
Lean's Comparator.  Build and verification instructions are given in the
repository README.

The original Python companion repository is
\href{https://github.com/liuchliuch/Holant-X-Dichotomy}%
{\textcolor{repositorylink}{\GitHubIcon\textit{Holant-X-Dichotomy}}}.
References below to \path{certificates/} and its Python scripts refer to
the separately distributed source and instructions in that repository.
The Lean release supplies its own exact certificates under
\path{Holant/Certificates/} and does not require the Python companion.

\clearpage
\appendix
\renewcommand{\thesection}{F.\Alph{section}}
\renewcommand{\theHsection}{fullappendix.\Alph{section}}
\section{Finite Classifications and Explicit Contractions}
\label{sec:exact-finite-certificates}

The finite classifications below supply specific inputs to the group
analyses. The main text verifies their hypotheses, realizes the required
contractions, and applies the resulting reductions to the retained signature set.
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}l>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X@{}}
\toprule
Results & Input to the main proof & Main use\\
\midrule
\cref{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-normal-form,thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-gadget-exits,thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-odd}
& Affine endpoint normal forms and contractions of quaternary signatures with full odd-parity support and fourth-root phases
& \cref{prop:p1-affine-eight-vertex-certificate,cor:p1-affine-eight-vertex-operational}\\

& (i) \cref{lem:octahedral-separator}, (ii) \cref{lem:tetrahedral-bridge},
  (iii) \cref{lem:icosahedral-separator}\\

& Arity-four ranks, reconstructions, and realizable outputs
& (i) \cref{lem:platonic-quaternary-boundary},

\Cref{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-gadget-exits} is also used in
\cref{sec:normalized-dihedral}.

Each calculation states its parameter space, tests, and exact
characteristic-zero field. Where completeness depends on exhaustive
computation, the proof identifies that dependency and the relevant script
in \path{certificates/} of the
\href{https://github.com/liuchliuch/Holant-X-Dichotomy}{Python companion repository}.
These programs emit assertions rather than independent proof objects.
For results proved structurally, the scripts provide supplementary checks.
The Lean repository has its own finite certificates, as described in Code Availability.

In a histogram, \(s^m\) means \(m\) objects of size \(s\).
Projective representatives are normalized by their first nonzero coordinate.
We use the conventions in
\cref{subsubsec:group-projective,subsubsec:boolean-affine-tools}
and Appendix~\ref{app:exact-algebraic-tools}, together with
\hyperref[subsubsec:projective-tensor-geometry]{the projective tensor conventions}
and \hyperref[subsubsec:symmetric-binary-spaces]{the symmetric-space conventions}.

\subsection{Endpoint-Nondegenerate Eight-Vertex Signatures}
\label{appsec:cert-eight-vertex}

We first classify the loop and two-copy contractions of quaternary
signatures with full odd-parity support whose nonzero entries, after
normalization, are fourth roots of unity. We then determine the affine
endpoint normal forms.
\Cref{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-normal-form,thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-gadget-exits,thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-odd}
state the consequences used in \cref{sec:p1-eight-vertex}.

\subsubsection{Fourth-root phases on full odd-parity support}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-mu4}

In this subsubsection, label the four inputs by \(0,1,2,3\),
with local label \(j\) corresponding to input \(j+1\) in the main text.
Here \({\mathbf{e}}_j\) denotes the string with a single 1 at the locally labelled input.
Normalize a quaternary signature with full odd-parity support by
\(a_j=f({\mathbf{e}}_j)\), \(b_j=f(\one-{\mathbf{e}}_j)\), and \(a_0=1\).  Its fourth-root
phase tuple is
\begin{equation}
  a_j=i^{\alpha_j},\qquad b_j=i^{\beta_j},
  \qquad
  (\alpha_1,\alpha_2,\alpha_3,\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_3)
  \in\mathbb Z_4^7.
  \label{eq:cert-mu4-state}
\end{equation}
There are \(4^7=16\,384\) phase tuples. Write \(N(z)=z\bar z\) for the Gaussian
norm.  For \(i<j\), with \(k<l\) the complementary pair, the six disequality self-loops and six equality-mediated loops are
\begin{equation}
 L^X_{ij}=\diag(a_i+a_j,b_i+b_j),
 \qquad
 L^I_{ij}=
 \begin{pmatrix}0&a_l+b_k\\a_k+b_l&0\end{pmatrix}.
  \label{eq:cert-twelve-loops}
\end{equation}
The latter inserts one equality vertex using two \(X\)-edges.  A
series equality support-flips the former:
\begin{equation}
 \diag(P,Q)XI=\begin{pmatrix}0&P\\Q&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 T_{\diag(P,Q)XI}=\diag(P,Q).
  \label{eq:cert-diagonal-loop-support-flip}
\end{equation}
In either construction, when \(P,Q\ne0\), the resulting transfer has
eigenvalue ratio \(P/Q\).

\begin{proposition}[Deterministic twelve-loop test]
\label{prop:cert-twelve-loop}
For each phase tuple in \cref{eq:cert-mu4-state}, inspect the twelve ordered pairs
in \cref{eq:cert-twelve-loops}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
  \item If exactly one entry of some pair is zero, declare a rank-one
  exit.
  \item Otherwise, for every pair with two nonzero coordinates \(P,Q\),
  compare their Gaussian norms.  If \(N(P)\neq N(Q)\), declare an
  infinite-projective-order exit.
  \item Otherwise retain the phase tuple and all zero--zero pairs.
\end{enumerate}
The resulting disjoint partition is
\begin{equation}
 16\,384=15\,792+224+368,
 \label{eq:cert-mu4-histogram}
\end{equation}
where the summands are respectively rank-one, infinite-order, and remaining
phase tuples.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The entries lie in \(\mathbb Z[i]\).  Up to a fourth root of unity, every
nonzero sum of two fourth roots is either \(2\) or \(1+i\).
Consequently two such sums have the same Gaussian norm precisely when their
quotient lies in \(\mu_4\).  If their norms differ, the
quotient has modulus different from one and therefore cannot have finite
multiplicative order.  Thus, for exactly the nonzero sums occurring here,
\(P/Q\) is torsion if and only if \(N(P)=N(Q)\).  Hence
\cref{eq:cert-diagonal-loop-support-flip} makes the three tests exact and
disjoint. Exhaustive evaluation gives \cref{eq:cert-mu4-histogram}.
\end{proof}

Exactly 64 remaining phase tuples satisfy
\begin{equation}
 b_j=\eps a_j\quad(0\leq j\leq3),\qquad
 \eps\in\{1,-1\},\qquad \prod_{j=0}^3a_j\in\{i,-i\}.
  \label{eq:cert-mu4-exceptional64}
\end{equation}
Indeed, after the normalization \(a_0=1\), exactly half of the \(4^3\)
choices of \((a_1,a_2,a_3)\) have product in \(\{i,-i\}\), and \(\eps\)
has two choices. Hence the count is \(32\cdot2=64\).
Join inputs \(0,1\) of two copies \(A,B\) by \(X\)-edges.  Denote the
resulting quaternary signature by \(Q\).  In the order
\((A_2,A_3,B_2,B_3)\), its coefficients are
\[
\begin{array}{ccccccc}
\toprule
{\mathbf{x}}&0000&1111&0011,1100&0101&1010&0110,1001\\ \midrule
Q({\mathbf{x}})&2a_0a_1&2b_0b_1&a_0b_0+a_1b_1&2a_3b_2&2a_2b_3&
a_2b_2+a_3b_3.\\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
\]
The contraction of two odd-parity signatures along two \(X\)-edges has even
external parity, and the two endpoint values \(2a_0a_1\) and \(2b_0b_1\)
are nonzero.  Under \cref{eq:cert-mu4-exceptional64}, exactly one of
\(a_0^2+a_1^2,a_2^2+a_3^2\) vanishes: indeed, each \(a_j^2\) is a sign and
\(\prod_j a_j^2=-1\), so exactly one of the two displayed pairs consists of
opposite signs.  Hence \(Q\) is a support-six eight-vertex signature.

For each other remaining phase tuple and \(\delta\in\mathbb Z_4\), define the
phase-shifted signature \(f_\delta\) by
\[
 f_\delta({\mathbf{e}}_j)=i^{\alpha_j},
 \qquad
 f_\delta(\one-{\mathbf{e}}_j)=i^{\beta_j+\delta}
\]
and keep \(f_\delta({\mathbf{x}})=0\) on every even-weight input.  Parameterize the odd
coset by
\[
 {\mathbf{x}}({\mathbf{y}})=(y_0,y_1,y_2,1\oplus y_0\oplus y_1\oplus y_2),
 \qquad {\mathbf{y}}\in\Ftwo^3.
\]
Define the phase-exponent function
\(\varphi_\delta:\Ftwo^3\to\mathbb Z_4\) by
\(f_\delta({\mathbf{x}}({\mathbf{y}}))/f_\delta({\mathbf{x}}(000))=i^{\varphi_\delta({\mathbf{y}})}\), and define its
linear coefficients \(\ell_j\) and mixed-quadratic coefficients \(q_{jk}\)
by
\[
 \ell_j=\varphi_\delta({\mathbf{e}}_j),\qquad
 q_{jk}=\varphi_\delta({\mathbf{e}}_j+{\mathbf{e}}_k)-\ell_j-\ell_k.
\]
Then \(f_\delta\) is affine if and only if
\begin{equation}
 q_{jk}\in\{0,2\}\ (j<k),\qquad
 \varphi_\delta(111)
 =\ell_0+\ell_1+\ell_2+q_{01}+q_{02}+q_{12}\pmod 4.
  \label{eq:cert-mu4-affine-test}
\end{equation}
Indeed, in the Boolean M\"obius convention of
\cref{subsubsec:boolean-affine-tools}, the expansion of
\(\varphi_\delta\) over \(\mathbb Z_4\) has the three
\(q_{jk}\) as its quadratic coefficients, while the difference between the
two sides of the second congruence is its cubic coefficient.  A modulo-four
phase is affine exactly when the quadratic coefficients are even
and that cubic coefficient vanishes.
Exactly two \(\delta\)'s pass: \(\{0,2\}\) for 176 phase tuples and
\(\{1,3\}\) for 128 phase tuples.

The 512 normalized affine signatures with full odd-parity support are given by
\begin{equation}
 (a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3)=(1,A,B,C),\qquad
 (b_0,b_1,b_2,b_3)=(xyzABC,zBC,yAC,xAB),
 \label{eq:cert-affine-odd-param}
\end{equation}
where \(A,B,C\in\mu_4\) and \(x,y,z\in\{1,-1\}\). The \(4^3\cdot2^3\)
choices give 512 distinct normalized phase tuples.  The locus having no rank-one
\(X\)-loop is the disjoint union
\begin{align}
 &x=y=z &&(128\text{ phase tuples}),\label{eq:cert-affine-odd-rank-a}\\
 &x,y,z\text{ not all equal},\quad
   (A^2,B^2,C^2)=(xy,xz,yz)
 &&(48\text{ phase tuples}).\label{eq:cert-affine-odd-rank-b}
\end{align}
The other 336 phase tuples have a rank-one loop.  For the remaining 176, let
\(G_{0j}\) join inputs \(0,j\) of two copies by corresponding \(X\)-edges and retain the other inputs in copy order.  Call a resulting
quaternary \emph{dense product-transformable} when it has the full even-parity form
in \cref{eq:p1-eight-vertex-product-dense-form}, namely
\[
 g({\mathbf{x}})=\rho r^{\wt({\mathbf{x}})}(-1)^{{\mathbf{s}}\cdot {\mathbf{x}}}
       \one[\wt({\mathbf{x}})\text{ is even}]
 \quad\text{for some }\rho,r\ne0,\ {\mathbf{s}}\in\Ftwo^4.
\]
Then
\begin{align}
G_{01}\text{ is dense product-transformable}
  &\Longleftrightarrow {\mathbf{x}}={\mathbf{y}}\text{ and }B^2=C^2,\notag\\
G_{02}\text{ is dense product-transformable}
  &\Longleftrightarrow {\mathbf{x}}=z\text{ and }A^2=C^2,\notag\\
G_{03}\text{ is dense product-transformable}
  &\Longleftrightarrow {\mathbf{y}}=z\text{ and }A^2=B^2.
\label{eq:cert-affine-odd-cover}
\end{align}
In \cref{eq:cert-affine-odd-rank-a} two squares agree. In
\cref{eq:cert-affine-odd-rank-b} the unique equal pair of \(x,y,z\) has
the corresponding equal squares.  Hence one test in
\cref{eq:cert-affine-odd-cover} always succeeds.  These formulas are a
self-contained mathematical certificate.

\subsubsection{Affine endpoint normal forms}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-affine-q4-endpoint}

The affine classification used in \cref{sec:p1-eight-vertex} reduces to
eighteen coefficient transforms and three endpoint-support types.  For a nonzero affine signature with support \({\mathbf{s}}+L\),
choose the lexicographically first string in the support as origin and the
lexicographically first spanning basis of \(L\) as intrinsic coordinates,
and divide by the value at that origin.  Define the eighteen representative matrices
\begin{equation}
\begin{split}
\mathscr K_{\mathrm{aff}}
={}&\{I,X\}\\
&{}\cup
\left\{
 \begin{pmatrix}1&r\\1&-r\end{pmatrix}:r\in\mu_8
\right\}\\
&{}\cup
\left\{
 \begin{pmatrix}
 1&u(1+\varepsilon\sqrt2)\\
 1&u(1-\varepsilon\sqrt2)
 \end{pmatrix}:
 u\in\mu_4,\ \varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}
\right\}.
\end{split}
\label{eq:cert-affine-q4-cores}
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}[Exact affine endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex certificate]
\label{prop:cert-affine-q4-endpoint}
For every canonically projectively normalized nonzero arity-four affine signature
\(f\in\cA\) and every \(K_0\in\mathscr K_{\mathrm{aff}}\), define the
transformed signature \(q\) by
\[
  q:=K_0^{\otimes4}f.
\]
Retain \((K_0,f)\) precisely when
\begin{equation}
  q({\mathbf{x}})=0\quad\text{for odd }\wt({\mathbf{x}}),
  \qquad
  q(\zero^4)q(\one^4)\ne0.
  \label{eq:cert-affine-q4-retention}
\end{equation}
Then the following assertions hold.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item Writing each affine support as \({\mathbf{s}}+L\), there are \(36\,720\)
normalized projective affine signatures:
\[
\begin{array}{cccccc}
\toprule
\dim L&0&1&2&3&4\\ \midrule
\#\text{ affine supports}&16&120&140&30&1\\
\#\text{ normalized signatures}&16&480&4480&15360&16384.\\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
\]
Among the \(36\,720\cdot18\) pairs \((K_0,f)\), exactly \(4200\)
satisfy \cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-retention}.  Six representative matrices retain \(612\)
inputs each and the other twelve retain \(44\) inputs each.

\item Every retained \(q\) has the form
\begin{equation}
  q=\gamma\,D(t)^{\otimes4}h,
  \qquad
  \gamma,t\in\AlgNums^\times,
  \label{eq:cert-affine-q4-diagonal-form}
\end{equation}
where \(h\in\cA\), \(h(\zero^4)=1\), and
\[
 \supp(h)\in
 \left\{
  \{\zero^4,\one^4\},\
  \{\zero^4,\one^4,\one_I,\one_{I^c}\},\
  \{{\mathbf{x}}:\wt({\mathbf{x}})\equiv0\pmod2\}
 \right\},
\]
with \(|I|=2\).  Let \(\mathscr R_{\mathrm{aff}}\) be the finite set of all
such normalized affine tensors \(h\).  Their support-size histogram is
\[
  2^4,\qquad4^{96},\qquad8^{512}.
\]
The three support cases form, respectively, \(1,24,256\) diagonal orbits.
Here
\[
 g\sim_{\mathrm{diag}}g'
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 g'=\gamma\,D(t)^{\otimes4}g
 \quad\text{for some }\gamma,t\in\AlgNums^\times.
\]

More explicitly, fix the lexicographic order on the six weight-two strings.
If \(v_1,\ldots,v_6\) are the corresponding central coefficients of an
endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature \(g\), define its
diagonal-orbit invariant vector by
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal J(g):=
 \left(
 \frac{v_rv_s}{g(\zero^4)g(\one^4)}
 \right)_{1\le r\le s\le6}
 \label{eq:cert-affine-q4-invariant}
\end{equation}
Then \(\mathcal J(g)\) is a complete invariant of these diagonal orbits.

\item For a support-four reference, let \(I\sqcup I^c=[4]\) be its two
pairs of inputs and write its logical coefficient matrix as
\[
 F=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\ C&D\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad ABCD\ne0.
\]
Here the row bit is the common value on the \(I\)-block, the column bit is
the common value on the \(I^c\)-block, and
\[
 A=h(\zero^4),\qquad B=h(\one_{I^c}),\qquad
 C=h(\one_I),\qquad D=h(\one^4).
\]
Then \(AD/BC\in\{1,-1\}\).  If \(AD=BC\), the signature is a tensor
product of two nonsingular weighted equalities.  If \(AD=-BC\), take two
labelled copies, join each of the two inputs in the \(I^c\)-block
of the first copy to the corresponding input of the second copy by a \(X\)-edge, and retain the two \(I\)-blocks as the four external inputs.
The resulting logical coefficient matrix is
\begin{equation}
 FXF^{\mathsf T}
 =\begin{pmatrix}
 2AB&AD+BC\\
 AD+BC&2CD
 \end{pmatrix}
 =\begin{pmatrix}2AB&0\\0&2CD\end{pmatrix},
 \label{eq:cert-affine-q4-support-four-gadget}
\end{equation}
and hence the output signature is a nonzero generalized equality on
its four external inputs.

\item The \(512\) reference signatures with full even-parity support have the disjoint first-witness
partition
\begin{equation}
  512=16+336+96+48+16.
  \label{eq:cert-affine-q4-full-partition}
\end{equation}
The first \(16\) have the dense product-transformable form of
\cref{eq:p1-eight-vertex-product-dense-form}.  The next \(336\) have a
rank-one \(X\)-self-loop.  For this construction, label the four inputs by \(0,1,2,3\)
(local label \(j\) corresponds to input \(j+1\) in the main text).
For each remaining reference signature, take two
labelled copies and, successively for \(j=1,2,3\), join inputs \(0,j\) of
the first copy by crossed \(X\)-edges to inputs \(j,0\) of the
second.  Order the external inputs as the uncontracted inputs of the first
copy in increasing order, followed by the corresponding inputs of the second
copy.  The first support-four product occurs for, respectively,
\(96,48,16\) new reference signatures.  It has the form
\[
  cD(r)\otimes D(r),
  \qquad c\in\mathbb C^\times,
  \qquad r\in\mu_4,
\]
with respect to the induced perfect matching and displayed external-input order.  Here \(c\) is a nonzero scalar and \(D(r)\) is the diagonal binary
matrix.  The histogram in the order \(r=1,-1,i,-i\) is
\[
  (56,56,24,24).
\]

\item Every normalized fourth-root signature \(p\) with full odd-parity support, as in
\cref{eq:cert-mu4-state}, is classified as follows.  The twelve loops in \cref{eq:cert-twelve-loops} give the partition
\[
  16\,384=15\,792+224+368
\]
of \cref{prop:cert-twelve-loop}.  Among the \(368\) remaining phase tuples,
exactly \(64\) satisfy \cref{eq:cert-mu4-exceptional64}. The displayed
two-copy gadget there is a support-six endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature.

For each of the other \(304\) phase tuples, let \(p_\delta\) multiply the four
weight-three values of \(p\) by \(i^\delta\) while leaving its weight-one
values fixed.  Exactly two \(\delta\in\mathbb Z_4\) make \(p_\delta\)
affine.  The valid sets are
\(\{0,2\}\) for \(176\) phase tuples and \(\{1,3\}\) for \(128\) phase tuples.
Every such \(p_\delta\) lies in the \(512\)-member family
\cref{eq:cert-affine-odd-param}.  Of that family, \(336\) phase tuples have a
rank-one \(X\)-loop. Every one of the remaining \(176\) phase tuples has
at least one two-copy output of the dense product-transformable form among
the three gadgets in
\cref{eq:cert-affine-odd-cover}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a support \({\mathbf{s}}+L\), put \(d=\dim L\).  In the canonical intrinsic
coordinates \({\mathbf{x}}=(x_1,\ldots,x_d)\), the normalized phases are exactly
\[
 i^{\sum_{j=1}^d\ell_jx_j+
      2\sum_{1\le j<k\le d}q_{jk}x_jx_k},
 \qquad
 \ell_j\in\mathbb Z_4,\qquad q_{jk}\in\Ftwo.
\]
There are
\[
 2^{4-d}\genfrac[]{0pt}{}{4}{d}_2\,4^d2^{\binom d2}
\]
such signatures of support dimension \(d\): the Gaussian binomial counts
the linear spaces, where
\(\genfrac[]{0pt}{}{4}{d}_2=\prod_{j=0}^{d-1}(2^4-2^j)/(2^d-2^j)\) and the empty
product is one.  The factor \(2^{4-d}\) counts their cosets, and the last two factors the
linear and mixed phase coefficients.  This proves the first table in
\emph{(i)}.  Applying the eighteen matrices in
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-cores} and testing
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-retention} gives the \(4200\) retained pairs.

For completeness of the orbit test, normalize the first endpoint to one,
write the central layer as \({\mathbf{v}}\), and write the second endpoint as \(e\).
If \({\mathbf{v}}=0\), two signatures are diagonally equivalent after choosing
\(t^4=e'/e\).  Otherwise equality of
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-invariant}, using one nonzero coordinate of \({\mathbf{v}}\),
gives
\[
  {\mathbf{v}}'=\lambda {\mathbf{v}},
  \qquad e'=\lambda^2e.
\]
Choosing \(t^2=\lambda\) proves
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-diagonal-form}.  The support and orbit histograms
then follow from the exhaustive retained list.

On a support-four affine plane, choose the logical coordinates \(u,v\) used
in the displayed table.  After projective normalization its phase is
\[
 h(u,v)=i^{\ell_1u+\ell_2v+2quv},
 \qquad \ell_1,\ell_2\in\mathbb Z_4,\quad q\in\Ftwo.
\]
Therefore \(AD/(BC)=(-1)^q\in\{1,-1\}\), proving the asserted cross-ratio.
The gadget construction in \cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-support-four-gadget} is then a
direct coefficient identity.  For full even-parity support, the exact replay tests the six disequality self-loops and the three crossed two-copy contractions in the stated
first-witness order. This gives
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-full-partition} and the \(r\)-histogram.

Finally it evaluates all \(4^7\) phase tuples of
\cref{eq:cert-mu4-state}, the twelve ordered pairs in
\cref{eq:cert-twelve-loops}, the exceptional two-copy output, all four
tests in \cref{eq:cert-mu4-affine-test}, and the three gadgets
\(G_{0j}\).  This gives the last assertion.

The verification script \path{certificates/verify_affine_q4.py}
regenerates all the finite objects above from their definitions and
evaluates the identities over \(\mathbb Z[i,\sqrt2]\) and
\(\mathbb Q(i,\sqrt2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Use in the main proof]
\label{rem:cert-affine-q4-endpoint-scope}
\Cref{prop:cert-affine-q4-endpoint} classifies coefficients for the eighteen
displayed representative matrices.  The main proof supplies the transformability witness,
reduces it to those representative matrices, and implements the required constructions in
the retained Holant problem.  In particular, the support-four gadget is
the two-copy construction in
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-support-four-gadget}.  The retained-list partition and the partitions for full even-parity support use exhaustive exact computation in
\path{certificates/verify_affine_q4.py}. 
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Affine endpoint normal forms]
\label{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-normal-form}
The matrix set used by the affine endpoint certificate is exactly the
eighteen-element set \(\mathscr K_{\mathrm{aff}}\) displayed in
\cref{eq:cert-affine-q4-cores}.  Let \(f\in\cA\) be a nonzero canonically
projectively normalized arity-four signature, let
\(K_0\in\mathscr K_{\mathrm{aff}}\), and put \(q=K_0^{\otimes4}f\).  If
\[
 q({\mathbf{x}})=0\quad(\wt({\mathbf{x}})\text{ odd}),\qquad
 q(\zero^4)q(\one^4)\ne0,
\]
then there are \(\gamma,t\in\AlgNums^\times\) and a normalized
\(h\in\cA\), with \(h(\zero^4)=1\), such that
\[
 q=\gamma\,D(t)^{\otimes4}h.
\]
Moreover, \(\supp(h)\) is exactly one of the following:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item the complementary endpoint pair \(\{\zero^4,\one^4\}\);
\item a four-point plane
      \(\{\zero^4,\one^4,\one_I,\one_{I^c}\}\) with \(|I|=2\);
\item the full even-parity support.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is the direct-use form of
\cref{prop:cert-affine-q4-endpoint}\textnormal{(i)--(ii)}.  The three
supports have distinct cardinalities, so the alternatives are disjoint.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Gadgets from affine reference signatures]
\label{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-gadget-exits}
Let \(h\) be supplied by
\cref{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-normal-form}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item If \(|\supp(h)|=2\), then \(h\) is a generalized equality.
\item If \(|\supp(h)|=4\), its logical coefficient matrix
      \(F=\begin{psmallmatrix}A&B\\ C&D\end{psmallmatrix}\) satisfies
      \(AD/BC\in\{1,-1\}\).  If \(AD=BC\), then \(h\) factors
      across its two pairs of inputs into nonsingular weighted equalities.
      If \(AD=-BC\), the two-copy \(X\)-edge construction directly
      realizes a nonzero generalized equality.
\item If \(h\) has full even-parity support, then either it has the dense
      product-transformable form of
      \cref{eq:p1-eight-vertex-product-dense-form}, an \(X\)-self-loop
      is nonzero of rank one, or one of the three crossed two-copy
      contractions specified in
      \cref{prop:cert-affine-q4-endpoint}\textnormal{(iv)} directly realizes
      \(cD(r)\otimes D(r)\), where \(c\ne0\) and \(r\in\mu_4\).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The support-two assertion is immediate, and the other two assertions are
exactly \cref{prop:cert-affine-q4-endpoint}\textnormal{(iii)--(iv)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Contractions of signatures with full odd-parity support and fourth-root phases]
\label{thm:cert-interface-affine-q4-odd}
Let \(p\) be a normalized quaternary signature with full odd-parity support
and fourth-root phases. With \(X\)-edges and binary equality \(I\), the
twelve-loop test of \cref{prop:cert-twelve-loop} selects a nonzero rank-one binary if one
exists. Failing that, it selects a nonsingular binary of infinite projective
transfer order if available. If neither occurs, retain \(p\) and all its
zero--zero loops. Then either two copies directly realize a support-six
endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature, or a certified phase shift
\(p_\delta\in\cA\) has a rank-one disequality self-loop or a dense
product-transformable two-copy output. These conclusions hold at every nonzero scale.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Combine the deterministic test in \cref{prop:cert-twelve-loop} with
\cref{prop:cert-affine-q4-endpoint}\textnormal{(v)}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Equality-Accessible Matching Decks}
\label{appsec:cert-equality-accessible}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-equality-q4-boundary}

This subsection gives the shared finite quaternary certificate statement used
in \cref{sec:weighted-equality} and by the later finite-group branches.

\begin{proposition}[Gadget equality-accessible quaternary certificate]
\label{prop:cert-equality-q4-boundary}
Let \((\cF,G,q)\) be a deck-stable retained state, with \(\cF\)
factor-closed and containing the supplied equality \(I\), and
\(q\ne0\) a quaternary irreducible signature.  Suppose its complete deck consists only of zero cards and nonzero scalar multiples of the fixed
realizable binary representatives of \(G\), and suppose
\(G\) is \(\CycX{2}=\{1,[X]\}\), a
monomial dihedral group
\(\DihZ{2m}=\{[D(t)],[XD(t)]:t\in\mu_m\}\) with \(m\ge3\), the
standard Pauli \(\KleinStd\), or the exotic group
\[
 \KleinEx=\left\{[I],[X],
 \left[\begin{pmatrix}i&1\\-1&-i\end{pmatrix}\right],
 \left[\begin{pmatrix}-i&1\\-1&i\end{pmatrix}\right]\right\}.
\]
Then a gadget using at most two copies
of \(q\) produces an endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature.  The assertion covers all
zero cards, both input orientations, all six unordered contracted pairs,
and the fixed nonzero scalars of the realizable group representatives.  In every
case, the endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature can moreover be chosen not to be a
\(G\)-matching signature.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Input dressings and reversal only permute the complete group, multiply cards
by invertible factors, and contribute known nonzero scalars.  It therefore
suffices to check every standard group kernel on the six unordered pairs.

\emph{The \(\CycX{2}\) case.}
Use Walsh coordinates only for this calculation, and define the Walsh
coefficients
\[
 a_S=\widetilde q(\one_S),\qquad S\subseteq[4].
\]
For any Walsh coefficient tensor indexed by subsets of \([4]\), call it
\emph{Walsh-complement-symmetric} when
\(a_{\bar S}=a_S\) for every \(S\), where
\(\bar S=[4]\setminus S\).
For a deleted pair \(ij\), the two realizable \(I/X\)-cards satisfy
\begin{equation*}
 \widetilde C_{ij}^{\pm}(\one_T)
 =2(a_T\pm a_{Tij}).
\end{equation*}
Here \(Tij:=T\cup\{i,j\}\), with \(T\) a subset of the two residual inputs.
The allowed nonzero Walsh binary cards are scalar multiples of \(I\) or \(Z\).  Singleton residual coordinates force
\(a_S=0\) for odd \(|S|\).  For
\(\{i,j\}\sqcup\{k,l\}=[4]\), define the four Walsh coefficient scalars
\[
 u=a_\varnothing,\quad v=a_{1234},\quad
 x=a_{ij},\quad y=a_{kl}.
\]
Safety for the \(ij\)-cards gives
\[
 (y+v)^2=(u+x)^2,\qquad (y-v)^2=(u-x)^2,
\]
and safety for the complementary \(kl\)-cards gives
\[
 (x+v)^2=(u+y)^2,\qquad (x-v)^2=(u-y)^2.
\]
Adding and subtracting these four equations gives
\begin{equation*}
 v^2=u^2,\qquad y^2=x^2,\qquad
 yv=ux,\qquad xv=uy.
\end{equation*}
If \(u\ne0\), one \(\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}\) satisfies
\[
 v=\epsilon u,\qquad a_{\bar S}=\epsilon a_S.
\]
When \(u=v=0\), call a complementary weight-two block \(S,\bar S\)
\emph{active} if \(a_S\ne0\) (equivalently \(a_{\bar S}\ne0\)), and call
\(a_{\bar S}/a_S\in\{1,-1\}\) its sign.  If all active blocks have one
common sign, the same conclusion holds.  In both cases Fourier inversion
gives one parity in the original basis and complement closure: support only on even-weight
Walsh indices gives \(q({\mathbf{x}})=q(\bar {\mathbf{x}})\), while
\(a_{\bar S}=\epsilon a_S\) makes the inverse transform vanish unless
\((-1)^{\wt({\mathbf{x}})}=\epsilon\).  A realizable \(X\)-dressing then moves a nonzero
complementary pair in the original variables to \(\zero^4,\one^4\).  The result is an
endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature and remains irreducible,
whereas every quaternary \(\CycX{2}\)-matching signature is a two-binary product.

It remains to treat mixed signs, which necessarily require at least two
active complementary blocks.  Input permutations permute the three block signs, and a realizable \(X\)-dressing reverses all of them.  We may therefore choose a
positive active block and a negative active block, make any third active
block positive, and write the Walsh coefficient scalars as \(x,y,z\), with
\(xz\ne0\), such that one \(X\)-dressing and a permutation give
\begin{equation*}
 a_{12}=a_{34}=x,\qquad
 a_{13}=a_{24}=y,\qquad
 a_{14}=z,\qquad a_{23}=-z,
 \qquad xz\ne0,
\end{equation*}
where \(y\) may vanish.  For \(p=12,13,14\), join the \(p\)-inputs of two
copies by the realizable path \(X-I-X\), and call the output \(r_p\).  Its
realizable kernel is \(I\), its Walsh kernel is \(2I\), and direct contraction
gives
\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{ccc}
\toprule
 &\text{common value on }0000,1111
 &\text{values on the three complementary weight-two blocks}\ \\ \midrule
 \widetilde r_{12}/4&x^2&(y^2+z^2,0,0)\\
 \widetilde r_{13}/4&y^2&(x^2+z^2,0,0)\\
 \widetilde r_{14}/4&z^2&(x^2+y^2,2xy,0).\\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
Every nonzero row is supported only on even-weight Walsh indices and is
Walsh-complement-symmetric.  The same inverse-transform identities just used
therefore make it a complement-symmetric, one-parity signature in the original basis. A realizable \(X\)-dressing makes its two endpoints nonzero.  Hence it becomes an
endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature.

A \(\CycX{2}\)-matching tensor whose Walsh coefficients are
complement-symmetric has Walsh support on the endpoints and exactly one
weight-two block, with the two common values differing by a sign.  Indeed,
in Walsh coordinates the group is \(C_2^{\rm std}\), and each realizable binary factor
is projectively \(I\) or \(Z\). Their tensor product on a
perfect matching has precisely that support, and Walsh-complement symmetry
forces the two factor signs to agree.

If \(y=0\) and \(x^2+z^2\ne0\), \(r_{13}\) has zero Walsh endpoints and
is not matching.  If \(x^2+z^2=0\), the nonzero part of the
\(13\mid24\) flattening is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}x&-z\\ z&x\end{pmatrix}.
\]
All other rows and columns vanish, so this matrix is the complete nonzero
block and has rank one, contradicting irreducibility.  The local Walsh
transforms are invertible and therefore preserve flattening rank and irreducibility.

If \(y\ne0\) and all three outputs were matching, the first two rows would
give \(\sigma,\tau\in\{1,-1\}\) with
\begin{equation*}
 y^2+z^2=\sigma x^2,
 \qquad
 x^2+z^2=\tau y^2.
\end{equation*}
Since \(2xy\ne0\), the last row forces \(x^2+y^2=0\).  Then the first
equation gives \(\sigma=1,z^2=2x^2\), while the second gives
\(3x^2=-\tau x^2\), impossible.  Thus a realizable endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature output is outside the
\(\CycX{2}\)-matching products.

\emph{The monomial dihedral case.}
For one pair, write its four residual slices as \(A,B,C,D_{11}\).  Define the
even and odd residual-card pencils produced by the standard realizable kernels by
\[
 E(t):=A+tD_{11},\qquad O(t):=C+tB,\qquad t\in\mu_m.
\]
The pencil normal form \cref{lem:nd-root-pencil} applies verbatim to
each of these pencils.  It says that the two endpoints are proportional on
one monomial line, or, in one fixed parity plane with axes \(E_0,E_1\),
\[
 U+tV=\lambda(E_0+\alpha tE_1)
 \quad\text{or}\quad
 U+tV=\lambda(tE_0+\alpha E_1),
 \qquad \alpha\in\mu_m.
\]
Its proof uses the exact M\"obius classification
\cref{lem:nd-root-mobius}. In particular, a zero sampled value belongs only
to the proportional branch.

Apply this fact to both pencils for all six pairs.  The resulting support
conditions have exactly the following solutions: choose one total parity
and a nonempty subset of its four complementary string pairs.  Their size
histogram is
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{crrrr}
\toprule
 |\supp q|&2&4&6&8\\ \midrule
 \#&8&12&8&2.\\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
\label{eq:equality-q4-monomial-supports}
\end{equation}
Hence the support is already one parity and complement-closed.  A realizable
\(X\)-dressing makes the same copy an endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature.

\emph{The two Klein cases.}
In the Pauli Bell basis, one flattening writes
\[
 q=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\Ftwo^2}
 t_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}B_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\otimes B_{\boldsymbol{\beta}}.
\]
Its row and column cards first make \(T=(t_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}})\) a partial
monomial matrix.  Intersecting the four allowed Bell-line preimages for
every kernel on the other four pairs leaves exactly the four maximal
four-planes
\begin{equation}
 V_{\pi,\epsilon}
 =\{q:q({\mathbf{x}})=0\text{ if }\wt({\mathbf{x}})\not\equiv\pi\pmod2,\
          q(\bar {\mathbf{x}})=\epsilon q({\mathbf{x}})\},
 \quad \pi\in\Ftwo,\quad\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}.
 \label{eq:equality-q4-pauli-planes}
\end{equation}
Thus a realizable Pauli \(X\)-dressing makes every nonzero member an endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature.

For \(\KleinEx\), define the representative matrices
\[
 E_0=I,\quad E_1=X,\quad
 E_2=\begin{pmatrix}i&1\\-1&-i\end{pmatrix},\quad
 E_3=\begin{pmatrix}-i&1\\-1&i\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Their Frobenius duals are
\(E_0/2,E_1/2,E_3/4,E_2/4\), so every dual contraction is again a realizable group direction.  The locus satisfying all six pair-card conditions is the union
of twenty-four maximal two-dimensional linear subspaces.  The
support-stratified calculation also produces sixteen one-dimensional
solution subspaces, but every one is contained in one of those twenty-four
planes and hence contributes no additional component to this locus.
Realizable local exotic dressings and permutations of inputs act transitively on
the twenty-four planes.  A representative is
\[
 q_{a,b}
 =a(E_0\otimes E_0+E_1\otimes E_1)
  +b(E_2\otimes E_2+E_3\otimes E_3),
\]
whose coefficient array is
\begin{equation}
q_{a,b}({\mathbf{x}})=
\begin{cases}
a-2b,&{\mathbf{x}}\in\{0000,0110,1001,1111\},\\
a+2b,&{\mathbf{x}}\in\{0011,0101,1010,1100\},\\
0,&\wt({\mathbf{x}})\text{ odd}.
\end{cases}
\label{eq:equality-q4-exotic-plane}
\end{equation}
If \(a-2b\ne0\), this is an endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature.  If \(a=2b\ne0\), realizable \(X\)-dressings
on the last two inputs make both endpoints \(4b\).  This proves the exotic
case.

In each of the latter three cases, the displayed endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature is obtained
from the original \(q\) only by invertible group dressings and a permutation of inputs.  It is therefore irreducible.  Since every quaternary
\(G(\cF)\)-matching signature is a tensor product of two nonsingular
group binaries, these endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signatures are not \(G(\cF)\)-matching.  The
strengthened conclusion in the \(\CycX{2}\) case was proved separately above.

The support and subspace completeness claims in
\cref{eq:equality-q4-monomial-supports,eq:equality-q4-pauli-planes,%
eq:equality-q4-exotic-plane} use the exact enumeration in
\path{certificates/verify_equality_q4.py}.  It tests all \(2^{16}-1\)
nonempty computational supports in the monomial case and all \(209\)
partial-injection supports in each Bell basis.  For the latter, it
intersects the allowed preimages for four cross-pairs and four kernels,
retaining zero cards and reducing the equations over \(\mathbb Q\) or
\(\mathbb Q(i)\).  The preceding pencil proof supplies the coefficient
constraint before this finite support classification.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Classification of equality-accessible quaternary signatures]
\label{thm:cert-interface-equality-q4}
Let \((\cF,G,q)\) be a deck-stable retained, factor-closed state
containing the supplied equality \(I\), and let \(q\ne0\) be an
irreducible quaternary signature.  Suppose every realizable card is zero or a
nonzero scalar multiple of one of the fixed representatives of \(G\), where
\(G\) is \(\CycX{2}\), \(\DihZ{2m}\) with \(m\ge3\), \(\KleinStd\), or \(\KleinEx\).
Then a gadget using at most two copies of \(q\) realizes an
endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature that is not \(G\)-matching.
More precisely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item In the \(\CycX{2}\) row, put
      \(a_S:=\widetilde q(\one_S)\).  A complementary weight-two block
      \(S,\bar S\) is \emph{active} when \(a_S\ne0\), and its sign is
      \(a_{\bar S}/a_S\in\{1,-1\}\).  If the two Walsh endpoints
      \(a_\varnothing,a_{[4]}\) are nonzero or all active blocks have one
      common sign, one dressed copy suffices.  Otherwise at least two active
      blocks have different signs. This is the \emph{mixed-sign} case.  For
      \(p\in\{12,13,14\}\), let \(r_p\) join the \(p\)-inputs of two copies
      by the realizable path \(X-I-X\).  One of these three contractions is
      endpoint-nondegenerate and nonmatching.
\item In the monomial-dihedral row, the support is exactly one total
      parity together with a nonempty subset of its four complementary string
      pairs.  One realizable \(X\)-dressing makes the same copy
      endpoint-nondegenerate.
\item In either Klein row, realizable dressings and permutations of inputs give the
      asserted irreducible endpoint-nondegenerate nonmatching copy.
\end{enumerate}
All clauses include zero cards, both orientations, all six contracted pairs,
and the fixed nonzero scalars of the chosen representatives.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The overall conclusion is \cref{prop:cert-equality-q4-boundary}.  Its
\(\CycX{2}\), monomial-dihedral, and two Klein proof cases give clauses
\textnormal{(i)}, \textnormal{(ii)}, and \textnormal{(iii)}, respectively.
The containment sentence in the exotic-Klein case makes its
representative-plane argument exhaustive.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Marked Dihedral Matching Decks}
\label{appsec:cert-marked-dihedral}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-normalized-dih-q6}

The six-input support classification below is used by the standard
\(\DihZ{2m}\) branch. The whole-network reductions in
\cref{thm:marked-B-reduction,thm:marked-A0-reduction} carry the
\(\DihY{2m}\) and \(\DihXzero{2m}\) cases to that branch.
For \(\DihXone{2m}\), \cref{lem:marked-A-gauge-transport,lem:marked-A-primitive-transport}
preserve this support argument with weighted kernels. We reduce its
\(2304\) labelled parameter choices to the explicit \(48\)-case table below.

Fix the distinguished pair \(p=\{1,2\}\) and residual inputs
\(R=\{3,4,5,6\}\).  For each sector
\(\epsilon\in\Ftwo\), choose a perfect matching
\[
 M_\epsilon=\{e_\epsilon,e'_\epsilon\}
 \quad\text{of }R,
\]
distinguish the fixed edge
\(e_\epsilon=\{u_\epsilon,v_\epsilon\}\), and order the other edge as
\(e'_\epsilon=(r_\epsilon,s_\epsilon)\) with
\(r_\epsilon<s_\epsilon\).    Choose
\(a_\epsilon,b_\epsilon,o_\epsilon\in\Ftwo\).  Define the eight-string
support \(S\subseteq\Ftwo^6\) by taking, for every
\(\epsilon,z\in\Ftwo\), the two strings satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 (x_1,x_2)&=(z,z+\epsilon),\\
 x_{u_\epsilon}+x_{v_\epsilon}&=a_\epsilon,\\
 (x_{r_\epsilon},x_{s_\epsilon})
   &=(o_\epsilon+z,o_\epsilon+z+b_\epsilon),
 \end{aligned}
 \qquad\text{over }\Ftwo.
 \label{eq:cert-nd-q6-four-flat-support}
\end{equation}
This is exactly the support form obtained from four complementary flat
endpoints: the fixed monomial factor supplies the two strings in a slice,
and the two endpoints of the variable factor are complementary.

\begin{proposition}[Six-to-four support lowering in the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) case]
\label{prop:cert-nd-q6-support-lowering}
Among the \(3^2\cdot2^8=2304\) labelled choices in \cref{eq:cert-nd-q6-four-flat-support}, exactly \(96\)
give a matching coset: for some perfect matching \(M\) and constants
\(c_e\in\Ftwo\), the support is
\[
  \{{\mathbf{x}}\in\Ftwo^6:x_u+x_v=c_{\{u,v\}}
      \text{ for every }\{u,v\}\in M\}.
\]
For every other choice there are an input pair \(e=\{e_1,e_2\}\) and
\(\gamma\in\Ftwo\) such that projection away from \(e\) is injective on
\[
 S\cap\{{\mathbf{x}}:x_{e_1}+x_{e_2}=\gamma\},
\]
and the projected quaternary support has size six or eight.  Choosing a
largest such projection gives \(1152\) support-six cases and \(1056\)
support-eight cases.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Permute the four residual inputs so that the fixed edge in sector zero is
\(34\), and its ordered varying edge is \((5,6)\).  Independently flipping
bits on inputs \(3,5,6\) removes \(a_0,o_0,b_0\), respectively: use the
flips \(a_0,o_0,o_0+b_0\).  Thus sector zero is
\[
 S_0=\{(z,z,w,w,z,z):z,w\in\Ftwo\}.
\]
These permutations and bit flips preserve matching cosets, projection
injectivity, and projected support sizes.  For each of the
\(3\cdot2\cdot2^3=48\) choices of sector-zero data, fix one such
normalization.  It bijects the \(48\) choices of sector-one data with the
following canonical choices: a fixed edge \(e\subseteq\{3,4,5,6\}\),
the increasingly ordered complementary edge \(e'=(r,s)\), and
\((a,b,o)\in\Ftwo^3\).  Their four strings satisfy
\[
 (x_1,x_2)=(z,z+1),\qquad
 x_u=w,\quad x_v=w+a\quad(e=\{u,v\},\ u<v),\qquad
 (x_r,x_s)=(o+z,o+z+b).
\]

The table gives a projection witness for every canonical choice.  An
entry \(ij_\gamma^s\) means: retain strings with \(x_i+x_j=\gamma\),
delete inputs \(i,j\), and obtain \(s\) distinct projected strings from
exactly \(s\) retained strings.  Thus the projection is injective.
\(\mathrm M\) denotes a matching coset.
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{c|cccccccc}
\toprule
\(e\backslash(a,b,o)\)&000&001&010&011&100&101&110&111\\
\midrule
\(34\)&\(15_0^8\)&\(25_0^8\)&\(\mathrm M\)&\(\mathrm M\)&\(15_0^8\)&\(25_0^8\)&\(15_0^8\)&\(16_0^8\)\\
\(35\)&\(16_0^8\)&\(26_0^8\)&\(24_1^6\)&\(14_1^6\)&\(14_0^6\)&\(24_0^6\)&\(26_0^8\)&\(16_0^8\)\\
\(36\)&\(15_0^8\)&\(25_0^8\)&\(24_1^6\)&\(14_1^6\)&\(14_0^6\)&\(24_0^6\)&\(25_0^8\)&\(15_0^8\)\\
\(45\)&\(16_0^8\)&\(26_0^8\)&\(23_1^6\)&\(13_1^6\)&\(13_0^6\)&\(23_0^6\)&\(26_0^8\)&\(16_0^8\)\\
\(46\)&\(15_0^8\)&\(25_0^8\)&\(23_1^6\)&\(13_1^6\)&\(13_0^6\)&\(23_0^6\)&\(25_0^8\)&\(15_0^8\)\\
\(56\)&\(13_0^6\)&\(13_1^6\)&\(15_0^6\)&\(15_0^6\)&\(13_0^6\)&\(13_1^6\)&\(13_0^6\)&\(13_1^6\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Each entry follows by substituting \((z,w)\in\Ftwo^2\) in the two
displayed sectors.  For example, at \(e=35\), \((a,b,o)=010\), the
\(24_1^6\) projection leaves
\[
 \{0001,0100,0111,1000,1011,1110\}
\]
on inputs \((1,3,5,6)\), with one preimage per string.

For maximality of the entries marked \(6\), an eight-string projection
would require a pair with constant XOR on both sectors.  The pairs with
constant XOR on \(S_0\) are
\(12,15,16,25,26,34,56\).  Substitution of the sector-one equations in
each \(6\)-entry eliminates these pairs, or leaves a projection that
identifies two strings.  A seven-string parity slice is impossible: on each
four-string affine sector its size is \(0,2\), or \(4\).  Thus the displayed
six-string images are maximal.  At the two \(\mathrm M\)-entries the
matching equations are, respectively,
\[
 x_3=x_4,\ x_1=x_5,\ x_2=x_6;
 \qquad
 x_3=x_4,\ x_1=x_6,\ x_2=x_5.
\]
A parity contraction of a three-edge matching coset has at most four
projected strings, so none of the other entries is a matching coset.
The canonical table therefore has \(2,24,22\) cases of the three stated
types.  Multiplying by the \(48\) sector-zero choices gives
\[
 48(2,24,22)=(96,1152,1056),
\]
as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[From support projection to contraction]
\label{rem:cert-nd-q6-support-scope}
In the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) setting, the supplied equality \(I\)
gives the contraction kernel \(XIX=I\), and a direct edge gives the kernel
\(X\). They implement parity \(\gamma=0\) and \(\gamma=1\), respectively.
Injectivity leaves one nonzero summand for each projected string, so the
conclusion holds for arbitrary nonzero coefficients on the support.
The main proof supplies the sector-ruling hypothesis and exposes any
resulting factors by \cref{lem:factor-saturation}. The realizable
contractions in the following theorem are taken in this setting.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Six-to-four contractions in the standard \(\DihZ{2m}\) case]
\label{thm:cert-interface-nd-q6}
Let \(h\) be an arity-six signature whose support is a set \(S\) of the form
\cref{eq:cert-nd-q6-four-flat-support}, and suppose every coefficient of
\(h\) on \(S\) is nonzero.  Then either \(S\) is a three-edge matching
coset, or there are an input pair \(e=\{e_1,e_2\}\) and
\(\gamma\in\Ftwo\) for which projection away from \(e\) is injective on
\[
 S\cap\{{\mathbf{x}}:x_{e_1}+x_{e_2}=\gamma\}
\]
and has image of size six or eight.  In the latter case, contraction by the
realizable kernel \(I\) for \(\gamma=0\), or \(X\) for \(\gamma=1\), directly
realizes a cancellation-free quaternary of support size six or eight.  That
      output is not a product of two nonsingular monomial binaries.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{prop:cert-nd-q6-support-lowering} gives the exhaustive support
alternative and injectivity.  The selected parity kernel leaves exactly one
nonzero summand above each projected string, proving noncancellation.  A product
of two nonsingular monomial binaries has support size four.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Klein-Four Matching Decks}
\label{appsec:cert-klein-four}
\label{proofsubsec:extended-v4-certificates}

The following certificate supplies the exotic Klein finite cases used
in \cref{sec:v4-deck}.

\subsubsection{The exotic Klein form
  \texorpdfstring{\(\KleinEx\)}{V4 (ex)}}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-exotic-v4}

\begin{proposition}[Exotic-Klein six- and eight-input certificate]
\label{prop:cert-exotic-v4-q6-q8}
Use the four exotic binary directions \(E_a\), their Frobenius duals, and
the label set \(V=\Ftwo^2\) from
\cref{eq:exotic-v4-basis,eq:exotic-v4-Gram}.  The following finite
statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item Let \(h\ne0\) have arity six, suppose every exotic pair card is zero
      or a product of two exotic binaries, and normalize one nonzero card by
      \[
        C_{12}^0=E_0^{(3,4)}E_0^{(5,6)}.
      \]
      The other three card directions range over the complete set of normalized
      card-direction assignments
      \[
        \bigl(\{0\}\cup\mathfrak D_4(\KleinEx)\bigr)^3,
        \qquad (1+48)^3=117{,}649.
      \]
      Over arbitrary algebraic nonzero-card scales, including every
      cancellation locus in every other card context, exactly seventeen
      projective scale lines survive.  Every one reconstructs a product of
      three exotic binaries. No irreducible arity-six line survives.
\item Let \(h\) have arity eight and suppose it has the normal form \cref{eq:exotic-v4-q8-linear-normal-form}, with one matching and
all four cards nonzero.  If every one of its
      \(28\cdot4=112\) exotic pair cards is zero or a product of three
      exotic binaries, then \(h\) has a proper tensor factor.
      \end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
All matching lines and card maps below are generated directly from the four
matrices in \cref{eq:exotic-v4-basis}.  The calculation first verifies the
Gram matrix, the dual basis
\[
 (E_0/2,E_1/2,E_3/4,E_2/4),
\]
and projective fusion closure, so its contractions use the same normalized
directions as the main argument.

For part~\textnormal{(i)}, there are \(3\cdot4^2=48\) projective
quaternary matching lines.  Encode zero by \(-1\), and encode the line with
matching \(m\in\{0,1,2\}\) and labels \(a,b\in V\) by \(16m+4a+b\).
Thus a nonnegative code denotes the canonically normalized line
\(P_m(E_a,E_b)\).  On each base residual edge \(34\) and \(56\), the
stabilizer under realizable operations contains the paired endpoint dressings with label pairs
\[
 (1,1),\qquad(2,3),\qquad(3,2),
\]
which fix the base \(E_0\)-edge projectively.  Together with the permutations of inputs
\[
 (3\,4),\qquad(5\,6),\qquad(3\,5)(4\,6),
\]
these generate the stabilizer under realizable operations used here.  Its
induced action on the 48 residual matching lines has order \(64\).  It
partitions
the full \(49^3\) card-direction assignments into 4,045 orbits, and the generated
orbit-size histogram is
\[
 1^{27},\quad2^{49},\quad4^{351},\quad8^{521},\quad
 16^{577},\quad32^{1830},\quad64^{690}.
\]
The orbit sizes weighted by these multiplicities sum to \(117\,649\), so
the generated orbits cover every card-direction assignment.  On one representative of every
orbit, exact \(\mathbb Q(i)\)-row reduction computes the union of the
preimages of the forty-eight matching lines within each nonbase pair/kernel
context, then intersects these unions over all 56 contexts.  A zero
output lies in every matching-line preimage, and nonzero-card scales remain
independent linear variables. In particular, no cancellation locus is divided away.

The only surviving direction triples are
\begin{equation*}
 (-1,-1,-1),\quad (1,2,3),\quad(1,3,2),\quad
 (4,8,12),\quad(4,12,8).
\end{equation*}
The first has the single scale line \((1)\).  Each of the other four has
exactly the four scale lines
\begin{equation*}
 (1,\epsilon,\delta/2,\epsilon\delta/2),
 \qquad \epsilon,\delta\in\{1,-1\}.
\end{equation*}
Thus the nonzero-card histogram consists of one line with a single nonzero card
and sixteen lines with every card nonzero.  An independent enumeration of all
\(15\cdot4^3=960\) three-binary product lines reconstructs exactly these
seventeen tensors, proving part~\textnormal{(i)}.

For part~\textnormal{(ii)}, the prescribed form is
\cref{eq:exotic-v4-q8-linear-normal-form}, with arbitrary
\(\lambda_{\boldsymbol\gamma}\ne0\) and \(A_j\in\GL_2(\Ftwo)\).
Apply the two-contraction argument in the proof of \cref{lem:exotic-v4-q8},
on \((3,5)\) and \((4,5)\) with \(E^0=I/2\). It uses exactly these
conditions to contradict the matching-product overlap formula
\cref{eq:exotic-v4-overlap-size}, giving the asserted factor conclusion.

Every scale condition in part~\textnormal{(i)} is a finite union of homogeneous
linear systems over \(\mathbb Q(i)\).  Prescribed liveness is also exact:
over an infinite field, a linear space contains a vector avoiding all
coordinate hyperplanes exactly when no coordinate vanishes identically on
that space.  Base change to \(\overline{\mathbb Q}\), or to
\(\mathbb C\), therefore creates no additional component, and zero target
cards are kept as zero-card subspaces.  The exact calculation for part~\textnormal{(i)} regenerates the states,
orbits, scale intersections, and factor tensors.
The Python verification script is
\path{certificates/verify_exotic_v4_q6_q8.py}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Six-input tensors for the exotic Klein group]
\label{thm:cert-interface-exotic-v4-q6}
Let \(h\ne0\) have arity six.  If every exotic pair card of \(h\) is zero
or a product of two exotic binaries, then \(h\) is a product of three
exotic binaries.  Consequently no irreducible arity-six remaining case exists.
This includes arbitrary algebraic scales and every cancellation locus.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is \cref{prop:cert-exotic-v4-q6-q8}\textnormal{(i)} after undoing the
normalization of one nonzero card.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Eight-input tensors for the exotic Klein group]
\label{thm:cert-interface-exotic-v4-q8}
Let \(h\) have arity eight and satisfy the displayed normal form \cref{eq:exotic-v4-q8-linear-normal-form}, with one matching and
all four cards nonzero.  If all \(112\) exotic
pair cards of \(h\) are zero or products of three exotic binaries, then
\(h\) has a proper tensor factor.  
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is \cref{prop:cert-exotic-v4-q6-q8}\textnormal{(ii)}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Platonic Matching Decks}
\label{appsec:cert-platonic}
\label{proofsec:platonic-matching-certificates}

The Platonic analysis first solves the separated-sign equations for
localized card maps. The incidence classification then determines how
these local configurations fit together. The final calculations handle
the tetrahedral arity-eight case and the arity-four contractions used by
the main proof. All configurations use the quaternion basis in
\cref{eq:quaternion-basis}.

Whenever a mixed three-point incidence is localized, realizable dressings put
its rainbow image in the common normalization
\begin{equation}
 A=P_0(Q_0,Q_0),\qquad
 B=P_1(Q_0,Q_0),\qquad
 C=P_2(Q_2,Q_2)=A-B.
 \label{eq:cert-A4-normal-incidence}
\end{equation}
Recall that matching-label type \((1,1,1)\) is rainbow, and \((s)\) is monochromatic. Set
\begin{equation}
\begin{gathered}
 R_0=\tfrac12P_2(Q_0,Q_0),\qquad
 R_1=-\tfrac12P_2(Q_1,Q_1),\qquad
 R_3=-\tfrac12P_2(Q_3,Q_3),\\
 R_+=Q_0+Q_1+Q_2+Q_3,\qquad
 R_-=Q_0-Q_1-Q_2-Q_3.
\end{gathered}
\label{eq:cert-platonic-common-vectors}
\end{equation}
Here \(R_+,R_-\) are the matrices already used in
\cref{eq:tetrahedral-H6-equivalence}.

\begin{lemma}[Matching-product solutions of the separated-sign equations]
\label{lem:platonic-bridge-formula}
Let \(S\) be the swap matrix, defined by \(S({\mathbf{u}}\otimes {\mathbf{v}})={\mathbf{v}}\otimes {\mathbf{u}}\).
For \(U,V\in\GL_2(\mathbb C)\) and \(\lambda\ne0\),
\begin{equation}
 \rank\bigl((U\otimes V^{\mathsf T})S-\lambda I_4\bigr)=1
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad V=\lambda U^{-\mathsf T}.
 \label{eq:platonic-swap-rank-one}
\end{equation}
For \(G\in\{\TetInt,\OctDD,\IcoZero\}\), put
\(\mathscr C_G=\{0\}\cup\operatorname{cone}(\mathfrak D_4(G))\).
Then
\begin{equation}
 \{W:W,A-2W,2W-B\in\mathscr C_G\}
 =\{0,A/2,B/2\}\cup
 \left\{\tfrac12P_2(J,J^{-\mathsf T}):[J]\in G,
                                      \ J^{\mathsf T}=J\right\}.
 \label{eq:platonic-symmetric-bridge}
\end{equation}
Write a symmetric group matrix as \(J_{\mathbf{u}}=aQ_0+bQ_1+dQ_3\), choosing a
nonzero real coefficient vector \({\mathbf{u}}=(a,b,d)\), and put
\(R_{\mathbf{u}}=\tfrac12P_2(J_{\mathbf{u}},J_{\mathbf{u}}^{-\mathsf T})\).
A triple of solutions to the separated-sign equations sums to \((A+B)/2\) precisely when it is a
permutation of \((0,A/2,B/2)\), or is \((R_{\mathbf{u}},R_{\mathbf{v}},R_{\mathbf{w}})\) with
\({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{w}}\) pairwise orthogonal in \(\mathbb R^3\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Conjugation by \(U^{-1}\otimes I\) reduces the rank condition to
\((I\otimes W^{\mathsf T})S-\lambda I_4\), with
\(W=U^{\mathsf T}V=\begin{psmallmatrix}a&b\\c&d\end{psmallmatrix}\).
Its matrix is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}
 a-\lambda&0&c&0\\
 b&-\lambda&d&0\\
 0&a&-\lambda&c\\
 0&b&0&d-\lambda
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
The second row has nonzero second entry.  Rank one forces the first row
to vanish, hence \(a=\lambda,c=0\). Comparing the second and third rows
then gives \(b=0,d=\lambda\).  Conversely, \(S-I_4\) has rank one.
This proves \cref{eq:platonic-swap-rank-one}.

The three exceptional separated-sign solutions \(0,A/2,B/2\) satisfy the conditions,
since \(C=A-B=P_2(Y,Y)\) and \([Y]\in G\).  For any other solution,
all three tested vectors are nonzero.  A line with two points of one
matching and a point of another would give flattening ranks \(1,1,4\),
which is impossible.  If \(W=P_0(U,V)\), the condition on \(2W-B\)
therefore gives matching \(2\).  Its rank-one flattening and
\cref{eq:platonic-swap-rank-one} imply
\(W=\tfrac12P_0(U,U^{-\mathsf T})\).
The condition on \(A-2W\) has matching \(0\), so
\(I\otimes I-U\otimes U^{-\mathsf T}\) is simple.  The simple-tensor
sum criterion forces \(U\) to be scalar, giving \(W=A/2\), a
contradiction.  The matching-\(1\) case similarly gives \(W=B/2\).

Thus \(W=P_2(U,V)\), and \(A-2W\) has matching \(1\).
Its \(13\mid24\) flattening is
\(I_4-2(U\otimes V^{\mathsf T})S\). Hence
\(W=\tfrac12P_2(U,U^{-\mathsf T})\).
Apply the invertible local matrices \((U^{-1},I,U^{\mathsf T},I)\),
which fix \(B\).  They send \(2W\) to
\(P_2(I,U^{-\mathsf T}U)\).  Since \(2W-B\) has matching \(0\),
\cref{eq:platonic-swap-rank-one} with \(\lambda=1\) now gives
\(U^{-\mathsf T}U=I\), or \(U^{\mathsf T}=U\).
Conversely, for symmetric \(U\), the two differences are local images
of \(P_1(Y,Y)\) and \(-P_0(Y,Y)\).  All leg matrices belong projectively
to \(G\), so both differences are in its matching cone.  This proves
\cref{eq:platonic-symmetric-bridge}, including arbitrary scales.

For the sum equation, polarization of
\(\tfrac12P_2(J_{\mathbf{u}},\operatorname{adj}(J_{\mathbf{u}}))\), where
\(\operatorname{adj}(J_{\mathbf{u}})=aQ_0-bQ_1-dQ_3\), defines an injective linear map
\(T\) from the symmetric \(3\times3\) matrices to quaternary tensors with
\[
 T({\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\mathsf T})=\tfrac12P_2(J_{\mathbf{u}},\operatorname{adj}(J_{\mathbf{u}})),\qquad
 T(I_3)=(A+B)/2,\qquad
 R_{\mathbf{u}}=T\!\left(\frac{{\mathbf{u}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\mathsf T}}{{\mathbf{u}}^{\mathsf T}{\mathbf{u}}}\right).
\]
The six coefficient tensors are independent and \(C\notin\operatorname{im}T\):
in the \(14\mid23\) binary basis they use only
\(Q_\mu\otimes Q_\nu\) with \(\mu,\nu\in\{0,1,3\}\), whereas
\(C=Q_2\otimes Q_2\).  Also
\[
 A/2=\tfrac12T(I_3)+C/4,\qquad
 B/2=\tfrac12T(I_3)-C/4.
\]
Comparison of the \(C\)-coefficient forces equal numbers of \(A/2\)
and \(B/2\). If they occur, the third summand is zero.  Otherwise the
trace of \(T^{-1}R_{\mathbf{u}}\) is one, so all three summands are nonzero
\(R\)-vectors.  Their sum is \(T(I_3)\) exactly when their normalized
real coefficient columns form a matrix \(E\) with
\(EE^{\mathsf T}=I_3\).  This is equivalent to
\(E^{\mathsf T}E=I_3\), proving the orthogonality criterion.
The three orthogonal rank-one projectors are independent. Together with
\(C\notin\operatorname{im}T\), they give card-map rank four.
The exceptional triple spans \(A,B\) and gives rank two.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The four fixed-equality octahedral forms
  \texorpdfstring{\(\OctDD,\OctTT,\OctDT,\OctTD\)}%
  {O (dd), O (tt), O (dt), and O (td)}}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-S4}

For the octahedral certificate, use the standard \(24\)-point set
\(\OctDD\) in
\cref{eq:quaternion-basis,eq:octahedral-domain}, ordered as the four axes, the
eight sign points, and the twelve pair points \(Q_\mu\pm Q_\nu\), with
pairs lexicographic. The second
unmixed distinguished-involution normal form is
\begin{equation}
 \OctTT=H_{\mathrm{tt}}^{-1}\OctDD H_{\mathrm{tt}}.
 \label{eq:cert-S4-second-form}
\end{equation}
Use the coefficient-similarity map \(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}\), transformation
tuple \(\mathbf H_{\mathrm{tt}}\), and induced coefficient operator
\(\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}\) fixed in
\cref{eq:octahedral-second-form,eq:octahedral-coefficient-map}. The
similarity induces the corresponding map on projective group classes.
Here \(P'_m\) denotes the same ordered matching placement as \(P_m\), but
in the \(\OctTT\) coefficient coordinates.
Writing \(X=Q_1\) projectively, direct ordered contraction gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}P_0(U,V)&\doteq
 P'_0(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(U),\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(V)),\\
\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}P_1(U,V)&\doteq
 P'_1(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(UX),\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(XV)),\\
\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}P_2(U,V)&\doteq P'_2(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(U),
                                  \theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(XVX)).
\end{aligned}
\label{eq:cert-S4-coefficient-transport}
\end{equation}
The operator \(\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}\) compares coefficient arrays.
Realizable dressings use the corresponding \(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(U)\)
representatives with their disequality-edge orientations and nonzero scalars,
as in the fixed-coordinate convention of \cref{sec:platonic-decks}.

The coset classification in \cref{prop:cert-platonic-incidence-atlas} gives
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\begin{tabular}{c@{\qquad}c@{\qquad}c}
\toprule
domain points&three-point lines&four-point lines\\
\midrule
24&32&18\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Every point lies on seven rich lines.  The three-lines and
four-lines are single realizable left/right orbits, represented by
\[
 \{[Q_0],[R_+],[R_-]\}
\]
and
\[
 \{[Q_0],[Q_1],[Q_0+Q_1],[Q_0-Q_1]\},
\]
respectively.

The quaternary deck has \(3\cdot24^2=1728\) projective points.  Its complete
maximal-rich-line histogram is
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\begin{tabular}{ccr}
\toprule
line size&matching-label type&number\\
\midrule
3&\((1,1,1)\)&13\,824\\
3&\((3)\)&4\,608\\
4&\((4)\)&2\,592\\
\midrule
&&21\,024\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
There is no \((2,1)\) line and no mixed four-line.  Representatives of
the three rows are
\begin{align*}
&\{P_0(Q_0,Q_0),P_1(Q_0,Q_0),P_2(Q_2,Q_2)\},\\
&\{P_0(Q_0,Q_0),P_0(Q_0,R_+),P_0(Q_0,R_-)\},\\
&\{P_0(Q_0,Q_0),P_0(Q_0,Q_1),
       P_0(Q_0,Q_0+Q_1),P_0(Q_0,Q_0-Q_1)\},
\end{align*}
where \(R_+\) and \(R_-\) are the two explicitly fixed representatives in
\cref{eq:cert-platonic-common-vectors}.

For a mixed three-point incidence, use the normalization
\eqref{eq:cert-A4-normal-incidence}.  For a selected ordered frame, let
\(\Phi\) be the linear card map determined by \(\Phi(Q_0)=C/2\) and by
\((\Phi(Q_1),\Phi(Q_2),\Phi(Q_3))\) equal to that ordered frame. Define the
image tensors \(W_j:=\Phi(Q_j)\), and write
\(\rank\Phi:=\dim\operatorname{im}\Phi\).  Before imposing the twelve pair
points of \eqref{eq:octahedral-domain}, the bridge is
\begin{equation*}
 \{0,A/2,B/2\}\cup\mathcal R,
\end{equation*}
where the subscripts in \(R_{\mu\nu}^{\epsilon\delta}\) specify the
two quaternion axes and the superscripts specify the displayed signs:
\begin{align*}
\mathcal R=\{&R_0,R_1,R_3,\\
&R_{01}^{+-}=\tfrac14P_2(Q_0+Q_1,Q_0-Q_1),
\quad R_{01}^{-+}=\tfrac14P_2(Q_0-Q_1,Q_0+Q_1),\\
&R_{03}^{+-}=\tfrac14P_2(Q_0+Q_3,Q_0-Q_3),
\quad R_{03}^{-+}=\tfrac14P_2(Q_0-Q_3,Q_0+Q_3),\\
&R_{13}^{++}=-\tfrac14P_2(Q_1+Q_3,Q_1+Q_3),
\quad R_{13}^{--}=-\tfrac14P_2(Q_1-Q_3,Q_1-Q_3)\}.
\end{align*}
By \cref{lem:platonic-bridge-formula}, these are all bridge vectors:
the symmetric octahedral directions are the three axes and the six
\(Q_\mu\pm Q_\nu\) directions with \(\mu,\nu\in\{0,1,3\}\).
An orthogonal triple is either the three axes or a pair of opposite
diagonal directions in one coordinate plane together with the remaining
axis.  There are therefore four unordered frames.  The sum equation
\(W_1+W_2+W_3=(A+B)/2\) leaves 30 ordered maps: the six
\(\rank\Phi=2\) maps permute \((0,A/2,B/2)\), and the other twenty-four are
the six orderings of each of the four \(\rank\Phi=4\) frames
\begin{equation}
 \begin{split}
 &\{R_0,R_1,R_3\},\qquad
 \{R_0,R_{13}^{++},R_{13}^{--}\},\\
 &\{R_1,R_{03}^{+-},R_{03}^{-+}\},\qquad
 \{R_3,R_{01}^{+-},R_{01}^{-+}\}.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:cert-S4-four-frames}
\end{equation}
The 1,727 deck points other than \([A]\) lie on 1,683 projective lines
through \([A]\). The numbers of other deck points on those lines are
\[
 1^{1645},\qquad2^{32},\qquad3^6.
\]
The single realizable kernel
\begin{equation*}
 K_*=Q_0+Q_1,
 \qquad [K_*]\in\OctDD
\end{equation*}
has the following property: for every ordered rank-four frame,
\(q_R=\Phi(K_*)=C/2+R\) is not a matching-deck point.  The 24 supports
have sizes
\[
 2^4,\qquad8^{12},\qquad16^8.
\]
The coefficient correspondence in
\cref{eq:cert-S4-coefficient-transport} identifies the second normal form.
Its realizable constructions use the
\(\OctTT\)-kernels and dressings in
\cref{prop:p1-cert-S4-uniform-eight-vertex-separator}.   Invertible local coefficient maps
preserve the three ranks.

For the two mixed forms, use the coefficient similarities
\(H_{\mathrm{dt}},H_{\mathrm{td}}\), groups \(\OctDT,\OctTD\), and
operators \(\mathcal L_{\mathrm{dt}},\mathcal L_{\mathrm{td}}\) fixed in
\cref{eq:octahedral-mixed-conjugators,eq:octahedral-mixed-forms,%
eq:octahedral-mixed-coefficient-transport}.  The three exact transport
identities there identify every matching label and absorb their displayed
orientation factors into matrices of the current mixed group.  They
compare coefficient tables, while the following proposition supplies
constructions in the current marked group.

\begin{proposition}[Gadget octahedral signature-separator certificate]
\label{prop:p1-cert-S4-uniform-eight-vertex-separator}
For every ordering of every frame in
\cref{eq:cert-S4-four-frames}, use the realizable kernel
\(K_*=Q_0+Q_1\) in the \(\OctDD\) form and the realizable kernel
\(K'_*:=\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(K_*)\) in the \(\OctTT\) form.  The
resulting direct quaternary can be dressed, using only realizable local
matrices in the current marked octahedral group, to an endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature of
flattening ranks \((2,2,2)\).  Similarity by \(H_{\mathrm{tt}}\) identifies the coefficient tables.

For \(\alpha\in\{\mathrm{dt},\mathrm{td}\}\), put
\(K_{*,\alpha}:=\theta_\alpha(K_*)\), so
\([K_{*,\alpha}]\in\OctForm{\alpha}\), and use the fixed realizable kernel in
that projective class, with its realization scalar recorded.  The resulting
direct quaternary has, using only current-group contractions and
dressings, one of three certified exits: an endpoint-nondegenerate
eight-vertex signature of ranks \((2,2,2)\), a generalized equality,
or a nonsingular binary \(B\) with \([BX]\notin\OctForm{\alpha}\).
The same three-way conclusion holds for each of the six rank-deficient maps in
each mixed form.  All representatives, determinants, and outside-transfer
directions are given in the proof below.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Define the tetrahedral sign matrices by
\[
  G_{\epsilon_1\epsilon_2\epsilon_3}
  =Q_0+\epsilon_1Q_1+\epsilon_2Q_2+\epsilon_3Q_3.
\]
For the first marked form the separator is \(q_R=C/2+R\), where \(R\)
is the first member of the ordered frame.  In the even-string order
\cref{eq:octahedral-even-order}, exact substitution gives the following
realizable dressings. Every omitted odd-weight coefficient is zero, and the
displayed vectors are projective.
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{ccc}
\toprule
\(R\)&\(\mathbf L\)&\(\mathbf e(\mathscr D_{\mathbf L}q_R)\)\\
\midrule
\(R_0\)&\(I^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,1,-1,1,1,-1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_1\)&\((I,I,Q_1,Q_1)\)&\((1,0,0,0,0,0,0,1)\)\\
\(R_3\)&\(I^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,1,-1,-1,-1,-1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_{01}^{+-}\)&\(G_{+++}^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,-1,1,-i,i,1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_{01}^{-+}\)&\(G_{+++}^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,-1,1,i,-i,1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_{03}^{+-}\)&\(I^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,1,-1,i,-i,-1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_{03}^{-+}\)&\(I^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,1,-1,-i,i,-1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_{13}^{++}\)&\(G_{++-}^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,i,-i,-i,-i,-i,i,-1)\)\\
\(R_{13}^{--}\)&\(G_{++-}^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,-i,i,i,i,i,-i,-1)\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Both endpoints are nonzero in every row.  Since the separator depends
only on the first frame member, these nine rows cover all twenty-four
ordered maps.  Direct row reduction of the three flattenings of
every displayed \(q_R\) gives \((2,2,2)\).

For the second marked form, use the coefficient-transport operator
\(\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}\) above, and define the transported card map
\(\Phi'\) and
transported direct card \(q'_R\) by
\[
  \Phi'(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}C_{\rm ker})=\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}\Phi(C_{\rm ker}),
  \qquad
  q'_R:=\Phi'(K'_*)=\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}q_R.
\]
Here \([K'_*]\in\OctTT\), so \(q'_R\) is a direct card in the
second marked form.
With the nonzero comparison scalar \(\lambda:=2\sqrt2(1-i)\), the identities
\begin{equation*}
  H_{\mathrm{tt}}H_{\mathrm{tt}}^{\mathsf T}=\lambda X,
  \qquad
  H_{\mathrm{tt}}^{-\mathsf T}H_{\mathrm{tt}}^{-1}=\lambda^{-1}X
\end{equation*}
show that \(\mathcal L_{\mathrm{tt}}\) bijects the two coefficient decks.
This is only
an analytic correspondence.  The following input matrices are separately
verified to belong projectively to the realizable group \(\OctTT\). In the
table, \(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}\) acts componentwise on every displayed tuple:
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{ccc}
\toprule
\(R\)&\(\mathbf L'\)&\(\mathbf e(\mathscr D_{\mathbf L'}q'_R)\)\\
\midrule
\(R_0\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(G_{++-},G_{++-},G_{++-},G_{+-+})\)&
\((1,-1,-1,-1,-1,-1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_1\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(I,I,I,I)\)&\((1,1,1,-1,-1,1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_3\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(G_{+++})^{\otimes4}\)&\((1,-1,1,1,1,1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_{01}^{+-}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(G_{+++},G_{--+},G_{--+},G_{+++})\)&
\((1,0,0,0,0,0,0,1)\)\\
\(R_{01}^{-+}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(G_{+++},G_{+--},G_{+--},G_{+++})\)&
\((1,-1,-1,1,1,-1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_{03}^{+-}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(I,I,Q_1,Q_1)\)&\((1,1,-1,-1,-1,-1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_{03}^{-+}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(I,I,Q_2,Q_2)\)&\((1,0,0,0,0,0,0,1)\)\\
\(R_{13}^{++}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(G_{++-},G_{-++},G_{++-},G_{-++})\)&
\((1,-1,-1,1,1,-1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_{13}^{--}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}}(G_{++-},G_{---},G_{+-+},G_{-++})\)&
\((1,0,0,0,0,0,0,1)\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Again both endpoints are nonzero.  All dressings are invertible, so the
three certified ranks remain \((2,2,2)\).

It remains to verify the mixed rows directly in their fixed-
\(I\) coordinates.  For \(\alpha\in\{\mathrm{dt},\mathrm{td}\}\), absorb the three
matching-dependent orientation factors in
\cref{eq:octahedral-mixed-coefficient-transport} into the current-
group representatives.  The kernel \(K_{*,\alpha}\) first gives the
realizable card \(\mathcal L_\alpha q_R\).  On inputs \(2,4\), apply the
current-group dressing \(\theta_\alpha(S_\alpha^{-1})\), where
\(S_\alpha=H_\alpha H_\alpha^{\mathsf T}\).  Its product with the
\(H_\alpha^{\mathsf T}\) leg of \(\mathcal L_\alpha\) is
\(H_\alpha^{-1}\), so the resulting direct card
\(q_{R,\mathrm{act}}^\alpha\) has the normalized representative
\begin{equation}
 q_R^\alpha:=(H_\alpha^{-1})^{\otimes4}q_R,
 \qquad q_{R,\mathrm{act}}^\alpha\doteq q_R^\alpha.
 \label{eq:cert-S4-mixed-direct-card}
\end{equation}
These realizable dressings preserve the retained reduction data.  When another dressing on inputs \(2,4\) is listed
below, compose the two group elements into one chosen representative on
that input.  The total is at most four dressings, or two dressings and one
pair contraction for an outside-binary row.  All exact
determinants below refer to this normalized representative. The corresponding
realizable output differs by a nonzero scalar, so only determinant
nonvanishing is used.  Put
\[
 \delta_\alpha(B):=
 [c_Q(H_\alpha(BX)H_\alpha^{-1})].
\]
Thus \(\delta_\alpha(B)\notin\mathcal R_{\OctDD}\) is exactly the assertion
\([BX]\notin\OctForm{\alpha}\).

For \(\OctDT\), the three first-frame entries in the following
table give endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signatures.  All odd
coefficients vanish, and the last column is in the even-string order fixed
above.
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{ccc}
\toprule
\(R\)&realizable dressing&\(\mathbf e(\mathscr D q_R^{dt})\)\\
\midrule
\(R_0\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(G_{--+})^{\otimes4}\)&
 \((1,-1,1,-1,-1,1,-1,1)\)\\
\(R_1\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(G_{---})^{\otimes4}\)&
 \((1,1,-1,-1,-1,-1,1,1)\)\\
\(R_3\)&identity&\((1,1,-1,-1,-1,-1,1,1)\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The other six possible first entries have the uniform outside-binary exits
listed below. The kernel acts on inputs \(1,2\).
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{cccc}
\toprule
\(R\)&realizable kernel&\(\delta_{\mathrm{dt}}(B)\)&\(\det B\)\\
\midrule
\(R_{01}^{+-}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(G_{---})\)&\([1,-1,1,0]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/64\)\\
\(R_{01}^{-+}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(G_{---})\)&\([1,-1,0,-1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/64\)\\
\(R_{03}^{+-}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(Q_0)\)&\([1,-3,1,-1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\(R_{03}^{-+}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(Q_0)\)&\([1,-3,-1,1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\(R_{13}^{++}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(Q_0)\)&\([1,-3,1,1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\(R_{13}^{--}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(Q_0)\)&\([1,-3,-1,-1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

For \(\OctTD\), no dressing is needed for the two endpoint rows:
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
 \mathbf e(q_{R_0}^{td})&=(1,i,-i,i,i,-i,i,-1),\\
 \mathbf e(q_{R_3}^{td})&=(1,i,-i,-i,-i,-i,i,-1).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
Both have zero odd coefficients and ranks \((2,2,2)\).  The \(R_1\) row is
\[
 q_{R_1}^{td}=\frac{3+2\sqrt2}{8}
  (\one[0011]+\one[1100]),
\]
so realizable \(X\)-dressings on inputs \(3,4\) give
\(\frac{3+2\sqrt2}{8}(\Eq_4)\), a generalized equality.
Compose the dressing on input \(4\) with its preceding correction. Together
with the corrections on inputs \(2,4\), this uses only three dressed inputs.
The remaining rows have the following outside-binary exits on inputs
\(1,2\):
\begin{center}
\normalsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabular}{cccc}
\toprule
\(R\)&realizable kernel&\(\delta_{\mathrm{td}}(B)\)&\(\det B\)\\
\midrule
\(R_{01}^{+-}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(Q_0)\)&\([0,2,-1,1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\(R_{01}^{-+}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(Q_0)\)&\([0,2,-1,-1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\(R_{03}^{+-}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(G_{--+})\)&\([1,2,0,1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/64\)\\
\(R_{03}^{-+}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(G_{--+})\)&\([1,-2,0,-1]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/64\)\\
\(R_{13}^{++}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(Q_0)\)&\([1,2,-1,0]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\(R_{13}^{--}\)&\(\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(Q_0)\)&\([1,-2,1,0]\)&
 \((51+36\sqrt2)/256\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Finally let \(q_\pi^\alpha\) denote the six normalized mixed reconstructed
rank-deficient outputs in the same input convention as
\cref{eq:cert-S4-mixed-direct-card}.  Put
\[
 K_{\mathrm{dt}}^{\mathrm{def}}:=\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(Q_0-Q_2),
 \qquad
 K_{\mathrm{td}}^{\mathrm{def}}:=\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(Q_2-Q_3),
\]
and let
\(B_{\pi,\alpha}^{\mathrm{def}}
 :=\partial_{12}^{K_\alpha^{\mathrm{def}}}q_\pi^\alpha\).
The fixed chosen representative of \([K_\alpha^{\mathrm{def}}]\) on inputs
\(1,2\) works uniformly for all six outputs in each row:
\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
\alpha&K_\alpha^{\mathrm{def}}&
 \{\delta_\alpha(B_{\pi,\alpha}^{\mathrm{def}}):\pi\in S_3\}
 &\det B_{\pi,\alpha}^{\mathrm{def}}\\ \hline
dt&\theta_{\mathrm{dt}}(Q_0-Q_2)&
 \{[1,-1,0,-1]^2,[1,-3,1,-1]^2,[1,-3,-1,-1]^2\}&
 (51+36\sqrt2)/256\\
td&\theta_{\mathrm{td}}(Q_2-Q_3)&
 \{[0,2,1,1]^2,[1,2,0,1]^2,[1,2,1,0]^2\}&
 (51+36\sqrt2)/256.
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
Here \([a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3]\) denotes the projective quaternion-coordinate
point \([(a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3)^{\mathsf T}]\), equivalently the matrix class
\([a_0Q_0+a_1Q_1+a_2Q_2+a_3Q_3]\) under \(c_Q\), and an exponent records
multiplicity.  Every determinant displayed in the mixed tables is nonzero.
Every pulled-back direction has support three, or support four with unequal
nonzero magnitudes, whereas the quaternion-coordinate points of
\(\mathcal R_{\OctDD}\) have support one, two, or four with equal nonzero
magnitudes.  Hence all the listed transfer
classes are outside their realizable mixed group.  Direct row reduction gives
rank pattern \((2,2,2)\) in every endpoint row.  This proves all full-rank
and rank-deficient mixed exits using only current-form kernels and
dressings, while the similarities serve only to write the coefficient
arrays.
\end{proof}

For the fixed-matching residue in the standard form, the four axes and
eight tetrahedral sign points give the quadratic test set of
\cref{lem:platonic-global-ruling}.  In any nonstandard form, apply the same
test after precomposing \(\Phi\) with the corresponding one of
\(\theta_{\mathrm{tt}},\theta_{\mathrm{dt}},\theta_{\mathrm{td}}\), and then pull the resulting
tensor identity back by its inverse.    In all four forms, the evaluation
matrix of the ten homogeneous quadratic
 monomials in the four quaternion coordinates has full column rank.  Hence
 every pulled-back Segre minor vanishes identically, giving a genuine
common factor rather than a new octahedral remaining case.

\Cref{prop:cert-platonic-incidence-atlas} proves the domain and deck
incidences by cyclic cosets, tensor factorization, and realizable group actions.
\Cref{lem:platonic-bridge-formula} proves the bridge intersections and
reduces their sum equations to the displayed orthogonal frames.

\subsubsection{The internal tetrahedral permutation classes}
\label{proofsubsec:cert-A4}

For the tetrahedral certificate, order the standard twelve points
\(\TetInt\) in \cref{eq:tetrahedral-domain} as the four axes
followed by the eight sign points.  The coset classification in \cref{prop:cert-platonic-incidence-atlas}
