      \(K_{\IcoZero}:=\phi Q_1-\tau Q_2+Q_3\) gives a quaternary of ranks
      \((3,3,3)\), and the fixed subsequent realizable kernel
      \(L_{\IcoZero}:=Q_0-\tau^2Q_1+\tau Q_3\) gives a nonsingular binary \(B\) with \([BX]\notin\IcoZero\), for all \(30\) solutions.  The statement
      includes arbitrary complex scales and cancellations.  For
      \(\IcoOne\), the realizable kernels
      \(K_{\IcoOne}=\kappa_D(K_{\IcoZero})\) and
      \(L_{\IcoOne}=\kappa_D(L_{\IcoZero})\) give the corresponding
      direct fixed-\(I\) identities.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Clause~\textnormal{(i)} is the exact octahedral bridge
\cref{eq:cert-S4-four-frames} together with the separate gadget certificate
\cref{prop:p1-cert-S4-uniform-eight-vertex-separator}.  Clause~\textnormal{(ii)}
is \cref{eq:cert-A4-bridge,eq:cert-A4-twelve-maps} and the all-card,
affine-phase, and realizable-orientation checks in
Appendix~\ref{proofsubsec:cert-A4}, including
\cref{prop:p1-cert-A4-eight-vertex-orientation-terminal}.  Clause~\textnormal{(iii)}
is the exact group, bridge, frame, and rank calculation in
Appendix~\ref{proofsubsec:cert-A5}, followed by the uniform explicit gadget reduction in
\cref{prop:cert-platonic-q4-exits}.
\Cref{lem:icosahedral-fixed-I-dictionary} gives the opposite display with
realizable \(\kappa_D\)-kernels and leaves the supplied equality unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Classification of Platonic quaternaries]
\label{thm:cert-interface-platonic-q4}
Let
\[
 G\in\{\TetInt,\OctDD,\OctTT,\OctDT,
 \OctTD,\IcoZero,\IcoOne\}.
\]
The finite quaternary certificates have the following directly usable form.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\roman*)}]
\item Let \(q\) be quaternary and define
      \(L_p(C)=\Phi_p^q(C)X\).  If, for every one of the six pairs \(p\)
      and every fixed chosen representative \(K_g\),
      \[
      L_p(K_g)=0\qquad\hbox{or}\qquad[L_p(K_g)]\in G,
      \]
      then, for each pair \(p\), put \(r_p:=\rank L_p\).  One has
      \(r_p\ne3\).  If \(r_p=0\), then \(q=0\). If
      \(r_p\in\{1,4\}\), then \(q\) has a binary-pair factorization. \(r_p=2\) can occur only for the octahedral forms: it is actually
      dressable to \(\Eq_4\) for \(\OctDD\), and to an
      endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature of ranks \((2,2,2)\)
      for \(\OctTT\), \(\OctDT\), and \(\OctTD\),
      with the mixed vectors in
      \cref{eq:p1-platonic-q4-mixed-eight-vertex}.
\item Let \(h\ne0\) have arity six and let \(\Phi_p^h\) have rank two or
      three.  Suppose, for every \(g\in G\), the card
      \(\Phi_p^h(K_g)\) formed with its fixed chosen representative is zero
      or a nonsingular \(G\)-matching product, with two nonzero cards in
      this \(p\)-family using different residual matchings.  Realizable localization
      produces a quaternary of ranks \((3,3,3)\), and a contraction
      of that quaternary gives a nonsingular binary \(B\) with
      \([BX]\notin G\).  This includes the separately verified
      \(\OctTT\) and mixed representatives and both icosahedral
      displays.
\item For each fixed \(G\), every corresponding enumerated rank-deficient
      quaternary has the following conclusion.  The same conclusion holds
      for the tetrahedral-orientation outputs when \(G=\TetInt\), the
      octahedral-frame outputs when
      \(G\in\{\OctDD,\OctTT,\OctDT,\OctTD\}\),
      and the icosahedral-frame outputs
      when \(G\in\{\IcoZero,\IcoOne\}\).  Each such quaternary
      directly realizes one of: an
      endpoint-nondegenerate eight-vertex signature of ranks \((2,2,2)\), a
      generalized equality of arity four, or a nonsingular binary
      \(B\) with \([BX]\notin G\).  Each realization uses the quaternary and
      at most four realizable \(G\)-binaries.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For clause~\textnormal{(i)}, use
\cref{lem:icosahedral-fixed-I-dictionary} for the \(\IcoOne\)
coefficient calculation, replacing every realizable kernel by its realizable
\(\kappa_D\)-image and leaving the \(I\) fixed.  Fix a pair \(p\), and let \(A_p\)
be the matrix of \(L_p\) in the corresponding quaternion root coordinates.
The card conditions at \(p\) give the first condition in
\cref{eq:cert-q4-two-sided-endomorphisms}.  For the complementary pair
\(\bar p\), let \(\mathbf R_X\) be the coordinate matrix of right
multiplication \(C\mapsto CX\).  Direct complementary-pair reshaping gives
\(A_{\bar p}=\mathbf R_XA_p^\sharp\mathbf R_X\). The fixed \(X\)-reversals
permute the root configuration, so the card conditions at \(\bar p\) give
the second condition. In the normalized coordinates this places \(A_p\) in
\(\mathscr E_{G_0}\), where \(G_0\in\{\TetInt,\OctDD,\IcoZero\}\) is the standard representative for \(G\).
Now \cref{prop:cert-platonic-q4-preservers} gives all stated rank and
reconstruction alternatives, including the separately verified
\(\OctTT\) eight-vertex output and the direct mixed reconstructions above.

Clause~\textnormal{(ii)} combines the realizable localization and rank
calculation in \cref{prop:cert-platonic-deficient-mixed} with the
corresponding uniform outside-binary row of
\cref{prop:cert-platonic-q4-exits}.  Clause~\textnormal{(iii)} is the
representative-wise realization statement of the latter proposition.  The
card and network covariance in
\cref{lem:icosahedral-fixed-I-dictionary} supplies all three clauses for
\(\IcoOne\) directly with realizable \(\kappa_D\)-kernels.
\end{proof}


\section{General Boundary Representations}
\label{app:general-boundaries}

The gadget convention of \cref{subsec:holant-definitions} uses exposed
vertex inputs and isolated edges. We now describe the general representation
with free edge endpoints and prove that it gives the same replacement
signature, gluing operations, and charge identities.

We also allow an edge to end at a free stub, a half-edge with no tensor
vertex.  In this extension,
\(H(\Omega)=(\bigsqcup_vH_v)\sqcup H_{\rm free}(\Omega)\).
Every free stub is paired and belongs to \(\partial\Omega\). A vertex
half-edge is unpaired exactly when it belongs to \(\partial\Omega\).
Thus \(E_{\rm int}(\Omega)\), the matching of pairs of half-edges, contains each
nonboundary vertex half-edge and each free stub exactly once.  An edge box
\(\mathsf E_W\) denotes one edge with default kernel \(W\), contributing
one factor \(W(a,b)\).  It has no vertex or separate \(H_v\).
By contrast, an \(I\) or \(X\) supplied as an input signature labels an
ordinary vertex, counted and transformed with all other tensor vertices.

Write \(\mathbf W=(W_e)_{e\in E_{\rm int}(\Omega)}\), with
\(W_e\in\{I,X\}\).  For a string \({\mathbf z}_{\partial\Omega}\) in boundary
order, define the raw boundary signature by
\[
 R_\Omega({\mathbf z}_{\partial\Omega})
 =\sum_{\substack{\tau:H(\Omega)\to\{0,1\}\\\tau|_{\partial\Omega}={\mathbf z}_{\partial\Omega}}}
    \prod_v f_v(\tau|_{H_v})
    \prod_{\{h,h'\}\in E_{\rm int}(\Omega)}W_{\{h,h'\}}(\tau_h,\tau_{h'}).
\]
For the \(j\)-th boundary half-edge, put \(A_j=I\) at a vertex half-edge and
\(A_j=W_e\) at a free endpoint of the edge \(e\).  The boundary tensor used
for vertex replacement is
\begin{align}
 T_\Omega&=\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{|\partial\Omega|}A_j\right)R_\Omega,
       \label{eq:half-edge-boundary-tensor}\\
 Z_{\mathbf W}(\Omega)&:=T_\Omega(\varnothing)
   \qquad(\partial\Omega=\varnothing).
 \label{eq:half-edge-partition-function}
\end{align}
For the ordinary boundary convention of
\cref{subsec:holant-definitions}, \(T_\Omega=R_\Omega\).
At a free endpoint, \(W_e^2=I\) cancels the incident edge: deleting that
endpoint and edge and exposing the ordinary mate preserves \(T_\Omega\)
and the boundary order.  An edge with two free endpoints is an isolated
\(\mathsf E_W\), with boundary tensor \(WWW=W\).  These cancellations
reduce every open network to unpaired vertex half-edges and isolated edge
boxes.  Gluing and vertex replacement are specified in
\cref{subsec:reduction-conventions}.

For homogeneous edges, abbreviate \(Z_{\mathbf W}\) by \(Z_W\).
The empty network is the nullary unit \(1\).

\subsection{Gluing and coordinate transport}
In either homogeneous model, joining two boundary half-edges contracts their
boundary-tensor coordinates against the default kernel \(W\). The remaining
boundary order is inherited.  The ordinary-boundary case is given in
\cref{subsec:reduction-conventions}. For one paired free endpoint and one
unpaired vertex half-edge,
splice the latter into the former's incidence, retaining its existing edge.
For two free endpoints on distinct edges, delete those endpoints and their
two edges and join their mates by one \(W\)-edge.  If both endpoints belong
to the same isolated \(\mathsf E_W\), remove that component and record the
scalar \(\sum_{a,b}W(a,b)^2=2\).  These rules follow from \(W^2=I\) and
\(W^3=W\).  In particular, vertex replacement first cancels the one-free-end
edges as above and then inserts the resulting ordinary-boundary gadget.
Each remaining isolated \(\mathsf E_W\) splices into a single edge.
Closed components containing only such edge boxes contribute known factors
of \(2\).  This proves \cref{eq:direct-gadget-reduction}, including loops,
without leaving free stubs in the oracle instance.

When transporting an open gadget with disequality edges by \(D\in GO(X)\), first cancel
its one-free-end edges in this way.  Every remaining internal edge between
vertex half-edges contributes the scalar from \(D^{\mathsf T}XD=\lambda X\).
An isolated edge remains available because \(DXD^{\mathsf T}=\lambda X\).
Thus its boundary tensor is transported by \(D\) up to a nonzero scalar. The closed-network edge count in \cref{eq:GOX-global-scalar} is used
only for closed instances.

\subsection{Boundary charge}
\label{app:boundary-charge}
For a homogeneous \(X\)-edge network (all structural pairs of half-edges have
kernel \(X\), any explicitly supplied \(I\) or \(X\) is an ordinary tensor
vertex), let
\(U(\Omega)=H(\Omega)\setminus\bigcup_{e\in E_{\rm int}(\Omega)}e\) be the
unpaired boundary half-edges (by definition,
\(U(\Omega)\subseteq\partial\Omega\)).  Call an assignment
\emph{edge-supported} when every structural edge-kernel factor is nonzero on
the two bits assigned to its endpoints.  Every edge-supported assignment has
one \(0\)-bit and one \(1\)-bit on each
\(X\)-pair. Hence, for every edge-supported assignment \(\tau\),
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_v q_{\arity(f_v)}(\tau|_{H_v})
  +\sum_{h\in H_{\rm free}(\Omega)}(2\tau_h-1)
  =\sum_{h\in U(\Omega)}(2\tau_h-1).
\end{equation*}
For a contribution to the boundary tensor \(T_\Omega({\mathbf{z}})\), an ordinary
boundary bit equals \(\tau_h\), while a free boundary bit equals
\(1-\tau_h\) by \cref{eq:half-edge-boundary-tensor}.  Moving the free-stub
sum to the right therefore gives
\(q_{|\partial\Omega|}({\mathbf{z}})=\sum_v q_{\arity(f_v)}(\tau|_{H_v})\).
In standard closed instances there are
no free stubs and \(U(\Omega)=\varnothing\), so
\(\sum_vq_{\arity(f_v)}(\tau|_{H_v})=0\).  Thus \(X\)-contraction preserves
the total charge in every closed contributing assignment.


\section{Exact Algebraic Tools}
\label{app:exact-algebraic-tools}

This appendix fixes the standard conventions used to decide common
transformability and extract the algebraic witnesses in
\cref{lem:algebraic-transform-witness}.

Work over the exact coefficient field \(L\). For a finite polynomial
system \(f_1,\ldots,f_s\in R=L[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\), let
\(I=(f_1,\ldots,f_s)\) be the ideal they generate and write
\[
 V_E(I)=\{\mathbf a\in E^n:f(\mathbf a)=0\text{ for every }f\in I\}
\]
for its zero locus over an algebraically closed extension \(E\supseteq L\).
A projective zero locus is defined by homogeneous equations in the
specified projective space. Zero loci are Zariski closed, their complements
are Zariski open, and finite unions of intersections of open and closed
sets are constructible. Nonvanishing conditions \(p_1\cdots p_t\ne0\)
can be encoded by an auxiliary variable \(z\) and the equation
\(z p_1\cdots p_t=1\), as in \cref{lem:algebraic-transform-witness}.

For a fixed monomial order, a Gr\"obner basis of \(I\) has leading
monomials generating the leading-monomial ideal of \(I\).
Buchberger's algorithm computes such a basis and terminates by the
ascending-chain condition on monomial ideals. Division by the basis
decides ideal membership. In particular, the weak Nullstellensatz gives
\[
 V_{\overline L}(I)=\varnothing\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad1\in I,
\]
equivalently, the reduced Gr\"obner basis is \(\{1\}\)
\cite[Chapters~2--4]{CoxLittleOShea2015}.

Exact algebraic points can be obtained by elimination and triangular
decomposition. For an ordered variable split, the elimination ideal is
\(I\cap L[x_{k+1},\ldots,x_n]\). In a triangular set, successive equations
have distinct largest variables; the leading coefficient in that variable
is the initial. For \(\Delta\in R\), saturation is
\[
 I:\Delta^\infty=\{f:\Delta^m f\in I\text{ for some }m\ge0\}.
\]
A regular chain is a triangular set whose successive initials are
non-zero-divisors modulo the ideal obtained by saturating the preceding
equations by their initials. Its constructible branch requires those
initials to be nonzero. Regular-chain decomposition produces finitely
many such branches. Generic algebraic specialization assigns algebraic
values to free coordinates outside the exceptional zero loci of the
required initials and denominators. Minimal polynomials with isolating
data then represent exact algebraic sample points
\cite{AubryLazardMorenoMaza1999,Cohen1993}.

These tools supply the emptiness test and exact witness construction in
\cref{lem:algebraic-transform-witness}.

For a field extension \(L'/L\), base change extends the coefficients and
sends \(I\) to \(I_{L'}=I\,L'[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\).
When \(L\subseteq\mathbb C\), complexification is base change to
\(L'=\mathbb C\).


\section{Character-Flat Extensions of the Reed--Muller Calculus}
\label{proofsubsec:rm-pauli-tools}
\label{proofsec:reed-muller}

Allowing all four Bell signatures extends the flat-Lagrangian network
construction to signatures with linear signs on their support. This
appendix characterizes that class and proves signed Radon localization
and recognition results. These structural extensions complement the
\(\CycX{2}\) result of \cref{thm:reed-muller-rigidity}. Closure for \(\KleinStd\) is proved by the
tensor-line argument in \cref{thm:standard-v4-relative}.
We retain \hyperref[subsubsec:symmetric-binary-spaces]{the symmetric-space conventions}.

We call an arity-\(2k\) signature \emph{character-flat} when a linear sign
character is also allowed:
\begin{equation}
 \mathsf{CFlat}_{2k}
 =\left\{
 c(-1)^{\ell({\mathbf{x}})}\one[{\mathbf{x}}\in {\mathbf{a}}+L]:
 c\in\AlgNums^\times,\ {\mathbf{a}}\in\Ftwo^{2k},\ \ell\in(\Ftwo^{2k})^\vee,
 \ L=L^\perp
 \right\}\cup\{0\}.
 \label{eq:character-flat-class}
\end{equation}
Define the signature class
\(\mathsf{CFlat}=\bigcup_{k\ge0}\mathsf{CFlat}_{2k}\).  This is a
subclass of \(\cA\).  The following network-calculus lemma describes
its realization by \(\RMcore\) and all four Bell binaries.

A common character-flat \(K\)-presentation is defined by
\cref{def:common-K-presentation} with \(\cC=\mathsf{CFlat}\).
It implies a common affine \(K\)-presentation because
\(\mathsf{CFlat}\subseteq\cA\).  Here an
\emph{\(\RMcore\)--Pauli network} means a tensor network whose
nonbinary vertices are retained copies of \(\RMcore\), whose binary
vertices are realizable ordered Bell binaries, and whose internal connections
are \(X\)-edges.  The next lemma gives the exact Pauli network
calculus for this class.  
The remaining lemmas extend Radon localization and recognition from
\(\FLag\) to \(\mathsf{CFlat}\).

\begin{lemma}[Character-flat Pauli network calculus]
\label{lem:v4-character-flat-calculus}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Every network whose vertices are \(\RMcore\) and realizable ordered Bell
      binaries \(B_{d,\ell}\), and whose internal edges are \(X\)-edges, has
      boundary signature in \(\mathsf{CFlat}\).
\item If \(\RMcore\) and realizable nonzero representatives of all four ordered
      Bell binaries \(B_{d,\ell}\) are retained, then, for every
      \(0\ne f\in\mathsf{CFlat}_{2k}\), an \(O(k^2)\)-size realizable
      \(\RMcore\)--Pauli network has boundary equal to a nonzero
      scalar multiple of \(f\).
\item Every nonzero character-flat signature of arity at most six is, after
      a permutation of inputs, a nonzero scalar multiple of a product of ordered
      Bell binaries on a perfect matching.
\item \(\mathsf{CFlat}\) is closed under nonzero scalars, tensor products,
      permutations of inputs, single-input ordered-Bell attachments through \(X\)-edges, and nonzero tensor factors.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let \(B\) be the displayed boundary-input set and let
\(V_{\rm vert}\) be the tensor-vertex set.  Use the boundary normalization of \cref{lem:rm-flat-contexts},
so \(B\) consists of unpaired vertex inputs.  Let \(H_{\rm int}\) be the set of internal half-edges.  For every
vertex let \(L_v\) be the translation space of its affine support.  The
Reed--Muller code and the two-coordinate repetition space of every
Bell binary are self-dual.  Define the internal equality subspace
\(E_{\rm eq}\), the ambient
constraint subspace \(H_{\rm con}\), and the vertex-code sum \(C_0\) by
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 E_{\rm eq}
   &=\{{\mathbf{y}}\in\Ftwo^{H_{\rm int}}:
       y_h=y_{h'}\text{ on every internal pair }\{h,h'\}\},\\
 E_{\rm eq}&=E_{\rm eq}^\perp,
 &H_{\rm con}&=\Ftwo^B\oplus E_{\rm eq},\\
 C_0&=\bigoplus_{v\in V_{\rm vert}}L_v=C_0^\perp.
 \end{aligned}
\]
Exactly as in the proof of \cref{lem:rm-pure-boundary}, the feasible boundary
translation space is
\(L=\pi_B(C_0\cap H_{\rm con})=L^\perp\).

Define the feasible half-edge code
\(\mathcal K:=C_0\cap H_{\rm con}\) and its
internal-fiber subspace
\[
 J:=\mathcal K\cap
   (\{\mathbf 0_B\}\oplus\Ftwo^{H_{\rm int}}),
\]
where \(\mathbf 0_B\) is the zero boundary string.
The product of the ordered-Bell signs is a linear character
\((-1)^{\lambda({\mathbf{z}})}\) on the half-edge variables.  For a fixed feasible
boundary string the internal fiber is \({\mathbf{z}}_0+J\), and
\[
 \sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in J}(-1)^{\lambda({\mathbf{z}}_0+{\mathbf{j}})}
 =(-1)^{\lambda({\mathbf{z}}_0)}\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in J}(-1)^{\lambda({\mathbf{j}})}.
\]
If \(\lambda|_J\ne0\), every nonempty boundary fiber cancels. Otherwise
the same nonzero scalar remains on every fiber and the character descends
to the boundary, proving item~1.

Conversely, the network in \cref{lem:rm-flat-contexts}
realizes the indicator of \({\mathbf{a}}+L\).  Attaching a Bell binary through \(X\)
induces, up to a nonzero sign, one of \(I,X,Z,Y\), so realizable ordered-Bell
attachments implement
  the shift and linear character, proving item~2.  More explicitly,
  \(B_{d,\ell}X\) is respectively \(X,Y,I,Z\) for
  \((d,\ell)=(0,0),(0,1),(1,0),(1,1)\), up to the fixed orientation signs.
Arity zero is the empty matching.  At positive arity at most six,
\cref{lem:rm-low-flat-matching} splits the self-dual translation space into
two-coordinate repetition lines.  Support shifts and characters turn
their factors into ordered Bell signatures, proving item~3.

For the factor claim in item~4, let \(f=f_1\otimes f_2\ne0\).
The two projections of its affine support are affine. Write its Cartesian
support as \(({\mathbf{a}}_1+L_1)\times({\mathbf{a}}_2+L_2)\), with
\(L=L_1\oplus L_2\).  Since \(L=L^\perp\), each \(L_j\) is
self-dual in its own coordinate space.  Fixing a supported string of the
other factor restricts the character of \(f\) to a character on
\({\mathbf{a}}_j+L_j\), up to a nonzero scalar.  Hence \(f_j\) is character-flat.
The other closure properties follow by the same direct-sum and character
identities.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[No Pauli-network inverse from \(\RMcore\) to \(\Hcore\)]
\label{cor:no-rm-to-H6}
No network using only \(\RMcore\), realizable ordered Bell binaries, and \(X\)-edges has boundary proportional to \(\Hcore\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Every nonzero such boundary lies in \(\mathsf{CFlat}\) by
\cref{lem:v4-character-flat-calculus}.  A character-flat six-input support is
a coset of a self-dual subspace of \(\Ftwo^6\), hence has dimension three.
By direct expansion of \cref{eq:H6}, the support of \(\Hcore\) is the
six-bit even-parity space, of dimension five.  Thus
\(\Hcore\notin\mathsf{CFlat}\), a contradiction.
\end{proof}

The support-coset and character data in
\cref{eq:character-flat-class} can be evaluated by affine Gaussian
elimination.

For every even \(n\), define the accessible-direction set
\[
\operatorname{Acc}_n
 =(\one^n)^\perp\setminus\{\zero^n,\one^n\}.
\]
Equivalently, \(\operatorname{Acc}_n\) consists of the nonzero even-weight
vectors other than \(\one^n\).  In the two smallest even arities,
\[
 \operatorname{Acc}_2=\varnothing,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Acc}_4
   =\{{\mathbf{e}}_i+{\mathbf{e}}_j:1\le i<j\le4\}.
\]
Recall
\[
 O(n,2):=\{A\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\Ftwo):A^{\mathsf T}A=I_n\}.
\]
For \(A\in O(n,2)\), the graph subspace \(\operatorname{Graph}(A)\)
is self-dual, as in \cref{lem:rm-radon-accessible}.
Let \(\Gamma_A\) denote a fixed realizable \(\RMcore/I/X\)-network supplied by
\cref{lem:rm-flat-contexts} whose boundary is a nonzero scalar multiple of
\(\one[\operatorname{Graph}(A)]\).

\begin{lemma}[Realizable signed Radon cards]
\label{lem:v4-signed-radon}
Assume \(\RMcore\) and chosen representatives of all four ordered Bell
binaries are retained, and let \(g\) have even arity \(n\).  For each
\({\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Acc}_n\), use the recorded data
\((A_{\mathbf{v}},i_{\mathbf{v}},j_{\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{v}},\Pi_{\mathbf{v}},W_{\mathbf{v}})\) fixed in
\cref{lem:rm-radon-accessible}, and fix the graph network
\(\Gamma_{A_{\mathbf{v}}}\).  For \(d,\ell\in\Ftwo\), using one copy of \(g\), this
graph network, \(X\)-edges, and the realizable ordered Bell binary
\(B_{d,\ell}\), one directly realizes, up to a nonzero scalar, the
signed card
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal S_{{\mathbf{v}},d,\ell}g([{\mathbf{x}}]_{\mathbf{v}})
 =(-1)^{\ell(A_{\mathbf{v}}{\mathbf{x}})_{i_{\mathbf{v}}}}
  \bigl(g({\mathbf{x}})+(-1)^\ell g({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{v}})\bigr),
 \qquad [{\mathbf{x}}]_{\mathbf{v}}\in H_{{\mathbf{v}},d}/\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{v}}\}.
 \label{eq:v4-signed-radon}
\end{equation}
The cases \(\ell=0\) and \(\ell=1\) are called respectively the
\emph{plus} and \emph{minus} signed cards on layer \(d\).  The prefactor
\((-1)^{\ell(A_{\mathbf{v}}{\mathbf{x}})_{i_{\mathbf{v}}}}\) is the exterior Bell sign in the chosen
quotient coordinates. After stripping this sign and the nonzero scalar, the two orbit numerators are
\(g({\mathbf{x}})+g({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{v}})\) and \(g({\mathbf{x}})-g({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{v}})\).
The remaining \(n-2\) graph-output coordinates give an explicit affine
isometry from this quotient to the boundary coordinate space.  Replacing
\((A_{\mathbf{v}},i_{\mathbf{v}},j_{\mathbf{v}})\) by another such choice changes the displayed analytic
card only by a quotient affine isometry, a nonzero scalar, and a linear sign
character.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Connect \(g\) to the input side of \(\Gamma_{A_{\mathbf{v}}}\) through \(X\)-edges, and let \({\mathbf{x}}\) denote the variables at \(g\).  The graph input
is therefore \({\mathbf{x}}+\one\).  Every \(A\in O(n,2)\) fixes \(\one\): invariance
of \({\mathbf{z}}\cdot {\mathbf{z}}={\mathbf{z}}\cdot\one\) gives \(A^{\mathsf T}\one=\one\), and hence
\(A\one=\one\).  Thus the graph output is
\({\mathbf{z}}=A_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathbf{x}}+\one)=A_{\mathbf{v}}{\mathbf{x}}+\one\).  Connect output positions \(i_{\mathbf{v}},j_{\mathbf{v}}\) to
\(B_{d,\ell}\) through their two \(X\)-edges.  The ordered Bell
arguments are then
\[
 u=z_{i_{\mathbf{v}}}+1=(A_{\mathbf{v}}{\mathbf{x}})_{i_{\mathbf{v}}},
 \qquad
 w=z_{j_{\mathbf{v}}}+1=(A_{\mathbf{v}}{\mathbf{x}})_{j_{\mathbf{v}}}.
\]
Consequently its support equation is
\[
 u+w
 ={\mathbf{x}}\cdot A_{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathsf T}({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{\mathbf{v}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{j_{\mathbf{v}}})
 ={\mathbf{x}}\cdot {\mathbf{v}}=d.
\]
For fixed remaining graph-output coordinates there are two lifts, differing
by \({\mathbf{e}}_{i_{\mathbf{v}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{j_{\mathbf{v}}}\) in \({\mathbf{z}}\) and hence by \({\mathbf{v}}\) in \({\mathbf{x}}\).  The Bell sign
is \((-1)^{\ell u}=(-1)^{\ell(A_{\mathbf{v}}{\mathbf{x}})_{i_{\mathbf{v}}}}\) and changes between those
lifts by \((-1)^\ell\), giving
\cref{eq:v4-signed-radon}.  The graph relation is single-valued before the
pair contraction, so the realization scalar is nonzero.

Each choice identifies the quotient affinely and isometrically with its
remaining graph-output coordinates.  The transition between two choices is
therefore an affine isometry.  Their selected-coordinate functionals differ
on the quotient by a linear form and a constant, which gives the last
assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Localization from two character-flat Radon cards]
\label{lem:v4-character-two-chart}
Assume the resources of \cref{lem:v4-signed-radon} are retained.  Let
\(g\) have even arity \(n\ge10\), fix
\({\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Acc}_n\), and suppose that the two plus cards
\(\mathcal S_{{\mathbf{v}},0,0}g\) and \(\mathcal S_{{\mathbf{v}},1,0}g\) are zero or
character-flat.  Define, only for the analysis, the orbit-sum signature
\[
 F_{\mathbf{v}}=\mathsf P_{\mathbf{v}}^+g.
\]
If \(F_{\mathbf{v}}\notin\cA\), connecting one copy of \(g\) to a
\(\RMcore\)--Pauli network realizes a nonzero \(H\notin\cA\) of
arity at most eight.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use the recorded data
\((A_{\mathbf{v}},i_{\mathbf{v}},j_{\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{v}},\Pi_{\mathbf{v}},W_{\mathbf{v}})\) from
\cref{lem:rm-radon-accessible}, and put \(W:=W_{\mathbf{v}}\).  The plane \(\Pi_{\mathbf{v}}\)
is nondegenerate, and the map \(\iota_{{\mathbf{v}},\epsilon}\) identifies \(W\)
isometrically with
\(H_{{\mathbf{v}},\epsilon}/\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{v}}\}\).  Since \(F_{\mathbf{v}}\) is
invariant under \(T_{\mathbf{v}}\), its two plus Radon cards, in these quotient coordinates, are
\[
 f_\epsilon({\mathbf{x}}):=F_{\mathbf{v}}(\epsilon {\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{v}}+{\mathbf{x}}),
 \qquad {\mathbf{x}}\in W.
\]
For each nonzero Radon card choose a presentation
\hypertarget{eq:v4-chart-presentation}{}
\begin{equation*}
 f_\epsilon({\mathbf{x}})
 =c_\epsilon(-1)^{\lambda_\epsilon({\mathbf{x}})}
  \one[{\mathbf{x}}\in {\mathbf{a}}_\epsilon+M_\epsilon],
 \qquad M_\epsilon=M_\epsilon^\perp\le W.
\end{equation*}
Here \({\mathbf{a}}_\epsilon\) is chosen modulo \(M_\epsilon\), and
\(\lambda_\epsilon\) is an ambient extension of the character on the
support.  Changing either choice changes \(c_\epsilon\) only by a sign, so
membership of \(c_1/c_0\) in \(\mu_4\) is independent of these choices.

If one Radon card is zero, the other gains only one affine layer equation and
\(F_{\mathbf{v}}\in\cA\).  Suppose both are nonzero.  Their union has affine support
exactly when \(M_0=M_1\).  When the translation spaces agree, the difference of
the two characters is absorbed by an allowed quadratic mixed term with the
layer bit, and the layer scales extend to an affine fourth-root phase
exactly when \(c_1/c_0\in\mu_4\).  Thus \(F_{\mathbf{v}}\notin\cA\) gives
\hypertarget{eq:v4-chart-obstruction}{}
\begin{equation*}
 M_0\ne M_1,
 \qquad\text{or}\qquad
 M_0=M_1\ \text{ and }\ c_1/c_0\notin\mu_4.
\end{equation*}

Represent \(\lambda_0+\lambda_1\) by \({\boldsymbol{\rho}}\in W\), so that
\((\lambda_0+\lambda_1)({\mathbf{x}})={\boldsymbol{\rho}}\cdot {\mathbf{x}}\).  When \(M_0\ne M_1\), choose
\({\mathbf{s}}\in M_0\setminus M_1\) and \({\mathbf{t}}\in M_1\) with
\({\mathbf{s}}\cdot {\mathbf{t}}=1\), and put \({\boldsymbol{\Delta}}={\mathbf{a}}_1-{\mathbf{a}}_0\).  The plane
\(\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{s}},{\mathbf{t}}\}\) is nondegenerate.  Put
\(S_0=\Span\{{\mathbf{s}},{\mathbf{t}},{\boldsymbol{\Delta}},{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\}\).  Its radical has dimension at most two.
Pairing a basis of that radical with dual vectors gives a nondegenerate
extension \(N\supseteq S_0\) of dimension at most six.  If \(N\) has odd
dimension, then its nondegenerate orthogonal complement is odd-dimensional
and contains an anisotropic vector. Adding that orthogonal line preserves
the bound and makes the extension even-dimensional.  Denote the result by
\[
 U\supseteq\Span\{{\mathbf{s}},{\mathbf{t}},{\boldsymbol{\Delta}},{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\},
 \qquad \dim U\le6.
\]
When \(M_0=M_1\), start from
\(S_0=\Span\{{\boldsymbol{\Delta}},{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\}\).  Pairing its radical needs at most two dual
vectors and gives a nondegenerate extension of dimension at most four. If
its dimension is odd, add an anisotropic vector from its odd-dimensional
nondegenerate complement.  This gives an even-dimensional nondegenerate
\(U\supseteq\Span\{{\boldsymbol{\Delta}},{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\}\) with \(\dim U\le4\).
Put
\[
 P:=U^{\perp_W},
 \qquad W=U\perp P,
\]
and let \(\pi_U,\pi_P\) be the corresponding orthogonal projections.  A
Witt decomposition of the even-dimensional nondegenerate space \(P\)
supplies
\[
 J\le P,
 \qquad J=J^{\perp_P}.
\]
Because \({\boldsymbol{\Delta}},{\boldsymbol{\rho}}\in U\), the two shifts have the same
\(P\)-projection
\[
 {\mathbf{p}}_0:=\pi_P({\mathbf{a}}_0)=\pi_P({\mathbf{a}}_1),
\]
and the two characters have the same restriction
\(\lambda_P:=\lambda_0|_P=\lambda_1|_P\).

Let \(S:=\Pi_{\mathbf{v}}\perp U\), put \(m:=\dim S\), and choose an isometry
\(\phi:S\to\Ftwo^m\).  Such an isometry exists because \(S\) contains the
nonalternating plane \(\Pi_{\mathbf{v}}\).  We have \(m\le8\) when \(M_0\ne M_1\) and \(m\le6\) when \(M_0=M_1\).  Define
\[
 R_0:=\{({\mathbf{s}}_0+{\mathbf{j}},\phi({\mathbf{s}}_0)):{\mathbf{s}}_0\in S,\ {\mathbf{j}}\in J\}
 \le\Ftwo^n\oplus\Ftwo^m.
\]
For two elements of \(R_0\), their inner product is
\[
 ({\mathbf{s}}_0+{\mathbf{j}})\cdot({\mathbf{s}}'_0+{\mathbf{j}}')
 +\phi({\mathbf{s}}_0)\cdot\phi({\mathbf{s}}'_0)={\mathbf{j}}\cdot {\mathbf{j}}'=0.
\]
Moreover \(\dim R_0=m+(n-m)/2=(n+m)/2\), so \(R_0=R_0^\perp\).
On \(R_0\), define the linear sign character
\[
 \chi({\mathbf{s}}_0+{\mathbf{j}},\phi({\mathbf{s}}_0)):=(-1)^{\lambda_P({\mathbf{j}})}.
\]
Choose an ambient linear-character extension and denote it again by
\(\chi\).  Hence, for
\(({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in\Ftwo^n\oplus\Ftwo^m\),
\begin{equation}
 C({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}):=\chi({\mathbf{x}}-{\mathbf{p}}_0,{\mathbf{y}})\,
 \one[({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in({\mathbf{p}}_0,\zero)+R_0]
 \label{eq:v4-localizing-context}
\end{equation}
is character-flat.  By \cref{lem:v4-character-flat-calculus}, choose an \(O(n^2)\)-size
\(\RMcore\)--Pauli network whose boundary
is \(\kappa C\), where the realization scalar
\(\kappa\in\AlgNums^\times\) is recorded.  Any fixed
coordinate complements contributed by its \(X\)-attachments are
absorbed into the displayed affine shift.

Contract this context with \(F_{\mathbf{v}}\).  Its boundary is
\hypertarget{eq:v4-localized-sum}{}
\begin{equation*}
 H^+(\phi({\mathbf{s}}_0))
 =\kappa\sum_{{\mathbf{j}}\in J}(-1)^{\lambda_P({\mathbf{j}})}
   F_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathbf{s}}_0+{\mathbf{p}}_0+{\mathbf{j}}),
 \qquad {\mathbf{s}}_0\in S.
\end{equation*}
When \(M_0\ne M_1\), write
\({\mathbf{a}}_\epsilon={\mathbf{u}}_\epsilon+{\mathbf{p}}_0\) with \({\mathbf{u}}_\epsilon\in U\).  The three boundary
points \(\phi({\mathbf{u}}_0)\), \(\phi({\mathbf{u}}_0+{\mathbf{s}})\), and
\(\phi({\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{v}}+{\mathbf{u}}_1)\) give nonzero sums.  On each support fiber the context
character cancels the common \(P\)-restriction of the character of the Radon card, so
there is no cancellation.  The fourth point
\(\phi({\mathbf{r}}_{\mathbf{v}}+{\mathbf{u}}_1+{\mathbf{s}})\) has value zero: otherwise some \({\mathbf{j}}\in J\subseteq P\)
would satisfy \({\mathbf{s}}+{\mathbf{j}}\in M_1\), and then
\[
 0=({\mathbf{s}}+{\mathbf{j}})\cdot {\mathbf{t}}={\mathbf{s}}\cdot {\mathbf{t}}=1.
\]
Thus the support of \(H^+\) is not affine.  When \(M_0=M_1\), the
two corresponding fibers have the same nonzero cardinality
\(|J\cap M_0|\).  Their value ratio differs from \(c_1/c_0\) only by a
sign, and therefore remains outside \(\mu_4\).  In both cases
\(H^+\ne0\) and \(H^+\notin\cA\).

Finally, contract the same realizable context with \(g\), and call its boundary
\(H\).  Put \({\mathbf{w}}:=\phi({\mathbf{v}})\).  Translation by \({\mathbf{v}}\) on the input of
\cref{eq:v4-localizing-context} is translation by \({\mathbf{w}}\) on its boundary,
and the context character is trivial on \(({\mathbf{v}},{\mathbf{w}})\).  Therefore
\[
 H^+=\mathsf P_{\mathbf{w}}^+H.
\]
If \(H\in\cA\), then \cref{lem:affine-two-translate-closure} would give
\(H^+\in\cA\), a contradiction.  Thus \(H\ne0\), \(H\notin\cA\), and its
arity \(m\) is at most eight.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Affine-to-character-flat recognition]
\label{lem:v4-affine-character-radon}
Let \(0\ne g\in\cA\) have even arity \(2r\ge10\).  If every signed card
\(\mathcal S_{{\mathbf{v}},d,\ell}g\), for
\({\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Acc}_{2r}\) and \(d,\ell\in\Ftwo\), is zero or
character-flat, then \(g\in\mathsf{CFlat}\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Input translation permutes the two values of \(d\) and reindexes the
corresponding quotient by an affine isometry.  Under this reindexing, it
changes a signed card only by a nonzero scalar and a linear sign character.
We may therefore
translate the affine support to a linear subspace and write
\hypertarget{eq:v4-recognition-normal-form}{}
\begin{equation*}
 g({\mathbf{x}})=c\,i^{Q({\mathbf{x}})}\one[{\mathbf{x}}\in U],
 \qquad c\ne0,
 \qquad d_U:=\dim U.
\end{equation*}
Put \(E:=\one^\perp\).  Every nonzero character-flat card has arity
\(2r-2\) and hence support dimension \(r-1\).

We first prove \(U=U^\perp\).  If \(E\subseteq U\), then
\(d_U\ge2r-1\).  For an accessible \({\mathbf{v}}\in E\), at least one signed card on
a nonempty layer is nonzero.  Restriction to the layer and quotienting by
\(\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{v}}\}\) can each remove at most one dimension.  More
explicitly, on an overlapping support orbit, factoring out \(g({\mathbf{x}})\) leaves
\[
 1\pm\zeta(-1)^{\theta({\mathbf{x}})},
 \qquad \zeta\in\mu_4,
\]
where \(\theta\) is a binary linear form.  If
\(\zeta\in\{\pm i\}\), this factor is nowhere zero.  If
\(\zeta\in\{\pm1\}\), its nonzero set is either empty, the whole current
support, or one affine hyperplane.  Thus a nonzero signed card loses at
most one additional support dimension through cancellation and has support
dimension at least
\[
 d_U-3\ge2r-4>r-1,
\]
a contradiction.  If \(E\subseteq U^\perp\), then \(d_U\le1\). Choosing
accessible \({\mathbf{v}}\in E\setminus U\) gives a nonzero card of support dimension
\(d_U\), again a contradiction.  Hence neither containment holds.

The two proper subspaces \(E\cap U\) and \(E\cap U^\perp\) do not cover
\(E\).  Since \(\dim E=2r-1\ge9\), choose
\[
 {\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname{Acc}_{2r}\setminus(U\cup U^\perp).
\]
There is no cancellation because \({\mathbf{v}}\notin U\), and each nonempty card has
support dimension \(d_U-1\).  Thus \(d_U=r\).

No accessible vector can lie in \(U^\perp\setminus U\), because such a
vector would give a nonzero card of support dimension \(r\).  Put
\(A:=E\cap U^\perp\).  It follows that
\hypertarget{eq:v4-A-contained}{}
\begin{equation*}
 A\setminus\{\zero,\one\}\subseteq U.
\end{equation*}
If \(\one\notin A\), or if \(\one\in A\cap U\), this gives
\(A\subseteq U\).  If instead \(\one\in A\setminus U\), then
\(\dim A\ge r-1\ge4\). Choose \({\mathbf{z}}\in A\setminus\{\zero,\one\}\).  Both
\({\mathbf{z}}\) and \({\mathbf{z}}+\one\) are accessible and lie in \(U\) by the preceding
display, forcing \(\one\in U\), a contradiction.  Thus \(A\subseteq U\)
in every case.

If \(U^\perp\subseteq E\), equal dimensions give \(U=U^\perp\).  Otherwise
\(\dim A=r-1\).  Since \(A\subseteq U\) and \(\dim U=r\), choose
\({\mathbf{u}}\in U\setminus A\), so
\(U=A\oplus\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{u}}\}\).  Because
\(A\subseteq U^\perp\), the vector \({\mathbf{u}}\) is orthogonal to \(A\).  If \({\mathbf{u}}\)
were even, then \({\mathbf{u}}\cdot {\mathbf{u}}=0\), hence \({\mathbf{u}}\in U^\perp\) and
\(U\subseteq U^\perp\). Equal dimensions would again give
\(U=U^\perp\).  Therefore \({\mathbf{u}}\) is odd and
\hypertarget{eq:v4-near-self-dual}{}
\begin{equation*}
 U=A\oplus\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{u}}\},
 \qquad {\mathbf{u}}\cdot {\mathbf{u}}=1.
\end{equation*}
Since \({\mathbf{u}}\) is odd, \(\one\cdot {\mathbf{u}}=1\), whereas every vector of \(A\) is
orthogonal to \({\mathbf{u}}\). Hence \(\one\notin A\).  Fix \(\zero\ne {\mathbf{s}}\in A\).
Here \(\dim A=r-1\ge4\), so \({\mathbf{s}}\) is accessible.  For this \({\mathbf{s}}\), a nonzero signed
card has either the full quotient translation space
\(U/\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{s}}\}\), of
dimension \(r-1\), or a codimension-one subspace of dimension \(r-2\).
The first is not self-dual because its orthogonal complement is
\(U^\perp/\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{s}}\}\ne
U/\Span_{\Ftwo}\{{\mathbf{s}}\}\). The second has the
wrong dimension.  On the supported layer \(d=0\), at least one of the plus
and minus cards is nonzero: after stripping their respective exterior Bell
signs and nonzero scalars, their orbit brackets are
\[
 g({\mathbf{x}})+g({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{s}})
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 g({\mathbf{x}})-g({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{s}}),
\]
whose sum is \(2g({\mathbf{x}})\).  This
contradicts the hypothesis.  Therefore
\[
 U=U^\perp.
\]

The orthogonal reduction in the proof of
\cref{lem:rm-flat-contexts} sends \(U\) to the code
\[
 \mathsf{Pair}_r
 :=\{{\mathbf x}({\mathbf{t}}):{\mathbf{t}}\in\Ftwo^r\},
 \qquad
 {\mathbf x}({\mathbf{t}}):=(t_1,t_1,\ldots,t_r,t_r).
\]
Orthogonal maps preserve \(\one\) and hence accessible directions.  Their
induced quotient affine isometries, together with nonzero scalar and
linear-character changes, preserve character-flatness, so the signed-card
hypothesis is invariant under this coordinate reduction.
Put
\[
 g({\mathbf x}({\mathbf{t}}))=c\,h({\mathbf{t}}),
\]
where
\begin{equation}
 h({\mathbf{t}})=i^{q_0+\sum_j\alpha_jt_j+
             2\sum_{j<k}\beta_{jk}t_jt_k},
 \qquad q_0,\alpha_j\in\mathbb Z_4,
 \quad \beta_{jk}\in\Ftwo.
 \label{eq:v4-pair-phase}
\end{equation}
Extend the quadratic coefficients symmetrically by setting
\(\beta_{jk}:=\beta_{kj}\) whenever \(k<j\), and put \(\beta_{jj}:=0\).
Let \(\bar {\mathbf{e}}_j\) be the \(j\)-th basis vector of \(\Ftwo^r\), and define
the \(j\)-th logical pair and its accessible direction in the original variables by
\[
 P_j:=\{2j-1,2j\},
 \qquad
 {\mathbf{v}}_j:={\mathbf{e}}_{2j-1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{2j}.
\]
Let
\[
 \mathcal T_j:=\{{\mathbf{t}}\in\Ftwo^r:t_j=0\}.
\]
Using the representatives \({\mathbf{t}}\in\mathcal T_j\), and stripping the exterior
Bell signs and nonzero scalars, the \(d=0\) plus and minus cards
in the \({\mathbf{v}}_j\)-direction have logical coefficient tensors
\begin{equation}
 A_j({\mathbf{t}})=h({\mathbf{t}})+h({\mathbf{t}}+\bar {\mathbf{e}}_j),
 \qquad B_j({\mathbf{t}})=h({\mathbf{t}})-h({\mathbf{t}}+\bar {\mathbf{e}}_j),
 \qquad {\mathbf{t}}\in\mathcal T_j.
 \label{eq:v4-pair-cards}
\end{equation}
Whenever one of these cards has support \(\mathcal T_j\),
character-flatness makes its logical coefficient tensor a nonzero scalar times
a sign character on \(\mathcal T_j\).

Define
\[
 L_j({\mathbf{t}}):=\sum_{k\ne j}\beta_{jk}t_k\in\Ftwo,
 \qquad
 \frac{h({\mathbf{t}}+\bar {\mathbf{e}}_j)}{h({\mathbf{t}})}=i^{\alpha_j}(-1)^{L_j({\mathbf{t}})}.
\]
Suppose first that some \(\alpha_j\) is even.  If \(L_j\ne0\), the two
logical coefficient tensors in \cref{eq:v4-pair-cards} are supported on complementary proper
hyperplanes, so neither nonzero card is character-flat.  Thus \(L_j=0\),
which gives \(\beta_{jk}=0\) for every \(k\ne j\).  One of \(A_j,B_j\) is
then zero and the other is a nonzero scalar multiple of
\(h|_{t_j=0}\).  Its being a sign character forces
\[
 \alpha_k\equiv0\pmod2\quad(k\ne j),
 \qquad \beta_{km}=0\quad(k,m\ne j).
\]
Together with the evenness of \(\alpha_j\), this proves that all
\(\alpha_k\) are even and all \(\beta_{km}\) vanish.

It remains to exclude the alternative in which every \(\alpha_j\) is odd.
Write \(i^{\alpha_j}=\delta_j i\), where \(\delta_j\in\{\pm1\}\).  The
plus bracket in \(A_j\) satisfies
\hypertarget{eq:v4-parity-bracket}{}
\begin{equation*}
 1+\delta_j i(-1)^{L_j}
 =(1+\delta_j i)(-\delta_j i)^{L_j}.
\end{equation*}
The multilinear modulo-four expansion of the Boolean parity \(L_j\) is
\hypertarget{eq:v4-parity-expansion}{}
\begin{equation*}
 L_j({\mathbf{t}})\equiv
 \sum_{k\ne j}\beta_{jk}t_k
 +2\sum_{\substack{k<m\\k,m\ne j}}
    \beta_{jk}\beta_{jm}t_kt_m
 \pmod4.
\end{equation*}
Since the nonzero full-support card \(A_j\) is a sign character, all mixed
quadratic coefficients in its phase vanish.  Hence, for all distinct
\(j,k,m\),
\begin{equation}
 \beta_{km}=\beta_{jk}\beta_{jm}.
 \label{eq:v4-beta-relation}
\end{equation}
View \(\beta\) as the adjacency matrix of a simple graph.  These identities
make the graph either empty or complete.  Indeed, if
\(\beta_{ab}=1\), then for every \(c\notin\{a,b\}\),
\[
 1=\beta_{ab}=\beta_{ca}\beta_{cb},
\]
so \(c\) is adjacent to both \(a\) and \(b\). Applying
\cref{eq:v4-beta-relation} with \(j=a\) joins every remaining pair.

The empty case is impossible: when \(L_j=0\), either nonzero card in
\cref{eq:v4-pair-cards} is a scalar multiple of \(h|_{t_j=0}\), whose
ratio in any remaining coordinate \(k\) is
\(i^{\alpha_k}=\pm i\), not the \(\pm1\) ratio of a sign character.

In the complete case, choose distinct logical pairs \(a,b,c\) and the
weight-two direction in the original variables
\[
 {\mathbf{w}}:={\mathbf{e}}_{2a-1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{2b-1}\in\operatorname{Acc}_{2r}.
\]
This vector is not in \(\mathsf{Pair}_r\).  The support of the plus card
\(\mathcal S_{{\mathbf{w}},0,0}g\) is the image of
\(\{{\mathbf{t}}:t_a+t_b=0\}\), and on the two-point \({\mathbf{w}}\)-translation orbit
represented by \({\mathbf x}({\mathbf{t}})\) its
coefficient is \(c\,h({\mathbf{t}})\), because \({\mathbf x}({\mathbf{t}})+{\mathbf{w}}\notin U\).  Toggling the
third logical pair \(c\) preserves this support, but its coefficient ratio
is
\[
 \frac{h({\mathbf{t}}+\bar {\mathbf{e}}_c)}{h({\mathbf{t}})}
 =i^{\alpha_c}(-1)^{t_c+\sum_{k\ne c}t_k}\in\{\pm i\}.
\]
A character-flat signature has ratio \(\pm1\) under translation by any vector in its
support translation space.  This contradiction eliminates the complete case.

Thus the all-odd alternative is impossible.  The earlier even-\(\alpha_j\)
case applies, and \cref{eq:v4-pair-phase} reduces to a nonzero scalar times
a linear sign character on the self-dual support \(U\).  This is exactly
\cref{eq:character-flat-class}.
\end{proof}
